# Decoupling spiral curvature from checkpoint tolerance

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical candidate, max4 round57. This is the
single mechanism authorized by TASK.md and master_guidance06950132d6f04c7a.
No mathematical program, numerical experiment, parameter scan, solver,
formal submission, or sub-agent was used. All new assertions and all
needed baseline lemmas are proved jointly below. None is an accepted fact.

The frozen baseline is cdd7f057606aeb18, SHA256
366cd6a12c8e4f56cf3c875c104f3e9bfdc185bf26c955e4f7e83d5bef7b607c.
It is not a premise. The present object is a new complete range-extension
draft, not an edit of that baseline or a report of its independent audit.

## 1. Result, domain, and conventions

The ordering kappa<=epsilon is not needed for this support arc and its
global assembly. It was used to obtain two sufficient coordinate claims:
q(theta)<=1 and u_j>=p_j. Both can be replaced. The vertical proximal
buffer is unchanged because y_j>=q_j. The negative horizontal normal is
paid by the checkpoint threshold, explicitly as follows.

Take independently

    0<epsilon<=1/2,  kappa>0.

Define the following word-independent constants. The fresh symbol
theta_sp avoids the unrelated accepted benchmark symbol Theta.

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_sp),
    d_sp=1-kappa^2,
    T_sp=(exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp,  0<kappa<1,
    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                    kappa>=1,
    H_sp=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1,
    A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon,
    a_sp=2A_sp/c_sp.                                      (1)

The formula for T_sp agrees continuously at kappa=1. Define also

    qmax_sp=1,                                      kappa<=epsilon,
    qmax_sp=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
             exp(kappa[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]),  kappa>epsilon,
    N_sp=max{0,kappa qmax_sp-epsilon},
    B_sp=1+max{epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon}.                (2)

The number qmax_sp is the EXACT vertical maximum of the unchanged arc;
N_sp is a uniform upper bound on the horizontal projection deficit,
not a claimed sharp maximum of that deficit. The main construction is
valid for all parameters in the displayed domain with selected steps
b_i>=B_sp. In particular,

    B_sp=1+epsilon^(-2) whenever 0<kappa<=1.              (3)

More generally the same threshold is certified whenever
epsilon N_sp<=1. Thus the entire rectangular range
0<epsilon<=1/2, 0<kappa<=1 works, including epsilon<kappa, with exactly
the old formulas for c_sp,H_sp,A_sp when kappa<1. At kappa=1 use (1).
The general kappa>1 extension pays the displayed threshold if needed.
No assertion is made that (2) is a necessary threshold or a maximal
unchanged-threshold range.

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be ANY finite nonnegative word of its ORIGINAL
physical length n. Write it in its original order as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),

where s_i is the sum of the possibly empty ordinary block_i. No upper
bound is imposed on any ordinary or selected step; zeros are retained.
Let W_n(h) be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)

over finite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth objectives with
an attained minimum, minimizers x_star, and x_0!=x_star, for physical GD
x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). This is W=2R.

For each i<k the backward construction in Section3, applied to the actual
ordered block_i, yields P_i,Q_i with

    0<=P_i<=c_sp s_i,  0<=Q_i<=H_sp,  P_i>=epsilon Q_i.

Put

    M_i=P_i+max{epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon},
    sigma_i=1/M_i,
    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).                        (4)

For k>=1 and b_i>=B_sp the finite max-affine function J_sp constructed
in Section5 defines ONE objective f=env_1 J_sp on R^(k+1), fixed before
the run, starting at x_0=e_0, with

    min f=0,
    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k),
    dist(e_0,argmin f)^2
       =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2].           (5)

All directed smooth-convex interpolation rows, including optimum and
terminal rows, hold for its actual data. Its exact closest-distance
payoff is

    (b_0-1)^2 / {[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k)}
       times product_(i=1)^(k-1) [(b_i-1)/M_i]^2.        (6)

This is bounded below by the fully explicit mass-only expression

    1/(1+2s_k) times product_(i=0)^(k-1)
       [(b_i-1)/(c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp})]^2.      (7)

The construction's exact payoff (6) and the lower estimate (7) are
different objects. For k=0 the usual one-dimensional hinge envelope
has exact closest-distance payoff 1/(1+2s_0).

A second realization of this SAME arc gives the source-format statement
for the entire parameter domain kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2, with threshold

    b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)),

and no N_sp threshold. Its specified-minimizer payoff is EXACTLY

    1/[2(1+s_k)] times product_(i=0)^(k-1)
       [b_i/(c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2.             (8)

Here the specified minimizer is 0 at initial distance1. Section7 also
gives an exact finite algebraic formula for that realization's TRUE
closest-minimizer radius, so the possible distinction is not hidden.
Its true-distance payoff is (8) divided by that squared radius, which
is positive and at most1. The main realization already has the simple
closed radius (5). No radius inflation is used in either bound.

For every L>0 replace the displayed objective by L times that objective.
The step h_t/L then produces the same physical trajectory and payoff.
Neither dimension growth nor smoothness normalization introduces an
unreported factor.

## 2. The unchanged support arc: exact extrema and normals

For 0<=theta<=theta_sp set

    e_sp(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t_sp(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    R_sp(theta)=c_sp exp(-kappa theta),
    v_sp(theta)=R_sp(theta)e_sp(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),
    n_sp(theta)=e_sp(theta)+kappa t_sp(theta).

Let K_sp be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. Its endpoints
are (c_sp,0) and (epsilon,-1). Direct differentiation gives

    p'=-R_sp(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'= R_sp(cos theta-kappa sin theta).

Thus epsilon<=p<=c_sp and q>=0 for every kappa>0. Also
q/p=tan theta<=1/epsilon. The maximum of q is at the endpoint if
kappa<=epsilon, and otherwise at theta_c=arctan(1/kappa). This proves
the exact formula (2). In the latter case qmax_sp>1. More explicitly,

    log qmax_sp = integral_(epsilon)^kappa
                         (kappa-t)/(1+t^2) dt,
    (kappa-epsilon)^2/[2(1+kappa^2)]
       <=log qmax_sp<=(kappa-epsilon)^2/2.              (9)

These follow by differentiating the explicit logarithm in its lower
parameter and integrating. They quantify the genuine overshoot; it
is not discarded as a per-checkpoint asymptotic error.

For delta=phi-theta in [-theta_sp,theta_sp],

    <n_sp(theta),v_sp(phi)>/R_sp(theta)
       =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).

Its derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta. The maximum
is1 at delta=0, since theta_sp<pi/2. Moreover
<n_sp(theta),0>=0<R_sp(theta). Hence n_sp(theta) is an outward
supporting normal to K_sp at v_sp(theta), for EVERY positive kappa.
At v_sp(0), the vector (0,1) is also supporting, because q>=0.

The horizontal component of the actual normal is cos theta-kappa
sin theta. It decreases from1 to (epsilon-kappa)/sqrt(1+epsilon^2).
It is negative exactly when kappa>epsilon and theta>theta_c. The
vertical component is -sin theta-kappa cos theta<0 throughout the arc.
These signs, not an assumed coordinate monotonicity, are used below.

## 3. Arbitrary nonnegative ordinary words and a uniform displacement

Fix an ordered ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_m, with alpha_j>=0.
Set r_m=v_m=v_sp(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1). Work backward. Given
r_j=(u_j,-y_j), with projected arc point v_j=v_sp(theta_j), let
alpha=alpha_j. If

    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp],                       (10)

put v_(j-1)=v_sp(0) and r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v_sp(0).
Otherwise choose the unique theta in (0,theta_j] solving

    <r_j,e_sp(theta)-kappa^(-1)t_sp(theta)>/R_sp(theta)
       =1-alpha,                                       (11)

and set v_(j-1)=v_sp(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v_(j-1).
These are exact implicit definitions with proved existence and
uniqueness, not executed root searches or numerical assumptions.

We prove backward by induction that v_j=Pi_(K_sp)(r_j), that

    u_j>=epsilon,  y_j>=1,  y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon,          (12)

and that before a cap, including the initial endpoint, there is t_j>=0
such that

    r_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e_sp(theta_j)
                                  +kappa t_j t_sp(theta_j)]. (13)

At j=m these statements hold with t_m=0. In case (10), put
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0, and

    r_(j-1)-v_sp(0)=mu n_sp(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).

Both coefficients of these supporting normals are nonnegative. This
proves projection at the cap. It also gives
y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-c_sp), so every earlier backward step is capped.

If (10) fails, the current point cannot already be capped. The left
side of (11) is1 at theta_j by (13), and is
(u_j-y_j/kappa)/c_sp<1-alpha at theta=0. Its derivative is

    (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R_sp(theta)>0.

There is exactly one root in (0,theta_j]. Since u_j,y_j are positive,
(13) shows that the polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j; therefore
<r_j,t_sp(theta)>=0 at the root. Equation (11) says precisely that
r_j+(alpha-1)v_sp(theta) is a nonnegative multiple of n_sp(theta).
This proves projection and (13) at the preceding index. Finally,
adding alpha(p,-q), with p>=epsilon and 0<=q/p<=1/epsilon, preserves
(12). This also covers alpha=0 without deleting that physical entry.

Let

    P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), S=sum_j alpha_j.

Then r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P<=c_sp S, and P>=epsilon Q. We next
bound Q independently of both the length and the size of the steps.

For two consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new, put
Delta=theta_new-theta_old. Taking the t_sp(theta_old) component in
the exact relation r_old=r_new+alpha v_sp(theta_old) gives

    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)t_new,
    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.            (14)

Both coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2, direct
differentiation gives

    A'-(d/kappa)A
       =-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.

Thus A<=exp(d Delta/kappa), for all kappa>0. If kappa<1 then
B<=Delta/kappa and 1+dB<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Consequently

    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).

Telescoping from the endpoint gives
t_j<=[exp(d(theta_sp-theta_j)/kappa)-1]/d<=T_sp.
If kappa>=1, A<=1 and B<=Delta/kappa, so (14) instead gives
t_j<=(theta_sp-theta_j)/kappa<=T_sp. No sign reversal in a
negative-d inequality is used. At an uncapped point,

    y_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin theta_j
                                    +kappa t_j cos theta_j]
       <=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]=1+H_sp.

A cap leaves y unchanged. Hence 0<=Q=y_0-1<=H_sp, as asserted.

Two more bounds repair precisely the places that lost kappa<=epsilon.
At every uncapped index, (13) gives

    y_j-q_j=R_sp(theta_j)t_j(sin theta_j+kappa cos theta_j)>=0. (15)

The same inequality holds at a cap because q_j=0. If the horizontal
normal is nonnegative, u_j-p_j>=0. Otherwise theta_j>theta_c,
p_j<=p(theta_c)=kappa qmax_sp, and u_j>=epsilon. At a cap u_j>=p_j.
Therefore the uniform bound is

    u_j-p_j>=-N_sp.                                    (16)

For clarity, (16) is a bound on the ACTUAL normal displacement, even
though its proof uses the separate bounds on u_j and p_j. It neither
asserts a nonnegative horizontal normal nor absorbs an error per step.

To prove (3), when kappa<=epsilon the deficit N_sp is0. Otherwise
qmax_sp decreases in epsilon, and

    qmax_sp<= exp(kappa arctan kappa)/sqrt(1+kappa^2).

The logarithmic derivative of the right side in kappa is arctan kappa,
so for kappa<=1 it is at most exp(pi/4)/sqrt(2)<2. One elementary
verification of the last strict inequality uses pi<4 and
exp(1)<=5/2+2/9=49/18<2sqrt(2), obtained by bounding the factorial
series from its third term by a geometric series of ratio1/4.
It follows that N_sp<2<=1/epsilon. This proves (3).

## 4. Finite local planes and both required zero regions

Suppress the block index. For b>=1+epsilon^(-2), take any positive
sigma such that

    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,
    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                  (17)

Define

    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b)>=0, Y=sigma Q,
    V_fin={0,v_sp(0),v_0,...,v_m},
    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.           (18)

The finite convex hull of V_fin is contained in K_sp and includes
every v_j. The projection inequalities from Section3 therefore hold
on this finite hull. At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j and z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,
the plane with slope sigma v_j is active in phi. Indeed, for every
v in V_fin, <r_j-v_j,v-v_j><=0. Thus sigma v_j belongs to the
subdifferential of phi at z_j. The proximal minimizer of phi at x_j
is exactly z_j, and grad(env_1 phi)(x_j)=sigma v_j.

For every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin, p>0 and 0<=q<=p/epsilon. Hence

    <v,-z_circ>
       =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]
       <=-p+sigma p[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=0.

It follows that phi=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. At (U,Y),

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0.         (19)

So phi(U,y)=0 whenever y>=Y. Both statements remain true of its
nonnegative envelope, whose zero set is exactly that of phi.

The local trajectory starts at (1,0), follows every actual ordinary
step, and ends that block at (U+sigma epsilon b,Y), with gradient
sigma(epsilon,-1). The step b then reaches (U,Y+sigma b). Physical
first coordinates decrease and second coordinates increase throughout;
in particular x_(j),1>=U and 0<=x_(j),2<=Y before the checkpoint.
This fact uses only p>0, q>=0, not the sign of a normal.

For the ONE-envelope global assembly we additionally need bounds at
the local proximal points. Monotonicity of backward y gives
1<=y_j<=1+Q. Together with (15),

    0<= (z_j)_2=sigma[1+Q-y_j+q_j]<=sigma(1+Q).        (20)

The upper bound is EXACTLY the old buffer: the overshoot qmax_sp-1
causes no added loss, because y_j-q_j>=0. Similarly, by (16),

    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]
       >=sigma[epsilon(b-1)-N_sp]>=0                  (21)

whenever b>=B_sp. This is the sole additional threshold used by that
assembly. The source-format sum assembly uses physical points only
and consequently needs (19), but not (21).

## 5. One static global envelope and the physical trajectory

Use (4) and the finite planes (18) with sigma_i=1/M_i. Conditions
(17) hold. In coordinates 0,...,k set

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),
    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max{z_k-ell_k,0},
    J_sp(z)=max_(0<=i<=k) Phi_i(z),
    f(x)=min_z [J_sp(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                  (22)

This is a specified finite maximum of affine planes, including 0.
It is nonnegative and J_sp(0)=0. Its proximal minimizer is unique,
because the objective in z is coercive and strictly convex.

Here are the needed envelope properties with their proofs. Write
z=prox(x) and g=x-z; the minimizing condition is g in partial J_sp(z).
For another input x', the two subgradient inequalities give
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, so the gradient candidate is 1-Lipschitz.
For an increment w, using z as a trial point gives
f(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Using the actual proximal point z'
and the subgradient inequality at z gives

    f(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

These estimates prove differentiability with gradient g, and the
global lower supporting inequality proves convexity. The function is
everywhere finite, has minimum0, and has 1-Lipschitz gradient.

At the start of block i the claimed physical state is

    x_j=ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i,
    x_i=ell_i+D_i,
    x_j=0 for j>i.                                    (23)

Start from e_0 and use the local trajectory of Section4 in coordinates
i,i+1. At a candidate GLOBAL proximal point of that local trajectory,
the current Phi_i has its stated subgradient. The preceding component
has first input U_(i-1), and second input at least
ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1), by (21), so is0. Earlier pieces remain in
their post-checkpoint zero regions. The next component has first
input at most0 by (20) and ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i), and second
input0. All later components, including the terminal hinge, also lie
in their pre-activation zero regions. Thus Phi_i attains the maximum
at the candidate proximal point, even in a zero-value tie. Its stated
subgradient is a subgradient of J_sp, proving that the local gradient
is the actual global gradient.

After the checkpoint, coordinate i freezes at ell_i+D_i U_i, while
coordinate i+1 is

    D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This proves (23) by induction through the entire supplied physical
word. No component or constraint is discarded at a transition.

During the terminal block, let u be the sum of its already executed
steps; 0<=u<=s_k. Its moving coordinate is
ell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. The proximal coordinate is at least ell_k,
since D_k-(u+1)delta_tail>=0. All old pieces vanish there. The actual
gradient is delta_tail e_k at every terminal-block query and at the
unqueried terminal point. Therefore

    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2
          =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].                          (24)

For ALL ordered pairs of samples a,b, include the terminal and any
minimizer, and write g_a=grad f(x_a), z_a=x_a-g_a, f_a=f(x_a).
The construction gives g_a in partial J_sp(z_a) and
f_a=J_sp(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2. Exact cancellation then yields

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2
        =J_sp(z_a)-J_sp(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.          (25)

For a minimizer g_star=0 and J_sp(z_star)=0, so no extra argument or
omitted optimum row is needed. Zeros give equal actual jets and also
satisfy (25). The Gram matrix is that of the explicit real vectors
in R^(k+1), so it is positive semidefinite. There is no independent
oracle branch, reset function, or unverified interpolation premise.

## 6. Exact closest radius for the single-envelope realization

Set the first two coordinates of x_star to

    (x_star)_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0),
    (x_star)_1=sigma_0(1+Q_0)=ell_1,

and all later coordinates to0. The first component is at its center,
the next has first input0 and second input0, and every later component
is in its pre-activation zero region. The terminal hinge also vanishes.
Thus J_sp(x_star)=0. Nonnegativity and attainment in (22) show that
argmin f={J_sp=0}.

Both endpoint planes of the first component have value0 at x_star.
Moreover

    e_0-x_star
      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c_sp] sigma_0(c_sp,0,0,...)
         +(1+Q_0) sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...).

Both coefficients are nonnegative by P_0>=epsilon Q_0. Every point
z of {J_sp=0} has nonpositive inner product with each active endpoint
slope after subtracting x_star. Hence
<e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0. Expanding ||e_0-z||^2 proves that x_star
is the unique closest minimizer and gives the radius in (5).
Combining with (24) proves the exact payoff (6).

For epsilon<=1 and nonnegative P,Q,

    sqrt((epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2)
       <=P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon<=M.

Also M_i<=c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}. These two estimates give
(7). For k=0, use J_sp(z)=delta max{z,0}, delta=1/(1+2s_0),
starting at1. Its nearest minimizer is0, and the same terminal
calculation gives exactly 1/(1+2s_0).

## 7. Source-format realization, with its radius accounted for

Fix the same independent parameters and now suppose only
b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)). For each i<k run Section3 on its actual
ordinary steps divided by2. In this section P_i,Q_i refer to those
HALF-step blocks, so P_i<=c_sp s_i/2 and Q_i<=H_sp. Use Section4
with local selected step beta_i=b_i/2 and

    sigma'_i=1/[c_sp s_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].

Both conditions (17) hold because
P_i+epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon<=c_sp s_i/2+A_sp.
Let phi'_i, z'_circ,i, U'_i, Y'_i be the objects in (18) for these
inputs, and put f_i=env_1 phi'_i. Define

    G_i=sigma'_i beta_i=b_i/[c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i],
    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i,
    ell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y'_i,
    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).

The single source-format objective is

    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2
               f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
          +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k),              (26)

where Hub_delta(z) is0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta, and
delta z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta. This is the same spiral mechanism
assembled as a sum of local envelopes instead of a global envelope.

Each local term is convex and 1-smooth on its two coordinates. Summing
their quadratic upper inequalities with coefficient1/2, and adding
the terminal term, counts each coordinate at most twice. Thus

    F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2.

To see directly that this proves a 1-Lipschitz gradient in this convex
case, apply the inequality to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and
w=-grad V(x). The minimum of V is at y, so

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
       >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.                  (27)

Adding the exchanged inequality and using Cauchy--Schwarz gives the
claimed Lipschitz bound. The function is nonnegative and F(0)=0 by
the zero regions and ell'_i>=0.

At the start of block i its frozen coordinates are
ell'_j+D'_j U'_j for j<i, its current coordinate is ell'_i+D'_i,
and its later coordinates are0. Along the local physical path,
x_i>=ell'_i+D'_i U'_i>=ell'_i, and
0<=x_(i+1)<=D'_i Y'_i=ell'_(i+1) before the checkpoint. Every old
local objective is therefore in its post-checkpoint zero region, and
every future one is in its pre-activation zero region. Each has zero
gradient there because it is differentiable and nonnegative. Only the
current term has a nonzero gradient. Its coefficient1/2 converts the
actual physical step h_t to the prescribed local step h_t/2. The
checkpoint leaves current coordinate ell'_i+D'_i U'_i and next
coordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). This proves the actual GD trajectory
by induction, using no bound on a horizontal PROXIMAL coordinate.

In the terminal block the physical gradient is delta'/2. The final
coordinate above ell'_k is D'_k-delta' s_k/2>=delta'. Substitution in
the terminal term gives

    F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)].                        (28)

The initial point is e_0 and 0 is an actual minimizer with distance1.
Since D'_k=product_i G_i, (28) proves the exact payoff (8) about0.
At k=0 simply use Hub_(1/(1+s_0))/2 from initial1; (28) holds with
D'_0=1. For every ordered pair of actual samples, including optimum
and terminal points, (27) with x=x_b and y=x_a is exactly Q_ab<=0.
Thus the second realization also retains every original directed row.

Here is an exact radius formula for (26); it does not replace the
simple main radius (5). Write the two coordinates of z'_circ,i as
c_(i,1) and c_(i,2). The zero set of phi'_i is exactly

    z_1<=c_(i,1),
    epsilon(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.             (29)

To prove equality, both inequalities are necessary because the two
endpoint slopes are included. They are sufficient because every
other (p,-q) equals a nonnegative combination of (c_sp,0) and
(epsilon,-1): its coefficients are (p-epsilon q)/c_sp and q.
The envelope has the same zero set. Since (26) is a sum of nonnegative
terms, its full minimum set is the following EXPLICIT polyhedron:

    C_rad={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
               epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2), i<k;
               x_k<=ell'_k},
    zeta_i=ell'_i+D'_i c_(i,1).                        (30)

It contains0. Let A_rad be the (2k+1)-by-(k+1) matrix whose first
two rows for index i are e_i^T and (epsilon e_i-e_(i+1))^T, with
right sides zeta_i and epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2); the final row
is e_k^T with right side ell'_k. Let t_rad be this right-side column.
For a subset S of linearly independent rows, write

    A_S=(A_rad)_S, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,
    K_S=A_S A_S^T, lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S.

Include the empty subset with value0. Then the EXACT squared radius is

    rho_source^2=dist(e_0,C_rad)^2
       =max_{S independent, lambda_S>=0} r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S. (31)

This is a finite algebraic expression determined by the given blocks.
It is not an executed active-set search or a numerical optimization.

For completeness, here is a proof of (31). For any feasible x and
any lambda>=0 on S, set w=A_S^T lambda. Then
<w,e_0-x>>=lambda^T r_S, and completing a square gives
||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||w||^2. At lambda=lambda_S the right
side equals r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S. This proves the lower bound for every
entry of the displayed maximum. Let x_* be the projection onto the
nonempty closed polyhedron C_rad, and w_*=e_0-x_*. For every direction
d with nonpositive dot product with every active row, x_*+td is
feasible for sufficiently small t>0; thus <w_*,d><=0. It follows
that w_* belongs to the nonnegative cone of the active rows. An
elementary justification is as follows. A finitely generated cone is
closed: from any nonnegative representation one can successively
remove a linear dependence while keeping coefficients nonnegative,
until only independent generators remain; it is consequently a
finite union of closed images of orthants under injective linear
maps. If w_* were outside this cone, subtracting its projection on
the cone would give a direction d with nonpositive dot product with
every generator but <w_*,d>>0, a contradiction. Choose an independent
active subset S representing w_*=A_S^T lambda with lambda>=0.
Activity gives r_S=A_S w_*=K_S lambda, hence lambda=lambda_S and
the corresponding entry in (31) equals ||w_*||^2. This proves equality.

For k>=1 the initial point is outside C_rad: its first-coordinate
constraint is 1<=c_(0,1)=1-sigma'_0(epsilon+P_0), which fails.
For k=0, C_rad is the nonpositive half-line. Therefore
0<rho_source^2<=1 in every case, the upper bound using 0 in C_rad.
The exact TRUE-distance payoff of (26) is (8)/rho_source^2. In
particular (8) is an actual payoff about0, not a quantity secretly
divided by an enlarged or unspecified radius.

## 8. Activation costs after decoupling: an exact finite family

The new range does not remove the endpoint/minimum reset cost. We
rederive a finite witness instead of importing the old unverified
obstruction. For a positive integer M put Delta=theta_sp/M and
theta_l=theta_sp-l Delta, 0<=l<=M. With A_D=A(Delta), B_D=B(Delta)
from (14), define

    t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),
    r_l=R_sp(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e_sp(theta_l)
                                      +kappa t_l t_sp(theta_l)],
    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D
                         +(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).      (32)

Every alpha_l>=0 because exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta<=1 and all
other terms are nonnegative. Resolving along e_sp(theta_l) and
t_sp(theta_l) proves exactly

    r_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v_sp(theta_l).

The supporting normal proves v_sp(theta_l)=Pi_(K_sp)(r_l). Thus the
ACTUAL finite chronological word (alpha_M,...,alpha_1), started at
r_M, runs backward through these indexed states to (epsilon,-1).
There is no numerical sweep or reliance on a continuous-time path.
Set S_M=sum_l alpha_l, P_M=(r_M)_1-epsilon and
Q_M=-(r_M)_2-1. Since theta_M=0,

    P_M=c_sp(1+t_M)-epsilon,
    1+Q_M=c_sp kappa t_M.                              (33)

To record exact limiting constants, define

    T_eq=[exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1]/d_sp, kappa!=1,
    T_eq=theta_sp,                                    kappa=1,
    S_eq=(1+kappa^2)(T_eq-kappa theta_sp)/d_sp, kappa!=1,
    S_eq=2theta_sp+theta_sp^2,                         kappa=1.

For every fixed positive kappa and epsilon,

    t_M -> T_eq, S_M -> S_eq.                          (34)

Here are discrete derivations of the limits. If kappa!=1 then
A_D=1+(d_sp/kappa)Delta+O(Delta^2), B_D=Delta/kappa+O(Delta^2).
For all sufficiently large M, A_D!=1, and the EXACT finite formulas
are

    t_M=B_D(A_D^M-1)/(A_D-1),
    S_M=M a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D(t_M-M B_D)/(A_D-1),
    a_D=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D.

Using M Delta=theta_sp and A_D^M->exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa) gives
(34), including negative d_sp. If kappa=1, then A_D=1+O(Delta^2),
B_D=Delta+O(Delta^2), and uniformly for l<=M the finite sum
t_l=B_D sum_(j=0)^(l-1) A_D^j equals l Delta+O(1/M).
Thus t_M->theta_sp and Delta sum_(l=0)^(M-1)t_l->theta_sp^2/2.
Substituting into the exact sum for S_M gives 2theta_sp+theta_sp^2.
The error constants here depend on the fixed parameters; the limits
are used to produce finite witnesses for each fixed parameter, not
as an error bound along an arbitrary growing checkpoint word.

For any local scale sigma, the endpoint plane (epsilon,-1) at the
specified zero-height origin imposes NECESSARILY

    sigma^(-1)>=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon.               (35)

This is also an original directed-row condition, not just a convenient
zero-set proof. The endpoint sample is x_end=(1-sigma P,sigma Q),
g_end=sigma(epsilon,-1), f_end=sigma^2(1+epsilon^2)/2. For prescribed
minimum data x_star=0,g_star=0,f_star=0 its original residual is

    Q_(end,star)=sigma[-epsilon
                   +sigma(epsilon^2+epsilon P+1+Q)].  (36)

It is positive precisely when (35) is violated. The extra q overshoot
or a new smoothness normalization does not cancel this row.

After paying the actual horizontal linear allowance c_sp S_M, the
finite words (32) have excess

    E_M=epsilon+P_M+(1+Q_M)/epsilon-c_sp S_M
       -> E_eq=c_sp[1+(1+kappa/epsilon)T_eq-S_eq].       (37)

Consequently any universal inverse scale c_sp S+A, with A>=0, preserving the
same endpoint and zero-height minimum requires A>=max{0,E_eq}.
With denominator c_sp S+epsilon beta+A at a fixed legal local
checkpoint beta, it requires A>=max{0,E_eq-epsilon beta}. This
accounts for the available horizontal slack BEFORE asserting a cost.
No positivity of E_eq is claimed for all large kappa.

In the useful decoupled small-curvature range
0<epsilon<=kappa<=1/2, T_eq=T_sp and (37) becomes

    E_eq=c_sp[1+(kappa/epsilon-2kappa^2/d_sp)T_sp
                         +kappa(1+kappa^2)theta_sp/d_sp]>0. (38)

The coefficient of T_sp is at least 1-2kappa^2/d_sp>=1/3.
Uniformly over 0<epsilon<=kappa as kappa tends to0,

    E_eq/[c_sp(kappa/epsilon)T_sp] -> 1,
    A_sp/[c_sp(1+kappa)T_sp/epsilon] -> 1,
    kappa A_sp/E_eq -> 1,
    kappa log(epsilon A_sp) -> pi/2,
    kappa log((epsilon/kappa)E_eq) -> pi/2.             (39)

For example the first ratio follows because the relative subtraction
2kappa epsilon/d_sp is at most2kappa^2/d_sp, and the remaining terms
are uniformly negligible compared with (kappa/epsilon)T_sp.
For the logarithmic limits, theta_sp->pi/2 uniformly in epsilon<=kappa,
log T_sp=d_sp theta_sp/kappa-log d_sp+o(1), and c_sp->1.
The displayed epsilon factors are essential: log(1/epsilon) need
not be negligible if epsilon is allowed to shrink arbitrarily fast.

Taking beta=1+epsilon^(-2) gives
epsilon beta=epsilon+1/epsilon=o(E_eq), uniformly in this small-curvature
range, since E_eq/(epsilon+1/epsilon) is asymptotic to
c_sp kappa T_sp/(1+epsilon^2), which tends to infinity.
Thus the source-format epsilon beta term does not erase this
activation obstruction. For every fixed parameter and a proposed
smaller A, sufficiently large FINITE M violates (35), hence (36).

This obstruction is confined to the same arc, endpoint jet and common
zero-height minimum condition. It does not refute different positive
heights, other curves, or the unrestricted W_n. The decoupled
construction itself remains viable because its scale pays A_sp.

## 9. Every loss, with an arbitrary number of selected checkpoints

For the single-envelope realization all paid quantities are visible:
the threshold B_sp, numerator b_i-1, linear coefficient c_sp, activation
constant A_sp, term epsilon b_i, and terminal factor 1/(1+2s_k).
Equations (20)--(21) prove that there is no extra factor involving
qmax_sp for each transition. Above kappa=1 the only new normal cost
in this realization is the explicit B_sp threshold. The exact radius
is (5); passing to (7) pays only the stated norm and displacement bounds.

When every s_i>0 for i<k, compare (7) to the FORMAL diagnostic

    I=product_i(b_i/s_i)^2/(1+2s_k).

This is not asserted to equal or control the benchmark. The exact
logarithm of I divided by (7) is

    Loss_env=2sum_i log[c_sp+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i]
                 -2sum_i log(1-1/b_i).                (40)

It is at least2k log c_sp and at most

    2k(c_sp-1)+2sum_i max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i
                                     +2sum_i 1/(b_i-1). (41)

For the source-format payoff (8), relative to the diagnostic using
the SAME terminal factor and numerator product, the exact loss is

    Loss_source=2sum_i log[c_sp+(2A_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i]. (42)

It is at least2k log c_sp and at most
2k(c_sp-1)+2sum_i(2A_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i. These finite identities
are valid for arbitrary k. They do not replace a sum over checkpoints
by a single-checkpoint error or presume a uniform little-o estimate.
If a block has sum0, the actual bounds (7) and (8) remain defined;
the corresponding diagnostic with division by s_i is not used.

The exact unavoidable coefficient contribution in both diagnostics is

    2k log c_sp = k log(1+epsilon^2)+2k kappa theta_sp.   (43)

This displays the tradeoff unlocked by decoupling. For instance, along
the ONE concrete small-parameter law epsilon=kappa^2, kappa tending0,

    c_sp=1+(pi/2)kappa+O(kappa^2),
    A_sp ~ kappa^(-2) exp(pi/(2kappa)),
    E_eq ~ kappa^(-1) exp(pi/(2kappa)),
    B_sp=1+kappa^(-4).

To check the two equivalences without discarding a constant in an
exponent, use the exact identity
c_sp exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)exp(theta_sp/kappa)
and theta_sp=pi/2-kappa^2+O(kappa^6). Formula (43) remains the
proper accumulated loss; no conclusion is drawn from its per-block
coefficient alone. These constants improve the epsilon-linked
activation exponent, but preserve an exponential in1/kappa.

Also, for ANY fixed kappa>0 and epsilon tending0, (1) has
A_sp=O_kappa(1/epsilon) while c_sp tends exp(kappa pi/2)>1.
This is a direct observation from the explicit formulas, not a
second constructed curve or a schedule experiment. Keeping curvature
fixed removes the exponential dependence on1/epsilon at the price
of a fixed coefficient loss whose product over k checkpoints is
exactly accounted for by (43). There is no uniform negligible-loss
claim over arbitrary words or checkpoint counts.

## 10. Source grounding, scope, and the remaining target

The literature grounding is Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is
(Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2, 2026,
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2, Section3.1 and AppendixA.2--A.3.
The exact HTML was downloaded and text extracted in downloads/. Its
equation (1) uses the project's W normalization. Its equation (40)
gives F(x_n)=D^2/[4(1+s_tail)] at initial distance1; retaining the
normalizing factor2 yields 1/[2(1+s_tail)], as in (8). The paper
explicitly weakens that factor in its stated Lemma2.2. This is a source
comparison only: the spiral, its local lemmas, and both global
realizations have all been derived in this joint candidate. No external
theorem is imported as a correctness premise.

Matlas returned small-step self-contracted GD, proximal curves and
projected continuous dynamics, not the present arbitrary nonnegative
explicit GD claim. Those statements are inapplicable as a substitute
for Sections2--7 and were not used. The exact primary paper supplies
the relevant assembly context, rather than any decoupled spiral theorem.

Accepted facts04219554e41d9750 and5e1848ee0b153f5f fix the original
benchmark normalization and conditional seed interfaces. They do not
prove a single lemma of this lower construction. The original ALL-split
benchmark remains

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The unresolved ORIGINAL obligation is: for every delta>0, find a
horizon threshold independent of h such that every nonnegative word
h of every larger physical length n has a legal actual-adversary
payoff at least (1-delta)/B_n. Optimizing (6), (7), or (8) over allowed
checkpoint selections, possibly together with other accepted channels,
has not been proved to meet that obligation. The accepted phase-cover
facts and this larger parameter domain do not settle that comparison.

Outcome of this assigned round: a complete JOINT UNVERIFIED positive
extension for kappa>epsilon, in particular the full independent range
0<epsilon<=1/2, 0<kappa<=1 at unchanged threshold, and a general
kappa>0 extension with (2). The source-format sum construction works
for all positive kappa at its original doubled threshold. Activation,
transfer, smoothness, all directed rows, physical word lengths and
both radius normalizations are explicitly accounted for. No fact has
been submitted, no new result is verifier-accepted, and the original
target remains open. This consumes the one authorized analytical
round; it does not authorize self-renewal or a formal gate.

## 11. Local glossary

All symbols are defined before use above. For quick reference:
theta_sp is the endpoint angle; c_sp the initial arc radius; R_sp the
arc radial function; e_sp and t_sp its radial and clockwise tangent
unit vectors; v_sp its supporting gradient; n_sp its supporting normal;
K_sp its convex hull with0; p,q its two coordinate magnitudes;
qmax_sp its exact vertical maximum; N_sp the certified horizontal
projection deficit; B_sp the sufficient physical checkpoint threshold;
T_sp the normal-parameter upper bound; H_sp the vertical displacement
allowance; A_sp the additive activation allowance; a_sp its source
format rescaling. The normal coefficient t_j is unrelated to t_sp.
P,Q are actual ordinary-block displacements and s_i their physical
step sums. M_i,sigma_i,D_i,ell_i specify the single-envelope scales
and shifts; J_sp is its finite nonsmooth maximum and f its envelope.
Primed quantities, G_i,F and Hub_delta belong to the second realization.
C_rad,A_rad,t_rad,r_S,K_S,lambda_S,rho_source specify its exact
polyhedral closest-radius formula. T_eq,S_eq,E_eq are the limiting
constants of the explicitly defined finite equal-angle family;
they are not numerical evaluations. Q_ab denotes the original ordered
smooth-convex residual. I and the two Loss quantities are diagnostic
comparisons, not the Zhang--Jiang benchmark. C_n,B_n,U_n^ZJ,W_n and R_n
retain their existing project meanings.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003185 -->
# C67: an exact moving-normal clock and a failed scalar-clock transfer

Status: COMPLETE UNVERIFIED statements and proofs below; the proposed
full-horizon power-epigraph program remains PARTIAL. No fact_submit,
trajectory experiment, optimization, installation, or new allocation.
C65 and C66 are not mathematical premises. All power-epigraph claims
used here are derived in this document.

## 1. Statements and scope

For any real p>2 put phi_p(r)=|r|^p/p and define the single global function

    K_p={(r,s):s>=phi_p(r)},
    F_p(X)=max_(g in K_p)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2).

For r real and v>=0 use the states

    eta=v+phi_p(r),
    u=eta*|r|^(p-2),
    X_p(r,v)=(r(1+u),-v),
    G_p(r)=(r,phi_p(r)).

F_p is globally convex and differentiable with 1-Lipschitz gradient.
Its minimizer set is {(0,y):y<=0}, its minimum value is zero, and

    grad F_p(X_p(r,v))=G_p(r),
    2F_p(X_p(r,v))
      =r^2+2(p-1)v|r|^p/p+(2p-1)|r|^(2p)/p^2.             (1)

For every nonzero initial r_0 and v_0>=0 the true initial squared
closest-minimizer distance is

    R_0^2=r_0^2[1+(v_0+|r_0|^p/p)|r_0|^(p-2)]^2=x_0^2.  (2)

Here x is the horizontal coordinate of X_p. All p,r,v,u,eta and clock
symbols in this document are local variables, not redefinitions of the
accepted project glossary.

For an actual consecutive run with nonnegative steps of total mass T,
whose nonzero horizontal gradients all have the same sign, the normalized
payoff with its TRUE entry horizontal distance obeys, if T>=1/(2p-2),

    2F_p(X_end)/x_entry^2 <= E_p(T),
    E_p(T)=[(2p-2)T+2p-1]^2
             /[4p(p-1)(T+1)^2(2T+1)].                    (3)

No v-coordinate is reset when applying this to a later block. Its ratio
to the ORIGINAL initial payoff includes x_entry^2/x_0^2 explicitly.

Define, for r>0 and v>=0,

    C_p(r,v)=log r+(p-1)v*r^(p-2)/(p-2)
                    +(2p-1)r^(2p-2)/[p(2p-2)].           (4)

At a single sign-preserving step h>0 the exact moving-state identity
in Section4 implies

    C_p(|r_plus|,v_plus)-C_p(|r|,v)
       <=-h+|r|^(p-2)(v_plus-v)/(p-2)
       =-h+h|r|^(2p-2)/[p(p-2)].                         (5)

For a whole one-sign interval [A,B] this gives the inherited-state budget

    C_p(|r_B|,v_B)-C_p(|r_A|,v_A)
       <=-T+J_(A,B)
       <=-T+|r_A|^(p-2)(v_B-v_A)/(p-2),                 (6)
    J_(A,B)=sum_(i=A)^(B-1)
                 |r_i|^(p-2)(v_(i+1)-v_i)/(p-2).

The tempting stronger statement with J omitted is FALSE, even at the
first actual small step of H_m. For p=4, r_0=2 and v_0=0, the first
step of every actual H_m, m>=128, preserves the positive sign and has

    C_4(r_1,v_1)-C_4(2,0)>3.                             (7)

Thus the fixed-profile scalar rule that every noncrossing step decreases
its clock by at least h does not transfer to this changing normal state.
This does not refute a full-horizon power-epigraph payoff bound, nor C65.

## 2. Global legality, values, and every ordered interpolation pair

The epigraph K_p is nonempty, closed and convex. Completing the square
in the definition of F_p gives a coercive, strictly convex projection
problem, so there is a unique maximizing gradient Pi_(K_p)(X).
Adding the two projection normal inequalities gives

    ||Pi_(K_p)(X)-Pi_(K_p)(Y)||^2
       <=<Pi_(K_p)(X)-Pi_(K_p)(Y),X-Y>.

Thus the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Comparison of the two defining
maxima bounds the first-order remainder of F_p between zero and
||Y-X||^2. This proves differentiability and the stated gradient.
Convexity follows from the supremum-of-affine-functions definition.

The difference X_p-G_p equals eta*(phi_p'(r),-1). For any (t,s) in K_p,

    <X_p-G_p,(t,s)-G_p>
       <=eta*[phi_p'(r)(t-r)-phi_p(t)+phi_p(r)]<=0.

The final inequality is the defining tangent inequality for the convex
differentiable function phi_p. This proves the gradient formula. Direct
substitution into <X_p,G_p>-||G_p||^2/2 proves (1).

For arbitrary two such states, write f_i=F_p(X_i). The complete ordered
interpolation residual, with no restriction on chronological distance, is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
       =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>
       =eta_j*[phi_p'(r_j)(r_i-r_j)-phi_p(r_i)+phi_p(r_j)]
       <=0.                                               (8)

Every point (0,y), y<=0, has F_p=0 by testing zero and bounding all other
feasible inner products. For x!=0, testing (t,phi_p(t)) for sufficiently
small t with the sign of x gives a positive value: the linear term xt
dominates the terms of orders two and higher. For x=0 and y>0, a small
positive vertical gradient gives positive value. These tests determine
the minimizer set exactly and prove (2).

At a physical step h>=0 the exact GD recurrence is

    v_plus=v+h|r|^p/p,
    x_plus=x-hr,
    x_plus=r_plus+v_plus*r_plus*|r_plus|^(p-2)
                         +r_plus*|r_plus|^(2p-2)/p.        (9)

The last right-hand side is odd, continuous, strictly increasing and
surjective. Its derivative off zero is
1+(p-1)v_plus|r_plus|^(p-2)+(2p-1)|r_plus|^(2p-2)/p>=1;
the derivative at zero is 1. Thus (9) defines the unique actual next
state of the SAME function. Its normal coefficient satisfies

    eta_plus-eta=(h-1)phi_p(r)+phi_p(r_plus)>=0 for h>=1.   (10)

The effective turning variable u=eta|r|^(p-2) need not increase.

## 3. The no-turn payoff retains accumulated v and the entry radius

By symmetry suppose the whole block is on the positive ray. For fixed
v define T_v(r)=r+v r^(p-1)+r^(2p-1)/p on r>=0. At each noncrossing
positive step, x_plus<=x and v_plus>=v, so its inverse in (9) gives
0<r_plus<=r. Consequently, with terminal r>0 and u=v*r^(p-2)+r^(2p-2)/p,

    x_entry=x_end+sum h_i r_i >=r(T+1+u),
    v_end=v_entry+sum h_i r_i^p/p >=T r^p/p.               (11)

Set z=r^(2p-2)/p and w=v_end*r^(p-2), so u=w+z and w>=Tz.
Equation (1), divided by r^2, is

    2F_p(X_end)/r^2=1+(2-2/p)u+z/p.

Since z<=u/(T+1), the ratio in (3) is at most

    [1+k_T u]/[T+1+u]^2,
    k_T=2-2/p+1/[p(T+1)].                                (12)

For u>=0 the derivative in (12) has the sign of
k_T(T+1)-2-k_T u. When T>=1/(2p-2), its maximum is attained at
u=T+1-2/k_T>=0 and equals k_T^2/[4(k_T(T+1)-1)]. Simplification is
exactly (3). Negative-ray blocks give the same result by reflection.
An absorbing endpoint has zero payoff and requires no logarithmic clock.

For p=2 the rational expression E_p(T) formally reduces to the parabolic
expression, but this argument and the clock statements here are stated
for p>2. For fixed p>2,

    lim_(T->infinity) T E_p(T)=(p-1)/(2p).                (13)

This is a no-turn result, not a full-family bound in the presence of
repeated turns. It does not assume a fresh v=0 at a later block.

## 4. Exact horizontal clock and the positive vertical-state cost

Let g_v be the inverse of T_v on the positive half-line and set
Psi_p(x,v)=C_p(g_v(x),v), for x>0. Direct differentiation gives

    d C_p(r,v)/dr=T_v'(r)/r,
    partial Psi_p(x,v)/partial x=1/g_v(x),
    partial Psi_p(x,v)/partial v=g_v(x)^(p-2)/(p-2).       (14)

For the last identity, differentiating T_v(g_v(x))=x at fixed x gives
partial g_v/partial v=-g_v(x)^(p-1)/T_v'(g_v(x)). The chain rule then
subtracts r^(p-2) from (p-1)r^(p-2)/(p-2), leaving the last expression
in (14). It is POSITIVE.

For one positive noncrossing step, write x_plus=x-hr>0. The exact split
into horizontal motion at the old v followed by the actual v increment is

    C_p(r_plus,v_plus)-C_p(r,v)
       =-integral_(x_plus)^x [dy/g_v(y)]
        +1/(p-2)*integral_v^(v_plus) g_s(x_plus)^(p-2) ds. (15)

The first integral is at least (x-x_plus)/r=h, since g_v(y)<=r.
For the second integral, x_plus<=x and s>=v imply g_s(x_plus)<=r.
This proves (5); summing it over the same-sign interval proves (6),
because its gradient magnitudes decrease. By evenness the same
calculation applies to a negative interval using absolute radii.

The cost J is an actual weighted vertical increment. It is not a
freely chosen auxiliary value or a discarded initial height. A proposed
block argument for power epigraphs must bound it, or replace this clock
with a potential that pays for it. Merely knowing that eta grows does
not justify deleting the positive second integral in (15).

## 5. An exact actual-H_m counterexample to omitting the cost

For m>=128 keep the enclosing-horizon physical parameters

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).

The elementary bounds 2/5<a<5/12 give
7/5<h=1+a<17/12<3/2 for the first actual step. For completeness,
sqrt(2)<99/70 gives c<(169/70)(129/128)<73/30 and hence
a>(127/128)(30/73)>2/5. Also a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12.

Fix p=4,r_0=2,v_0=0. Then

    X_0=(34,0), R_0^2=1156,
    x_1=34-2h>31,
    v_1=4h>28/5.

The unique positive r_1 is determined by T_(v_1)(r_1)=x_1, where
T_v(r)=r+v r^3+r^7/4. At r=3/2,

    T_(v_1)(3/2)=3/2+(27/2)h+2187/512,
    x_1-T_(v_1)(3/2)=65/2-(31/2)h-2187/512
                     >37/4-2187/512>0.

Strict monotonicity implies r_1>3/2. Also r_1<2 follows from x_1<x_0
and v_1>v_0. Thus the step is genuinely positive and sign-preserving.
By (4),

    C_4(r_1,v_1)-C_4(2,0)
       >log(3/4)+(3/2)(28/5)(9/4)
                    +(7/24)[729/64-64]
       =log(3/4)+189/10-23569/1536
       >-1/3+189/10-23569/1536>3.                        (16)

Here log(4/3)<1/3 follows by integrating 1/t<1 on (1,4/3).
The final rational difference above 3 is 1707/7680=569/2560>0.
No scalar root was approximated or trajectory numerically evaluated.
Equations (1),(2),(8),(9) give the global function, true denominator,
every ordered interpolation pair, and actual prescribed step.

This refutes any universal claim C_plus-C<=-kappa h with kappa>=0
for this moving-state clock. It is not a counterexample to payoff
convergence or to the separate parabolic argument.

## 6. Literature grounding and the open quantitative interface

The exact power projection equation agrees with Propositions3.2--3.3
of Chierchia, Pustelnik, Pesquet and Pesquet-Popescu, "Epigraphical
splitting for solving constrained convex formulations of inverse
problems with proximal tools", arXiv:1210.5844v3. Its exact downloaded
paper and extracted text are in downloads/c66_epigraph_projection_1210.5844.
The Hilbert product is Euclidean, the generator has full domain and is
proper closed convex, and their parameters beta=p,tau=1/p,zeta=-v give
T_v(r)=x. The definitions and both projection proofs were re-read.
No theorem from that paper is a premise of this self-contained proof.

The precise failure is a proof migration from a FIXED scalar gradient
profile to an epigraph whose vertical coordinate changes along the
same legal trajectory. Equation (15) supplies the omitted term.
There is still no estimate of the sum of the J costs and crossing
gains over every chronological block on H_m, in terms of R_0 and the
exact benchmark. Consequently the full p>2 family, including quartic
multiple-turn trajectories, remains open. No new original-target
claim or verifier allocation follows from this result.

# Centered tree projection: fixed discovery batch, max54

Status: all five authorized jobs completed and passed their registered
numerical checks. Discovery only; NO verifier call, NO fact_submit,
NO new fact. The original exact-profile Goal remains OPEN. This closes
the ONE round allocated after completed53/attempted54; actual completion

are DERIVATION.md. Those files and both source files were hashed before

## 1. Frozen object and actual function

For the enclosing integer m set beta=1-1/m,
c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a=beta/c, r_tree=2/c, and
w_j=r_tree^j/sum_(k<m)r_tree^k. Set H_0=() and
H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j); every child keeps the same m.
The physical length is n=2^m-1. Each full leaf feature is the signed
sum of sqrt(w_j) times its orthonormal internal-node coordinates.
K is the centered convex hull of all2^m leaf features, and
F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).

The actual initial point is the all-left leaf. The joint derivation
proves that the full feature matrix has rank2^m-1, the leaves form a
simplex with0 in its interior, grad F_K=P_K, smoothness1 is sharp,
the unique minimizer is0, and the true initial squared distance is1.
Thus the reported normalized value is V=2F_K(x_n)/(L R^2)=2F_K(x_n).
The actual GD step is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i P_K(x_i), with L=1.

Off-spine automorphisms leave the trajectory invariant. Their fixed
section is exactly the m-dimensional simplex with vertices b_k: positive
sqrt(w_j) coordinates before the first-right exit k, negative sqrt(w_k)
at k, zero later; b_m is the all-left leaf. A residual in this spine has
the same normal on every full leaf in an exit group as on its b_k.
Every group weight lifts to a uniform nonnegative mixture of that group's
leaves. Thus reduced barycentric feasibility and all m+1 reduced normals
are precisely the original full-body conditions, not a substituted metric.

DERIVATION.md gives the weighted q-coordinate objective and two independent
inverse barycentric formulas. Ordinary probability-simplex thresholding
is not used. Every query receives a fresh core test. A core identity is
used only at that tested input; no suffix is skipped and no permanent
core claim is inferred from an entry time or the two-sided diagnostics.

## 4. Face and core history relevant to a future full-history bound

Every query including the terminal one was evaluated and logged.

| m | noncore queries | observed final all-core segment | distinct supports |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | 1 through7 | 8 through63 | 6 |
| 8 | 1..30,32..37,40 | 41 through255 | 12 |
| 10 | 1 through72 | 73 through1023 | 14 |

These final segments are checked finite histories, not a permanence
theorem. In particular m8 enters the core at31, leaves at32, enters
again at38, leaves again at40, and re-enters at41. Both numerical
precisions agree at every query. At query31 the actual next step is
48.612488871744068...; the minimum represented core mass is about
0.00244371348441130. Nevertheless the next state is outside, with
||y_32||^2=0.0002141183496716297... . At query38 the smallest mass is
about0.000830006766364056, but query40 is again outside with
||y_40||^2=6.859079945932178e-7. Thus even a later core visit with
positive interior margin is insufficient for a permanent-core shortcut.

The later faces are not always prefixes of exit groups. At m8 query8,
the support is exactly the numerically identified groups{0,1,2,3,4,6},
with mass0.040403237538339567... on group6 while group5 has zero mass.
Its inactive normal at group5 is-0.003700780730117329... . Those are
large margins compared with the numerical residuals, and they recur
in the independent high-precision run. The complete nonprefix query
list for m8 is8,16,18,32,33,34,35,36,37,40 (plus initial leaf0's group8).
For m10 it is16,17,18,19,24,72 (plus its initial singleton group10).
This is numerical evidence against a future universal prefix-support
induction; it is not a formally verified counterexample.

The exact forward identity rederived in DERIVATION.md is

 ||x_(i+1)||^2=(1-h_i)^2||x_i||^2
       +h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)<y_i,g_i>.

Both signed defect terms are saved at every physical step. At m8 query8
they are respectively +0.006957665854579188... and
-0.000221830220359244...; dropping the noncore residual is unjustified.
At query40 they are +6.147169188795793e-7 and
-9.164753975367975e-7. Their combined sign can change, so the termwise
record supports joint accounting rather than a fixed sign assumption.
The largest discrepancy of the iterated forward norm accounting was
9.965986369486757e-16 in binary64 m10. The stable function computation
does not depend on obtaining a tiny terminal value by subtracting these
large historical quantities.

Fresh two-sided diagnostics x_k in K and -x_k/c in K passed at sampled

For m8 query64, the smallest displayed mass of -x_k/c is about

# Joint analytic prerequisites (UNVERIFIED, max54)

These are rederived here as one draft. No unverified earlier finding is
used as an accepted premise, and no formal gate is authorized this round.
All m used below are integers >=2, so beta,c,a,w are positive.

For each internal node v of the full binary tree of depth m let e_v be
an orthonormal vector. The leaf vector a_s is the sum over its ancestors
at depths j of (-1)^(next bit)*sqrt(w_j)e_v. Its norm squared is sum w_j=1.
Every coordinate row of the leaf matrix has zero sum. Distinct rows are
orthogonal: disjoint supports have zero product; on a nested pair the
descendant's two signs cancel where the ancestor is constant. Each row
has positive norm. The matrix consequently has rank2^m-1 and kernel the
constant leaf vector. Thus the leaves are affinely independent, average0,
and their convex hull K has0 in its full Euclidean interior.

For any x the unique maximizer of <x,g>-||g||^2/2 over compact K is the
minimizer of ||x-g||^2. Its characterization is
g in K and <x-g,v-g><=0 for every vertex v. Necessity follows by the
one-sided derivative along g+t(v-g); sufficiency by expansion of distance.
Adding these inequalities for two projections yields
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, hence the projection is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing formulas sandwich the first-order error of F between
0 and <P_K(x+d)-P_K(x),d>, bounded by||d||^2. This proves convexity,
differentiability and grad F=P_K. On the interior of K, grad F(x)=x,
so smoothness1 is sharp. F>=0. If x!=0, a sufficiently small positive
multiple t*x belongs to K and <x,t*x>-||t*x||^2/2>0. Thus the unique
minimizer is0. At x0=a_(0^m) the true squared minimum distance is1.

Permuting the two children of off-spine tree nodes is an orthogonal signed
coordinate transformation preserving K and fixing every spine vector.
Uniqueness makes projection commute with these transformations. Their
fixed subspace consists exactly of the m coordinates e_(0^j): swapping
the children at any off-spine node negates that coordinate. GD from x0
therefore stays in this subspace at every physical query.

Group leaves by their first right turn k<m, plus the final all-left group
k=m. Averaging a group cancels every off-spine coordinate and gives b_k,
whose j coordinate is sqrt(w_j) for j<k, -sqrt(w_k) for j=k<m,
and0 for j>k; b_m has all coordinates sqrt(w_j). Averaging any convex
representation shows K intersect spine=conv{b_0,...,b_m}. Conversely each
b_k is such an average, so lies in K. They are affinely independent.
For a spine residual y, <y,a_s> depends only on the first-right group and
equals <y,b_k>. Therefore checking all m+1 reduced normals checks every
one of the2^m original full-leaf normals, without assuming sparsity.
A group mass p_k is expanded uniformly over2^(m-k-1) leaves for k<m;
p_m is the last leaf mass. This gives full feasibility from reduced masses.

Write x_j=sqrt(w_j)z_j and let q_j=sum_(k>=j)p_k, with q_0=1.
Then g_j=sqrt(w_j)(2q_(j+1)-q_j), and its exact projection uniquely
minimizes sum_j w_j[z_j-(2q_(j+1)-q_j)]^2 subject to
1=q_0>=q_1>=...>=q_m>=0. This is the true feature metric; ordinary
Euclidean probability-simplex thresholding changes it.

For fresh core membership solve q_(j+1)=(q_j+z_j)/2 and take
p_j=q_j-q_(j+1), p_m=q_m. An independent equivalent formula is
p_k=2^(-k-1)[1+sum_(j<k)2^j z_j-2^k z_k] for k<m,
p_m=2^(-m)[1+sum_(j<m)2^j z_j]. Affine independence proves membership
iff every p is nonnegative. Only at that input does this imply g=x;
it says nothing by itself about the next input or any later face.

For any nonempty candidate face I with base vertex v0 and edge matrix E,
the affine closest point is v0+E t with (E^T E)t=E^T(x-v0).
E has independent columns, so its Gram is positive definite. Alternatively
the barycentric affine solution solves [V^T V,1;1^T,0](p,nu)=(V^T x,1).
This augmented system is nonsingular: a homogeneous solution has1^T p=0
and p^T V^T V p=0, which forces p=0 by affine independence, then nu=0.
An affine solution with nonnegative masses and all vertex normals<=0
is the true projection by the distance expansion. An active-set search
starts feasible, descends to an affine face minimizer, stops at a first
zero weight if needed, and adds a vertex with violated normal. We do
not rely on a complexity/termination theorem: a cap invalidates the case,
and only checked feasible/KKT outputs can be used. The small full-body
calibration instead enumerates its entire finite face list independently.

For y=x-g, F=||g||^2/2+<y,g>, with <y,g>>=0 from the vertex-normal
condition at0. The equality also gives2F=||x||^2-||y||^2. For sampled
jets of this SAME function, the original interpolation convention of
accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 reduces exactly to
Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
    =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.
Full normals therefore control every past/future directed pair, without
fitting values. Floating-point checks are evidence, not exact certification.

The physical step is h_i=1+a*c^(v2(i+1)), where v2 is the exponent of2.
This follows by induction from H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j).
Every child uses the same enclosing m. Set A_i=||x_i||^2 and cex_i=<y_i,g_i>.
The directly expanded GD identity is
A_(i+1)=(1-h_i)^2 A_i
        +h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)cex_i.
The pilot logs both defect terms and all states to locate the remaining
full-history issue. It uses this forward identity for diagnostics, avoiding
division by tiny prefix products. A finite observed final core segment
is not declared permanent for untested inputs or m.

Only two accepted comparisons are evaluated: Huber=1/(1+2sum H_m), from
b98bb807d514e80d (or its predecessor's direct Huber construction), and
B_(2^m-1)<=4(1+sqrt2)^m from04219554e41d9750 proof(9.3).
Thus the latter is an UPPER multiplier, not the exact benchmark B.
No new benchmark recurrence is computed, no full-complex optimum is
estimated, and no analytic all-m terminal estimate is asserted.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003187 -->
# An exact SDP for optimizing only the final predetermined step

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No new submission is
allocated. The only accepted mathematical predecessor is 1252b8823bb5a290.
In particular the earlier hull/minimax candidate is not a proof premise here.
This proof obtains the exchange through explicit finite-dimensional separation,
with reconstruction and all zero-step cases, rather than postulating minimax.

## 1. The complete prefix and the new terminal constraints

Fix k>=0 and a finite predetermined nonnegative prefix
h_pre=(h_0,...,h_(k-1)). The prefix is empty when k=0. Let

    rho_last(h_pre)=inf_(b>=0) W_(k+1)(h_pre,b),

with W in its original arbitrary-finite-dimensional and L>0 normalization
from fact1252b8823bb5a290. Normalize the finite formulations to L=1,
x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and ||x_0||^2<=1.

Use formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_k,w_term), their full Gram matrix G_term,
real prefix values f_0,...,f_k, and a real objective variable gamma_term.
Define the positions, proximal points, and offsets by

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,       0<=i<=k,
    J={star,0,...,k},
    y_j=x_j-g_j,  delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,
    y_star=delta_star=0.

Keep EVERY distinct ordered prefix interpolation inequality Q_ij<=0 on J,
with Q as in the accepted predecessor. For every j in J put

    Bterm_j=gamma_term-2delta_j
                  -2<x_k-y_j,w_term>+||w_term||^2.

Consider the following program, denoted SDP_last(h_pre):

    maximize gamma_term
    subject to G_term PSD, G_term_(x0,x0)<=1,
               Q_ij<=0 for all distinct i,j in J,
               Bterm_j<=0 for all j in J,
               <g_k,w_term>=0.                         (1)

Every expression is affine in the Gram entries, values, and gamma_term.
Thus (1) is an SDP with k+3 formal vectors. It has no final-position
variable, no stepsize variable, and no rank constraint. Its exact value is

    value SDP_last(h_pre)=rho_last(h_pre)>0.             (2)

Both this primal maximum and its dual minimum are attained. Every optimal
dual produces a finite optimal final step b>1. Conversely every optimal
final step has an optimal dual of (1). The reconstruction is explicit below.

No earlier stepsize is optimized in (1). Its prefix interpolation still
depends on every supplied earlier step in its actual chronological position.

## 2. A direct lower certificate against every last step

Take ANY feasible point of (1) and realize its Gram matrix by vectors.
Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the maximal legal prefix interpolant F=H*,
where

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(j in J)(<y_j,v>-delta_j).

The terminal inequalities and orthogonality give, for every real b,

    gamma_term
      <=2<x_k,w_term>-2H(w_term)
       =2<x_k-b g_k,w_term>-2H(w_term)
      <=2F(x_k-b g_k).                                 (3)

For b>=0 this is a legal final GD step after precisely the supplied prefix.
If ||x_0||>0, the last expression is at most
W_(k+1)(h_pre,b)||x_0||^2<=W_(k+1)(h_pre,b). If ||x_0||=0,
all prefix iterates equal zero and F(x_k-b g_k)=0. Therefore, writing v_line
for the supremum in (1),

    v_line<=rho_last(h_pre).                            (4)

More generally, a particular feasible prefix and orthogonal vector give the
quantitative lower certificate

    rho_last(h_pre) >= 2min_j[delta_j+<x_k-y_j,w_term>]
                                      -||w_term||^2,    (5)

when its initial norm is one. Every prefix anchor appears in this minimum.
There is no presumed two-anchor support and no adaptive stepsize on its RHS.

## 3. Primal attainment and positivity

The complete feasible prefix is compact in Gram/value coordinates. Indeed,
the predecessor interpolates each feasible prefix by a legal function, and,
with D_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), gives

    ||x_i||,||g_i||<=D_i,       0<=f_i<=D_i^2/2.

These bounds and Cauchy--Schwarz bound every prefix Gram entry. The set is
closed and nonempty. For purposes of maximizing (1), restrict gamma_term>=0:
gamma_term=0,w_term=0 is feasible because delta_j>=0 for every prefix jet.
The star constraint implies

    ||w_term-x_k||^2<=||x_k||^2-gamma_term,

so ||w_term||<=2D_k and 0<=gamma_term<=D_k^2. All additional Gram entries
are bounded. This restricted feasible set is compact, proving primal attainment.

Its value is positive. Choose distinct theta_1,theta_2 in
(0,1/[2(1+sum h_t)]) and the actual quadratic

    f(z_1,z_2)=(theta_1 z_1^2+theta_2 z_2^2)/2,
    x_0=(1/sqrt(2),1/sqrt(2)).

Each prefix polynomial P_l=product_(t<k)(1-h_t theta_l) is positive.
At x_k, both coordinates are nonzero and x_k is not parallel to g_k.
The quadratic along x_k-b g_k has a positive attained minimum: in the
positive definite quadratic metric, the line misses the unique minimizer 0.
Equivalently its minimum is the positive expression proved in Section8 below.
At that minimizing step, let w_term be its actual terminal gradient and
gamma_term its positive normalized objective. Exact scalar differentiation
gives <g_k,w_term>=0. The actual outgoing interpolation inequalities,
expanded using that orthogonality, are exactly Bterm_j<=0. This is a positive
feasible point of (1), so v_line>0.

## 4. A strict primal on a positive compressed prefix

First suppose all k prefix steps are positive, allowing k=0. Choose k+2
distinct eigenvalues theta_l in (0,1), a diagonal matrix A with those
eigenvalues, and an x_0 with every coordinate nonzero and ||x_0||<1.
Use the quadratic f(x)=<x,Ax>/2 for the actual prefix. The k+2 formal
columns (x_0,g_0,...,g_k) evaluate the polynomials

    1, z, z(1-h_0 z),...,z product_(t<k)(1-h_t z)

at the distinct eigenvalues, with nonzero coordinate factors. Their degrees
are 0,...,k+1 and their leading coefficients are nonzero, so the evaluation
matrix is invertible. All distinct prefix positions differ, including from
zero: their polynomial differences are nonzero and cannot vanish at all
k+2 nodes. For every distinct prefix pair,

    Q_ij=-<x_i-x_j,A(I-A)(x_i-x_j)>/2<0.

Adjoin a nonzero w_term in one fresh orthogonal coordinate. The enlarged
Gram matrix is positive definite and <g_k,w_term>=0. Choose gamma_term
sufficiently negative to make every Bterm_j strictly negative. This is
a strict point for every inequality, inside the orthogonality equality.

## 5. Separation and an attained full-space dual

Here are explicit dual-attainment details; Slater is not used as an unexplained
black box. Collect all the Q and Bterm residuals into a vector R(z), with
z=(G_term,f,gamma_term), and set E(G_term)=2<g_k,w_term>.
Consider the set O of (s,t,c,e) for which there exists G_term positive
definite and real f,gamma_term with

    s>R(z) coordinatewise,
    t>G_term_(x0,x0)-1,
    c<gamma_term,
    e=E(G_term).

It is convex. It is open: a positive definite witness and its finitely many
strict margins persist under sufficiently small changes; any small requested
change in e is realized by a small change in the (g_k,w_term) Gram entry.
It is nonempty, and (0,0,v_line,0) is outside it by definition of v_line.
Finite-dimensional separation gives a nonzero functional with coefficients
(lambda,alpha,q_cert,-eta,b), where lambda are the Q coefficients and alpha
are the Bterm coefficients. Independent upward changes of s,t and downward
changes of c force lambda,alpha,q_cert,eta>=0; b is unrestricted.

One has eta>0. If eta=0 and any of lambda,alpha,q_cert is positive, substitute
the strict equality-feasible point of Section4 into the separating inequality
and take limits to its residuals. Its left side is strictly negative and
E=0, a contradiction. If all those coefficients vanish, a nonzero functional
could only be b e, but O contains e of both signs, also a contradiction.
Divide all coefficients by eta to set eta=1.

Taking limits gives, for EVERY G_term PSD and EVERY real f,gamma_term,

    sum lambda_ij Q_ij+sum alpha_j Bterm_j
      +q_cert(G_term_(x0,x0)-1)-gamma_term+b E(G_term)
                          >=-v_line.                  (6)

The extension from positive definite to PSD uses G_term+epsilon I followed
by epsilon down to zero; there is no equality restriction in (6), because e
is itself an output coordinate of O. Free gamma_term in (6) forces
sum_j alpha_j=1. Free f_i force the COMPLETE prefix flow

    out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i,          0<=i<=k.

After these cancellations, (6) contains the full quadratic

    M_line=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_j alpha_j delta_j
       +||w_term||^2
       -2<w_term,x_k-sum_j alpha_j y_j-b g_k>
       +sum_(i!=j in J)lambda_ij Q_ij.                  (7)

This is PSD on ALL formal vectors, without imposing E=0: otherwise a scaled
rank-one Gram matrix along a negative direction contradicts (6). Taking zero
Gram in (6) yields q_cert<=v_line. Conversely, evaluate (7) on a feasible
point of (1). Its unreduced form is

    M_line=q_cert||x_0||^2-gamma_term
                 +sum lambda Q+sum alpha Bterm+bE.

All added residual terms are nonpositive and E=0; PSD gives
gamma_term<=q_cert||x_0||^2<=q_cert. Therefore v_line<=q_cert, so equality
holds and this dual is attained.

Define

    V_alpha(b)=x_k-b g_k-sum_j alpha_j y_j,
    R_line=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_j alpha_j delta_j
                         -||V_alpha(b)||^2+sum lambda Q.

The precise certificate identity is

    M_line=||w_term-V_alpha(b)||^2+R_line.               (8)

As w_term is a free formal vector, full PSD is equivalent to PSD of the
entire prefix quadratic R_line. This is exactly the full certificate
parameterization of accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 for an appended step b,
except that separation has not yet proved b nonnegative.

## 6. Positivity of the recovered step, equality, and reconstruction

Let M_pre=W_k(h_pre)>0 and D=D_k. For every legal normalized prefix with
last function value t=f_k, convexity and Cauchy--Schwarz give
t<=<g_k,x_k><=D||g_k||. A step of length1 therefore satisfies

    2f_(k+1)<=2t-||g_k||^2<=2t-t^2/D^2.

Here 0<=t<=M_pre/2<=D^2/2, and the last expression is increasing in this
interval. Consequently

    W_(k+1)(h_pre,1)
        <=M_pre-M_pre^2/(4D^2)<M_pre.                   (9)

By (4), v_line is at most the left side. If the attained dual in (8) had
b<=0, then its full PSD identity would still bound every actual trajectory
with that real final step: interpolation necessity is independent of the
sign of a chosen step. Convexity gives

    f(x_k-b g_k)>=f(x_k)-b||g_k||^2>=f(x_k)

for each instance. Taking the supremum over prefixes contradicts
q_cert=v_line<M_pre. Hence b>0. Now (8) is a legal full upper certificate
for the nonnegative schedule (h_pre,b), so

    W_(k+1)(h_pre,b)<=v_line<=rho_last(h_pre)
                              <=W_(k+1)(h_pre,b).

All quantities are equal. This proves (2), finite final-step attainment,
and the claimed exact optimization order without any minimax assumption.

Take ANY primal maximizer of (1), and form its maximal interpolant F.
Pair it with ANY optimal final step b. At this step, (3) and normalization
give equal endpoints:

    v_line<=2<x_k-b g_k,w_term>-2H(w_term)
       <=2F(x_k-b g_k)<=v_line||x_0||^2<=v_line.

Because v_line>0, ||x_0||=1. Uniqueness of the conjugate maximizer gives

    grad F(x_k-b g_k)=w_term,
    2F(x_k-b g_k)=v_line.                              (10)

Thus the auxiliary vector is a TRUE terminal gradient at a point on the
original gradient ray. Every omitted incoming terminal inequality holds
for this actual F. No collinearity constraint has been silently dropped.

Every optimal final b has a selected tight full certificate by the accepted
predecessor. Substituting its alpha and prefix multipliers into (7)-(8)
gives an optimal dual of (1) with equality multiplier b. This proves the
converse assertion as well.

Here g_k and w_term are nonzero: if g_k=0 the actual prefix point is a
minimizer, contradicting (10), and w_term=0 contradicts the positive value
and the star inequality. Cocoercivity between the last query and (10),
using <g_k,w_term>=0, yields

    b||g_k||^2 >= ||g_k-w_term||^2
                 =||g_k||^2+||w_term||^2.

Hence EVERY optimal final step satisfies

    b>=1+||w_term||^2/||g_k||^2>1.                      (11)

The same primal maximizer and its one function F work for every such b,
and (3) gives 2F(x_k-b' g_k)>=v_line for ALL b'>=0. This also proves
the final-stage minimax identity in the original class:

    inf_(b>=0) sup_(legal instances) terminal_error
       =sup_(legal instances) inf_(b>=0) terminal_error.

The supremum on the right is attained by this one F. It lives in the span
of the k+2 prefix formal vectors: at equality (8) gives w_term=V_alpha(b).

## 7. Zero steps and full original-coordinate lifting

For a prefix with zero steps, compress its positive steps, retaining as
representatives the original indices of those steps and original index k
for the last queried gradient. If two queried positions coincide, their
two ordered Q inequalities add to ||g_i-g_j||^2<=0; then their individual
inequalities give f_i=f_j. Thus feasible original prefixes, including their
Gram matrices, reduce to exactly the corresponding compressed prefixes.
Their terminal constraints Bterm duplicate those at identical jets.
This proves equality of the primal SDP values in both directions, and
the same equality of actual W values follows from the trajectory recursion.

Apply Sections4-6 to the positive compressed prefix, including length zero.
Lift its dual by setting omitted alpha and prefix multipliers to zero and
using the coordinate selection of the accepted fact. The equality multiplier
still multiplies 2<g_k,w_term>, with original last index k. The full lifted
matrix is T_pick^T Mhat_line T_pick; unused formal-gradient columns are zero.
It is PSD on the ENTIRE original formal space without imposing any duplicate
gradient equality there. The same b and q_cert are therefore valid, attained,
and optimal. This treats leading, interior, trailing, and all-zero prefixes.

## 8. A quantitative two-curvature prefix-polynomial guard

Here is an explicit feasible family, including a closed optimized value.
Choose distinct theta_1,theta_2 in (0,1] for which

    P_l=product_(t<k)(1-h_t theta_l)!=0,    l=1,2.

Put c_l=P_l^2>0, d_l=c_l theta_l^3, and

    omega=sqrt(d_2)/(sqrt(d_1)+sqrt(d_2)).

For the FIXED legal quadratic f(z_1,z_2)=(theta_1 z_1^2+theta_2 z_2^2)/2
and initial point (sqrt(omega),sqrt(1-omega)), its normalized error curve
after the supplied prefix and an arbitrary final b is

    E(b)=omega c_1 theta_1(1-b theta_1)^2
                  +(1-omega)c_2 theta_2(1-b theta_2)^2.

The quadratic coefficient is D_spec=omega d_1+(1-omega)d_2>0, and its
minimizing b is

    b_spec=[omega c_1 theta_1^2+(1-omega)c_2 theta_2^2]/D_spec>0.

Expanding the discriminant gives, for every b>=0,

    E(b)=q_spec+D_spec(b-b_spec)^2,

    q_spec=theta_1 theta_2(theta_1-theta_2)^2 c_1 c_2
                  / (sqrt(d_1)+sqrt(d_2))^2 >0.          (12)

For an arbitrary weight w in [0,1], the minimum is the same numerator
times w(1-w)/(w d_1+(1-w)d_2). Its maximum over w is attained at omega,
with the factor 1/(sqrt(d_1)+sqrt(d_2))^2. This follows from

    w d_1+(1-w)d_2
      -w(1-w)(sqrt(d_1)+sqrt(d_2))^2
        =(w sqrt(d_1)-(1-w)sqrt(d_2))^2>=0.

Thus the explicitly optimized two-coordinate family gives the quantitative
full-prefix-polynomial bound

    rho_last(h_pre)>=q_spec.                            (13)

All data are from one deterministic function, and its stationary terminal
gradient is a feasible w_term in (1). This illustrates (5) without assuming
any reduction of the complete SDP to quadratics. Its P_l retain the complete
prefix product but discard order; no constant-preserving comparison with
the Zhang--Jiang profile is asserted for this restricted spectral family.

## 9. Scope and literature audit

The equality (2) is a one-stage result. Earlier GD steps remain inside every
Q inequality, so there is no exchange over them. The probabilities alpha_j
are certificate weights, while b is one fixed algorithmic step recovered from
an equality multiplier. The full prefix PSD condition in (8) is indispensable.

The primary paper de Klerk, Glineur, Taylor, arXiv1606.09365v2, was downloaded
as PDF and HTML, its HTML text extracted, and Sections2.1,3.2,6 read. It treats
strongly convex functions and an initial FUNCTION-gap normalization. Its
multi-step orthogonality SDP is explicitly a relaxation of the collinearity
constraints; tightness there uses a matching quadratic. It is not an applicable
black box for (2). Its discussion motivated checking reconstruction carefully.
No external theorem from it is a proof premise here, and no literature result
is being promoted without the project's verifier gate.
