## DOC-003188 — project/research/shared_evidence/ca69568f812f2b3d.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\shared_evidence\ca69568f812f2b3d.md

原始字节数：23275；原始 SHA-256：bb6fc50e9ec42e12f7e6aac4320adc69b5426875e94a9ae1d2d383980782134f

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003188 -->
# C75. Exact phase threshold for the actual C72 seed substitution

Status: UNVERIFIED joint research draft. Sixth max3 launch, second and final
authorized round, cumulative round 15. This is output (3) of TASK.md: a
concrete missing scalar estimate, with the mappings, full-function lift and
limits made explicit. There is no new seed, quantitative improving phase
certificate, optimizer call, phase scan, installation or verifier call.

The accepted background used here is fact 0be8b54e0a33381c: its exact
benchmark recurrences, the actual Huber lower bound for every nonnegative
word, and the square-root contraction of the dominant join. Its full proof
was read. All new certificate algebra is reproduced here; C72 is not used
as a verified predecessor. The explicitly identified external scalar phase
theorems below are matched literature inputs to this joint candidate, not
new facts in the project graph.

## 1. Source matching, indexing, and objective normalization

Use the exact S_circle and S_bullet recurrences of fact 0be8b54e0a33381c.
Write

    P_n=1+S_circle(n),  B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1,  U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2),  rho=log_2(lambda).

Thus P_n is also the C_n of the accepted project glossary; it is not the
prefix sum P_j used in the single-cut witness. Define the homogeneous joins

    Omega(a,c)=(a+c+sqrt((a+c)^2+4ac))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

Direct substitution of the accepted phi and psi gives

    P_0=B_0=1,
    P_n=max_(k+l+1=n) Omega(P_k,P_l),
    B_n=max_(k+l+1=n) G(P_k,B_l).                         (1)

For a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) denotes the
supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite
Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz
gradient, every minimizer x_star and x_0!=x_star, under
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Smoothness L>0 is fixed.

The primary source is Yu Liu, Kang Chen, Rujun Jiang and Tianyu Wang,
*Optimal Recursive Composition and Dyadic Phase Laws for Gradient Descent
with Predetermined Stepsizes*, arXiv:2609.11788v1 (2026),
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1 . Its HTML/PDF are in downloads, and
the extracted text was read. Two Matlas queries returned unrelated papers;
this source is web-grounded, NOT represented as a Matlas hit. The requested
local SOURCE_PHASE_AMPLIFICATION_20260927.md was absent.

Put hats on the source's scalar quantities to avoid its overloaded W:

    source N = our n+1,
    source K = Omega, source A(u,w) = G(u,w),
    source rho = our lambda, source p = our rho,
    source Uhat_N = P_(N-1), source What_N = B_(N-1).       (2)

Here is an induction, rather than a transfer of certificate classes.
Proposition 2.5 has Uhat_1=1 and
Uhat_N=max_(1<=a<N) Omega(Uhat_a,Uhat_(N-a)). With k=a-1 and l=N-a-1,
this is the first recursion in (1). Source equations (34)-(37) have
eta^F_1=1, What_N=1/eta^F_N, and
What_N=max_(1<=a<N) G(Uhat_a,What_(N-a)). The same k,l substitution gives
the second recursion in (1), including its initial value. This proves (2).

Source equation (35) bounds the gap by eta^F_N*(L/2)||x_0-x_star||^2.
Consequently eta^F_(n+1)=1/B_n is the project's normalized coefficient W
for those benchmark schedules. If R denotes gap/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),
our W=2R and source eta=2R. The source What_N is a reciprocal denominator,
not our worst-case functional W_n(h). No proximal or gradient-norm metric
is imported. The arbitrary primitive seed below is never asserted to be
s-composable.

The minimal external input for the main limit calculation is Theorem 6.3:
there is a positive Lipschitz 1-periodic Psi_F such that, with

    C^F_N=N^rho/B_(N-1),
    eps_N=C^F_N-Psi_F(log_2 N),

the supremum of |eps_N| over 2^j<=N<=2^(j+1) tends to zero. Theorem 6.5
adds nonconstancy. For the separate baseline-gap observation in section 7
we also use Theorem 5.5 and the enclosure in Theorem 6.5:

    P_(N-1)=N^rho Phi(log_2 N),  phi_min=min Phi in (0,1),
    max Phi=Phi(0)=1,
    c_star<min Psi_F<max Psi_F<=c_star/phi_min.             (3)

For section 9, source Lemma 6.4 also supplies the support inequality
G(a,d)^q_pow<=K_sup*a^q_pow+d^q_pow for a,d>=0, where q_pow=1/rho,
K_sup=max_(0<=x<=1) 2^q_pow*(1-x^(2q_pow))/(1-x)^q_pow,
the continuous endpoint values are 2^q_pow and 0, and c_star=K_sup^(-rho).
For all other sections, only positivity of c_star and (3) are used.
All profiles are
functions on R/Z, so arguments are understood modulo one. The proofs of
Theorem 6.3, Appendix D.2, D.3-D.9, and the relevant symmetric-phase
proofs were read. The uniformity follows there from adjacent-value control,
compactness and dense fixed-ray limits; the strict inequalities in (3)
come from support-deficit concentration. They do not provide an explicit
amplitude or an oriented comparison for the phases needed below.

## 2. Full original-space seed condition

Normalize L=1, translate a selected minimizer to zero, and subtract f_star
so that its value is also zero. For a finite
nonnegative word h of length r, let s=sum h_i, A=1+s, and retain every
formal gradient g_0,...,g_r, including gradients at zero steps. Put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
          +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0. We call the seed
primitive when V_h>=0 on every such trajectory in every finite dimension.
A concrete sufficient certificate retains ALL ordered finite multipliers
Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j, and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column, and set

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

The feasibility conditions are

    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0.            (4)

Indeed substituting the GD equations and collecting function coefficients
and gradient inner products gives the exact identity

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

To audit the matrix formula, the position terms of sum Lambda Q contribute
sym(L_flow(T-I)); the squared-difference terms contribute
sym(L_flow)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V_h's diagonal yields (4).
Thus (4) proves the required actual primitive inequality. It has no support,
rank, symmetry, quotient, or terminal-edge restriction.

Adding optimum multipliers cannot supply a missing positive x_0 diagonal:
that diagonal is zero. Its mixed coefficient with g_i is
-Lambda_(i,star), so PSD forces each Lambda_(i,star)=0. Summing the finite
function-flow equations then forces sum_i Lambda_(star,i)=0, and their
nonnegativity makes all these multipliers zero. Therefore the original
formal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_r) is retained, with its x_0 row zero.
No equality between formal gradients at duplicated query positions is
imposed. For the implication used here, sufficiency of (4) is enough;
no unproved dual attainment is invoked.

## 3. Complete primitive and dominant composition identities

Primitive join: let a,b be primitive, with capacities A=1+sum a and
C=1+sum b. Choose d>0 by d(A+C+d)=AC, put T_mass=A+C+d and
kappa=(T_mass/C)^2, and use the physical word h=(a,1+d,b).
Let p be its bridge query, z its terminal query, and j range over the
nonterminal queries of b. Direct expansion gives

    V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1) E_PP,
    E_PP=sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z)
                                      +sum_j b_j Q_(j,z).       (5)

For a coefficient audit, the value part of E_PP is
2sum b_j f_j+(d-C)f_p+(2-C-d)f_z. Its gradient part is

    sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2
       +[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_p||^2+(C+d)||g_z||^2/2.

The remaining coefficient identities are
(kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d), kappa d^2=A^2, and kappa C^2=T_mass^2.
They follow from d T_mass=AC. Also 0<d<C and kappa>1, so all
added multipliers are nonnegative. If the child certificates have arbitrary
PSD remainders, the full parent remainder is their original-coordinate sum
M_a+kappa M_b, embedded on the actual child-gradient indices. The resulting
capacity is exactly Omega(A,C).
The physical bridge obeys 1+d=phi(A-1,C-1), as required by (1).

Dominant join: for a word h with terminal z, put D=2sum h+1. For
nonnegative weights u_i summing to D define

    J_(h,u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z
                                  -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.    (6)

The empty word has u=1 and J=0. A primitive word is dominant with
u=(h,s+1), since direct expansion gives J=V_h. In general J>=0 implies
2f_z<=||x_0||^2/D on every actual trajectory.

Let a be a primitive left child of capacity A and b a dominant right child
with mass D and weights v. Define

    mu=2A/D,  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2,
    gamma=A+1,  beta=1+A/zeta.

Use the physical parent word (a,beta,b), and weights u=(a,gamma,zeta v).
Their mass is G(A,D)=D zeta^2=2A+zeta D=2sum(parent)+1. With p the
bridge query, all right-child queries i>p, including its terminal, give

    J_parent=V_a+zeta^2 J_right
                         -2mu sum_(i>p)v_i Q_(i,p).             (7)

The physical bridge is exactly beta=psi(A-1,(D-1)/2); multiply
zeta^2-zeta=2A/D by D/zeta to check this against the radical in psi.

This is an equality on the full original formal space. Function
coefficients use mu D=2A. Expanding all squares and the GD bridge uses

    (A-1)A+mu D+gamma=gamma^2,
    zeta^2=zeta+mu,  zeta^2 beta=mu+gamma zeta;

these cancel the mixed terms and give (7). The child remainder is pulled
back from the actual right-child starting position x_p-beta g_p; hence the
original x_0 and all inherited gradients remain in the certificate.
All extra interpolation coefficients are nonnegative. Induction on the
actual tree supplies a full PSD objective certificate: divide (6), after
inserting its nonnegative interpolation representation, by D. Its PSD
remainder is the retained final square plus the embedded child remainders,
all divided by D. This is an all-function objective bound, not a bound on
a quadratic, projection or temporal subclass.

## 4. The exact physical substitution and the untouched dominant leaf

Fix an ACTUAL seed a satisfying section 2, of length r=m-1 and capacity
A=1+sum a>=1. For each n choose a fully expanded maximizing dominant tree
for B_n in (1), for example the smallest maximizing split at each node.
The unique dominant right spine ends at one dominant empty leaf. Every
other leaf is primitive: there are exactly n of them, by induction on (1).

Replace each primitive empty leaf by the actual word a, retaining every
original node, and recompute each physical bridge by (5) or (7). Leave the
last dominant empty leaf empty, with mass 1. Call the resulting word H_n
and its true mass D_n=2sum H_n+1. There are n old bridge steps and nr
inserted seed steps. Thus

    length(H_n)=mn,  W_(mn)(H_n)<=1/D_n.                       (8)

Every primitive branch capacity is multiplied by A, by homogeneity of
Omega. If the last dominant leaf were ALSO changed from mass 1 to A in
an auxiliary scalar evaluation, homogeneity of G would make the root
A B_n. This last change is scalar only, not an additional physical block.
The square-root join is

    sqrt(G(a,d))=(sqrt(d)+sqrt(d+8a))/2.

Fact 0be8b54e0a33381c proves that it is increasing and 1-Lipschitz in
sqrt(d). Propagating the final-leaf difference along the whole spine gives

    sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)<=sqrt(D_n)<=sqrt(A B_n).            (9)

Writing c_A=sqrt(A)-1 and ell_n=c_A/sqrt(A B_n), this implies

    (1-ell_n)^2<=D_n/(A B_n)<=1,
    0<=A B_n-D_n<=2c_A sqrt(A B_n)-c_A^2.                    (10)

The lower square-root bound is positive because B_n>=1. The accepted
bound B_n>=2n+1 already makes ell_n tend to zero; the phase theorem gives
the sharper ell_n=O(n^(-rho/2)). The bound is independent of tree depth,
split ties and multiplier choices.

In fact (8) is equality. The actual nonnegative word H_n has sum
(D_n-1)/2. The one-dimensional Huber function in fact 0be8b54e0a33381c,
started at x_0=1 with threshold 1/D_n and minimizer 0, attains normalized
error 1/D_n on that entire word. Its initial distance is genuinely 1.
Together with the full upper certificate this proves, within the joint
candidate,

    W_(mn)(H_n)=1/D_n.                                      (11)

Restoring any L>0 by multiplying the normalized objective by L leaves
the specified h_i/L GD updates and the ratio 2gap/(L distance^2) unchanged.

## 5. Exact phase comparison, errors, and attaining sequences

Let X=n+1 and Y=mn+1=mX-(m-1). Source matching and the definition of eps
give the exact scalar ratio

    B_(mn)/B_n=(Y/X)^rho
        [Psi_F(log_2 X)+eps_X]/[Psi_F(log_2 Y)+eps_Y].         (12)

Thus the normalized actual objective satisfies

    B_(mn) W_(mn)(H_n)
      =(1/A)(Y/X)^rho
        [Psi_F(log_2 X)+eps_X]/[Psi_F(log_2 Y)+eps_Y]
        *[A B_n/D_n].                                      (13)

The last factor lies in [1,(1-ell_n)^(-2)]. Formula (13) retains all
three errors: finite-index phase errors eps, Y=mX-(m-1) rather than mX,
and the untouched terminal leaf. If L_Psi is a Lipschitz constant, then

    dist(log_2 Y,log_2 X+log_2 m)
        <=(m-1)/(Y log 2),

using -log(1-u)<=u/(1-u). Since min Psi_F>0, the denominator in (12)
stays bounded away from zero for large n. Uniform eps convergence,
this phase displacement, and (10) therefore make (13) converge uniformly
in its moving input phase to

    R_m,A(t)=(m^rho/A) Psi_F(t)/Psi_F(t+log_2 m).              (14)

More explicitly, fix ANY t in [0,1) and set

    X_j=floor(2^(j+t)),  n_j=X_j-1,  output horizon=m n_j.

Then dist(log_2 X_j,t)<=1/(X_j log 2), and (13) tends to R_m,A(t).
This constructs the required inputs, including at a minimizer of (14).
It does not assume that a convenient dyadic ray happens to minimize it.

Define the now-identified scalar threshold

    tau_m=m^rho min_(t in R/Z) Psi_F(t)/Psi_F(t+log_2 m).      (15)

The minimum is attained. Compactness gives the reverse inequality for
any sequence n->infinity, and the displayed n_j attains it. Therefore

    liminf_(n->infinity) B_(mn) W_(mn)(H_n)=tau_m/A.           (16)

Consequently A>tau_m is NECESSARY AND SUFFICIENT for a fixed relative
improvement along some infinite subsequence of THIS actual substitution.
For sufficiency take delta=(1-tau_m/A)/2 and sufficiently large j in the
constructed minimizing-phase sequence. For necessity, a fixed improvement
would force the left side of (16) below 1. This is stronger than merely a
sufficient comparison of certificates because of the Huber equality (11).
It does not characterize other possible uses of the same seed.

## 6. What nonconstancy does and does not prove

For m=2^k, the phase shift is zero, so tau_m=m^rho. For every integer
m>=2 that is not a power of two, log_2 m is irrational: if it equalled
a/b with integers a,b>0, m^b=2^a would force m to be a power of two.
If Psi_F(t)>=Psi_F(t+log_2 m) for all t, integrate the continuous
nonnegative difference around the circle. Its integral is zero, so equality
holds everywhere. Iterating the irrational rotation and using continuity
would then make Psi_F constant. This contradicts Theorem 6.5. Thus

    tau_m<m^rho for every non-dyadic integer m.              (17)

This is a qualitative lowering, not the quantified threshold requested
as TASK output (1). It supplies neither a rational interval inside the
gap nor the sign of a comparison at a specified pair of rays.

A concrete caution: fix a nonzero irrational shift alpha and any
0<a_0<1. Let psi_e(t)=K(1+e cos(2pi(t-t_0))), with K>0 and

    0<e<(1-a_0)/(1+a_0).

It is positive, Lipschitz, periodic and nonconstant, but every ratio
psi_e(t)/psi_e(t+alpha)>a_0. Moreover at t=t_0 that ratio is strictly
greater than 1. Hence no prescribed quantitative lowering or specified
improving input phase follows from those qualitative properties alone.
If desired, this example also meets the numerical-free enclosure in (3):
take K=c_star*(1+1/phi_min)/2 and make e smaller than
min{1-c_star/K,c_star/(phi_min K)-1}. Those bounds are positive.
This is NOT a counterexample to the Bellman recurrence or to the real
source phase. It identifies exactly the missing Bellman information.

## 7. Comparison with the primitive baseline

There is an unconditional lower comparison within this same scalar model:

    tau_m>=P_(m-1).                                        (18)

To prove it, use a recursively generated maximizing primitive word of
length m-1 as the seed. Such a word is produced from empty leaves using
(5), and has capacity P_(m-1) by (1). Its substituted word is itself an
ordinary recursive dominant word of length mn, so D_n<=B_(mn) by (1).
Equation (9) with A=P_(m-1), followed by a sequence for any phase t, gives
m^rho Psi_F(t)/Psi_F(t+log_2 m)>=P_(m-1). Minimize over t.
Thus a seed capacity strictly above the primitive baseline is necessary;
strictly above the power envelope is sufficient; their difference cannot
be silently discarded.

One can further prove the existence of nondyadic lengths with a strictly
positive intervening gap, although these source inputs do not identify
such a length quantitatively. Let

    Gamma=(min Psi_F)/(max Psi_F).

By (3), Gamma>phi_min, and (15) gives tau_m>=m^rho Gamma. Choose t_star
with Phi(t_star)=phi_min. Integers
m_j=floor(2^(j+t_star)) approach this phase. Since phi_min<1=Phi(0),
these integers are nondyadic for large j. Continuity gives, eventually,

    P_(m_j-1)/m_j^rho=Phi(log_2 m_j)
                  <(phi_min+Gamma)/2<Gamma,
    tau_(m_j)-P_(m_j-1)
                  >(Gamma-phi_min)m_j^rho/2>0.               (19)

So the threshold of THIS operation is not universally identical to the
finite primitive baseline. This does not construct an actual primitive
seed in the intervening capacity interval, nor decide whether tau_3 or
tau_5 strictly exceeds its baseline. Those are separate scalar questions.

## 8. Concrete missing estimate and a fully specified next seed interface

For each fixed positive integer b define the exact ray value

    Z_b=lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1)
       =b^rho/Psi_F(log_2 b).                               (20)

Existence and the equality follow directly from the matched phase law.
Consider the explicit scalar estimate

    E5:    Z_15 <= (773/100) Z_3.                            (21)

It has NOT been proved here and could fail on these specific rays. It is
a concrete missing comparison, rather than an unnamed phase infimum. If
it holds, then ANY actual primitive seed of length 4 and capacity
A>773/100, satisfying (4), gives fixed asymptotic improvement by (13) on

    n_j=3*2^j-1,  output horizon=5n_j=15*2^j-5.

Indeed the output leaf count is 15*2^j-4; the bounded offset 4 disappears
by (12), and the exact limiting ratio is Z_15/(A Z_3). The last dominant
leaf remains unchanged throughout.

The target constant is strictly between the exact finite baseline and
the former sufficient power threshold, with an explicit baseline gap:

    P_4 < 77/10 < 773/100 < 5^rho,
    773/100-P_4 > 3/100.                                   (22)

Here is finite exact arithmetic establishing (22), independently of an
asymptotic phase scan. Write q_3=Omega(1,lambda). Then

    4<q_3<201/50,  P_3=lambda^2,
    P_4=Omega(lambda,q_3).

For the upper bound on q_3, its defining polynomial at 201/50 is greater
than 181/8750>0 after replacing sqrt(2) by 99/70; the larger root is q_3.
At 4 its value is 7-5sqrt(2)<0. Also
Omega(1,201/50)<29/5<lambda^2, proving P_3 from the two available splits.
Using lambda<169/70 and lambda^2<204/35 gives

    Omega(1,lambda^2)<38/5,
    Omega(lambda,4)>38/5,
    Omega(lambda,201/50)<77/10.

For these three claims, the relevant quadratic evaluations at 38/5,
38/5,77/10 are respectively >6/175, < -12/25, and >71/1750.
This proves both the maximizing P_4 split and P_4<77/10.
For the last power comparison, exact integer arithmetic gives

    lambda^70=a_70+b_70 sqrt(2),
    a_70=311363698964240484013304163,
    b_70=220167382952941249990598278,
    5a_70+7b_70-5*2^89=3140077278340482752898201>0,
    5^89*100^70>773^70.

Since sqrt(2)>7/5, these imply rho>89/70 and
5^rho>5^(89/70)>773/100. They are a few fixed integer/rational checks,
not a parameter scan; their displayed identities are the proof premises.

The conditional next seed problem is exactly: find h_0,...,h_3>=0 with
s=sum h_i>673/100, twenty nonnegative ordered Lambda_ij on i,j=0,...,4,
and the full five-by-five PSD matrix (4), with omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_3,2s)
and D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i),-s(s+2)). Retain the zero x_0 row, all five
formal gradients and every incoming terminal multiplier. No product,
sparsity, reflection, tensor or fixed-remainder restriction is added.
This is a concrete feasibility specification, not a claim of feasibility,
an allocated search, or a consequence already obtained from E5.

For the smallest nondyadic scalar multiplier m=3, the analogous explicit
test is Z_9<=(101/25)Z_3. It would use the same input n_j and output
9*2^j-3. Here P_2<201/50<101/25<3^rho, the last inequality following
from rho>89/70 and 3^89*25^70>101^70. This is also unproved. Length four
is specified above as the next seed interface beyond the previously
studied small-length obstructions; no unverified small-length obstruction
is used as a premise of this proof.

## 9. Direct attempts at (21), and the precise remaining obstacle

First attempt: use the source's reciprocal sandwich. On fixed rays it
gives 2P_(b-1)<=Z_b<=lambda P_(b-1). These separate upper/lower bounds
do not orient the two objective phases and retain the factor lambda/2>1.
The stronger enclosure (3) still gives only
Z_15/Z_3<5^rho/phi_min, which exceeds the desired power threshold. It
therefore cannot prove (21).

Second attempt: use the homogeneous support barrier at the first pivot.
Define the symmetric continuum capacity
P_cont(x)=x^rho Phi(log_2 x), and d_star=1/c_star. A prospective
one-pivot upper bound based only on the global continuation cap is

    sup_(0<=a<=x) G(P_cont(a),d_star*(x-a)^rho),
    P_cont(0)=0.

It equals d_star*x^rho: the support inequality bounds it above by this
quantity, but a=0 attains it because G(0,d)=d. Thus this relaxation
cannot detect any strict phase penalty. Deleting a=0 does not repair it,
since the same value is approached as a tends to zero. One must charge
the total cost of a string of small pivots before the first macroscopic
one, as the source's concentration proof does qualitatively, or retain a
sharper phase-dependent continuation bound. A finite prefix of dyadic
ray values supplies lower approximations; without a certified remainder
it supplies no upper enclosure for the numerator Z_15.

The cosine family in section 6 explicitly falsifies a proposed shortcut
from nonconstancy/continuity/enclosure to a predetermined gap or favorable
ray. It does not reject a phase-refinement program that uses more of (1).

The remaining scalar task is (21), an alternative explicitly quantified
phase pair with its own attaining integers, or a rigorous obstruction for
the proposed pair. Separately, no actual seed satisfying the resulting
full certificate and capacity requirement is known. The all-schedule
original target remains open, and no new fact has been submitted.

# Exact value-flow prices and two complete redundancy results

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft. This is max2's first analytical round
after completed baseline22, under TASK.md and guidance f7608aa358751108.
No numerical evaluation, solver, parameter scan, replay, machine algebra,
formal verification, installation, or subordinate agent was used.

Accepted inputs: 04219554e41d9750, Section10 (the ORIGINAL primitive
certificate), and 1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraphs1--2 (all-pair smooth
convex interpolation and its actual conjugate construction). Everything
else needed below is proved in this document. Earlier C82/C83 and coupling
drafts are comparison targets, never accepted premises.

The required concrete outcome is exact redundancy of value pricing on
the ENTIRE unit-curvature repaired-cut family, including all its legal
repair amplitudes, arbitrary nonnegative words, zero steps, and all
original coordinates. The lifted-cut geometry in2948d4cd3660413f also has
identically zero price. A separate C82 calculation gives an exact price
and a fully minimized formula in a specified parameter domain. It proves
redundancy there when a^2+a<=1; its complementary regime is not asserted
to resolve a surviving full-cone region.

The original asymptotic target, existence/nonexistence of a threshold
seed, and general four-step cone reversal remain OPEN. No new fact is
asserted and no theorem below is promoted past the verifier gate.

## 1. Original orientation, objective, and formal coordinates

Let n>=0, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a finite nonnegative word, s=sum h_i,
C=1+s, and I={0,...,n}. Keep every original gradient coordinate, including
the terminal coordinate and every coordinate at a zero step. Fixed
physical vectors x_i,g_i in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space obey

    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.

For sampled values f_i put

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V(f)=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2.       (1)

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 proves Q_ij<=0 for every ordered pair of actual
smooth convex samples, and constructs a single interpolant from these
inequalities. Assume initially that all these finite inequalities hold.
An optimum sample will be retained explicitly when it is part of the
fixed geometry; Section3 also handles the case where its location is free.

For the original nonnegative multipliers Lambda_ij, put Lambda_ii=0 and

    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
    L^T e=omega.                                         (2)

Here e is the (n+1)-entry all-ones column. Thus omega_i is outgoing minus
incoming flow. The TERMINAL is the source, of mass2s, and index i<n is
a sink of mass2h_i. Reversing these arrows gives the wrong value problem.

With T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i, let

    D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<n, -s(s+2)),
    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives, on every physical realization,

    V(f)+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.   (3)

The full certificate requires M PSD on ALL of R^(n+1). Nothing below
deletes a coordinate, restricts Lambda support in that feasibility
question, or identifies formal gradients at zero steps. Sparse flows
constructed below solve an auxiliary transport minimum; they are NOT
claimed to have PSD matrices.

## 2. An exact finite price, with elementary proof and attained flow

Fix legal base values f_i and put w_ij=-Q_ij>=0 for distinct i,j. Regard
w_ij as the cost of the directed arc i->j. Let d_i be the least cost of
a path from n to i, with d_n=0. The complete finite graph makes every
distance finite. A minimizing path exists: a repeated-vertex segment
has nonnegative cost and can be deleted, so it is enough to consider
finitely many simple paths.

For replacement values f_i+z_i, the complete constraints are exactly

    z_i-z_j<=w_ij.                                      (4)

Summing along an n->i path gives z_n-z_i<=its cost. Therefore

    z_i-z_n>=-d_i,
    V(f+z)>=V(f)-Pflow,
    Pflow=2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.                             (5)

All h_i are nonnegative, including possibly zero. The simultaneous
choice z_i=-d_i attains every lower bound in(5): the distance inequality
d_j<=d_i+w_ij is precisely its feasibility in(4). Thus this is an
attained minimum of the value problem, not only a lower bound from a
dual program.

The same number is the exact minimum slack cost of the ORIGINAL flow:

    min_{Lambda>=0, L^T e=omega} sum Lambda_ij w_ij
       =Pflow.                                         (6)

To prove the lower bound, multiply w_ij>=d_j-d_i by Lambda_ij and sum.
The result is

    sum Lambda_ij w_ij >= -sum_i omega_i d_i
                         =2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.

For attainment, choose a shortest n->i path for every i<n with h_i>0
and send mass2h_i along that path. Sum these path flows. Intermediate
vertices have equal incoming and outgoing mass; the endpoints give
exactly(2). Each path contributes2h_i d_i. Zero-weight sinks need no
path, but their vertices and all their available arcs remain present.

Combining(3) with(6) gives a necessary condition for any full certificate:

    V(f)>=Pflow.                                        (7)

The selected optimal values give the same condition as V(f-d)>=0. Indeed
their new slacks are w'_ij=w_ij+d_i-d_j, and every prescribed-flow array
satisfies

    sum Lambda_ij w'_ij=sum Lambda_ij w_ij-Pflow.

The Gram matrix and the formal certificate matrix do not change with
the values. The full PSD restriction can impose more than the minimum
over flows in(6); it has not been dropped from the original problem.

### Zero costs, coincident gradients, and fixed optimum constraints

No strict positivity of costs is needed. A zero-cost directed cycle
has every arc of cost zero, and(4) then forces equal shifts around that
cycle. Distances and the path-flow construction remain valid without
choosing a unique shortest path.

If two base gradients coincide, Q_ij+Q_ji=0, so legality forces BOTH
residuals to vanish. Hence replacement shifts at these two samples are
equal, even when their positions differ. If two base positions coincide,
the sum of the two residuals is ||g_i-g_j||^2; legality first forces
coincident gradients and then coincident values. These are properties of
actual jets, not extra formal equations in the PSD certificate.

One may also include a fixed optimum jet x_star=g_star=f_star=0 as an
extra graph vertex. Let d_i^* be distances in this larger graph. Then
z_i=d_star^*-d_i^* is feasible, has z_star=0, and minimizes the same
value objective subject to ALL finite and optimum pairs. Its improvement
is2sum h_i d_i^*. In general d_i^*<=d_i, so this price need not equal
the original finite-flow price. They WILL agree in every concrete family
below. The distinction is important: optimum edges are not original
primitive multipliers and cannot silently be used as such.

## 3. The optimized finite values give one static function and real minima

This section also explains why(5), without a fixed optimum vertex, is
not merely a formal jet result. For any legal replacement values f'_i
on the finite samples, set

    y_i=x_i-g_i, beta_i=||g_i||^2/2-f'_i,
    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I){<y_i,v>+beta_i},
    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                              (8)

This is the construction in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, written out here
to identify its minimizers. H is finite, continuous, 1-strongly convex,
and has quadratic growth. The supremum has one maximizer v(x). Strong
subgradient inequalities give

    <x-x',v(x)-v(x')>>=||v(x)-v(x')||^2,

and the maximizing inequalities give

    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<v(x),x'-x>
      <=<v(x')-v(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

Thus F is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with
gradient v(x). The all-pair inequalities say that at v=g_i the i-th
quadratic in H is active and has gradient x_i. Consequently v(x_i)=g_i
and F(x_i)=f'_i. This is ONE fixed F used for all steps.

Let beta_max=max_i beta_i, and A={i:beta_i=beta_max}. The exact minimizer
set of F is

    argmin F=conv{y_i:i in A},   min F=-beta_max.          (9)

Indeed gradient F(x)=0 is equivalent to x being a subgradient of H at0.
The subgradients there are exactly the convex hull of the active affine
slopes y_i. To check the reverse inclusion without an unproved formula,
if x is outside that convex hull, a separating vector u has
<x,u> greater than every <y_i,u> for i in A. For sufficiently small
positive t, inactive affine pieces remain inactive at tu, and
H(tu)-H(0)=t max_(i in A)<y_i,u>+t^2||u||^2/2,
contradicting the subgradient inequality for x. The forward inclusion
follows by convexly combining the active supporting inequalities.

In particular a real minimizer always exists, even if none was fixed in
the graph. The true initial distance is exactly

    dist(x_0,conv{y_i:i in A}).                           (10)

No arbitrary radius or changed function is inserted into the GD run.
The specific families below either preserve the optimum0 or identify
the closest minimizer explicitly.

## 4. Entire unit-curvature repaired-cut family: price exactly zero

This is the main complete redundancy result. It works for any n>=1,
any nonnegative word, and any cut j with0<=j<n and h_j>1. Put

    P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t,
    eta=(h_j-1)/(1+T_j)>0,
    p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for0<=i<=j.

Choose an amplitude epsilon>=0. Define two scalar coordinate arrays:

    xB_i=1+sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t, gB_i=1        (i<=j),
    xA_i=gA_i=p_i                            (i<=j),

    R_k=sum_(t=k)^(n-1)h_t                    (k>j),
    xB_k=-eta*(1+R_k), gB_k=-eta              (k>j),
    xA_k=p_j*xB_k, gA_k=-eta*p_j              (k>j).

Empty sums have value zero. At the cut,
xB_(j+1)=1-h_j=-eta*(1+T_j), and the same equality multiplied by p_j
holds for coordinate A. Before the cut the A coordinate follows unit
quadratic GD. After it both coordinates have constant gradients. Hence
the vectors

    X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*xA_i),
    G_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*gA_i),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2                            (11)

obey every original GD equation. Include X_star=G_star=F_star=0.

Here are ALL interpolation pairs. For saturated values(11),

    Q_ik=<G_i-G_k,X_k-G_k>.

For a prefix target k<=j, its normal is

    X_k-G_k=(sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t,0).

Every prefix source has residual zero. Every tail source has residual
-(1+eta)sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0. The optimum source has residual
-sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0.

For a tail target k>j, its normal is

    X_k-G_k=-eta R_k*(1,sqrt(epsilon)*p_j).

Every tail source has residual zero. A prefix source i<=j has residual

    -eta R_k*[1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)].           (12)

The optimum source has residual
-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2)<=0. Every pair into the optimum is zero.
These cases exhaust all directed finite and optimum pairs.

If T_j>0, legality is EXACTLY the finite set of inequalities

    1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)>=0   for i<=j.        (13)

The necessity follows by using target j+1, where R_(j+1)=T_j>0.
If T_j=0, every tail normal vanishes and no condition(13) is necessary.
Zero p_j, zero epsilon, zero intermediate steps and repeated gradients
cause no exception in these formulas. This specifies the full legal
repair-amplitude family, rather than only its most-used endpoint.

For EVERY legal epsilon, the finite zero-cost arcs include

    n->j,
    j->i for every i<j,
    n->k for every j<k<n,
    i->n for every i<n.                                 (14)

The first and last groups hold because X_j=G_j and X_n=G_n. The second
group holds because both sources and targets are prefix samples. The
third holds because all tail gradients coincide. Thus every original
sample is in the zero-cost component of n, so

    d_i=d_i^*=0 for EVERY i,
    Pflow=0.                                           (15)

Equivalently, all feasible value replacements have z_i=z_n. With the
fixed zero optimum, Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=0 also forces z_n=0. Thus ALL
sampled values in(11) are forced, not merely optimal for this objective.

An explicit attaining ORIGINAL flow sends2sum_(i<=j)h_i from n to j,
sends2h_i from j to i for i<j, and sends2h_k directly from n to k for
j<k<n. Its other entries vanish. The balance is(2), and every used arc
is in(14). It has zero cost. This does not assert its matrix is PSD.

There is one actual static smooth convex witness for every legal
amplitude: use(8) with the zero optimum included. For its prefix samples,
beta_i=-sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t; for its tail samples,
beta_k=-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2). These constants are nonpositive,
and equality in either formula implies that the corresponding normal
is zero. The optimum sample also has constant and normal zero. Thus(9)
gives the UNIQUE minimizer0 for this single conjugate interpolant. Its
TRUE squared initial distance is(1+P_j)^2+epsilon, in every case above.

The projection construction used in the earlier drafts is also valid:
let K=conv({0} union {G_i}) and define

    F(X)=max_(G in K){<X,G>-||G||^2/2}.                  (16)

The complete normal list proves that the optimizer at X_i is G_i.
Projection onto K is1-Lipschitz: add the two projection normal
inequalities to obtain ||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>. The same maximizing
inequalities used in Section3 prove differentiability. Since0 is in K,
F>=0=F(0). This is a real minimizer, and(11) are its actual values.

The saturated-value telescoping identity is

    V=||X_0||^2-||X_n+sG_n||^2.

To check it, use2h_i F_i+h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2
=||X_i||^2-||X_(i+1)||^2, sum, and substitute F_n.
Here X_n=G_n, so the family gives exactly

    V=(1+P_j)^2+epsilon
         -C^2 eta^2*(1+epsilon*p_j^2).                  (17)

Reoptimizing values adds NO inequality to nonnegativity of(17), for
any legal epsilon, word, cut or zero-step pattern. Consequently it adds
none to an existing collection of necessary conditions that already
includes this ENTIRE family. This is a family-level exact redundancy
claim, not global redundancy for arbitrary queried geometries.

### The actual four-step C83 member and true radius

Take h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), a,b,c,d>0, j=2, and

    C=5+a+b+c+d, Q=3+a+b, eta=c/(2+d), p_2=ab.

The only possibly negative coefficient in(13) is i=1, where p_1=-a.
If b eta<1, the full amplitude interval is

    0<=epsilon<=(1+eta)/(a^2 b(1-b eta)).                (18)

The other two coefficients have positive p_i. The endpoint in(18)
is exactly the repaired-cut value used in d3abee1b17da52da; there is
no new function-value freedom at ANY amplitude in this interval.

For0<ab<1 and epsilon>0, the two prefix gradients
(1,sqrt(epsilon)) and(1,-a sqrt(epsilon)) have G_2 in the relative
interior of their segment. Since G_3=-eta G_2, zero is in the interior
of their nondegenerate triangle with G_3. Thus the minimizer of(16) is
uniquely0: a nonzero normal at0 would have positive pairing with a
point of an open ball in K. The TRUE squared distance is Q^2+epsilon.
At epsilon=0 the instance is scalar and0 is again the closest minimizer
to the displayed initial point. No benchmark ratio is inferred from V.

## 5. The lifted-cut coupling geometry also has zero value price

This section reconstructs the actual geometry used in draft
2948d4cd3660413f, independently of that draft's quantitative matrix gap.
Let a,b,c,d>0, put u=1+a, C=5+a+b+c+d, H=3+c+d, eta=b/H,
and assume a eta<1. Define

    nu=(1+eta)(1-a eta)/a>0,
    U=(1,0), Z=-eta U+sqrt(nu)*(0,1),
    X=(U+uZ, U, -bU, -(2+d)eta U, -eta U),
    G=(Z, U, -eta U, -eta U, -eta U),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (19)

The original four updates follow from b=H eta. Include the zero optimum.
The normals at targets1 and4 vanish. At target0 the normal is U+aZ;
the source1 residual is zero, and each source2,3,4 residual is -a nu.
The optimum-source residual there is -(1-a eta)<0. At target2 the
normal is -(b-eta)U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual
-(1+eta)(b-eta), and tail sources have residual zero. At target3 the
normal is -(1+d)eta U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual
-(1+eta)(1+d)eta, and tail sources have residual zero. Optimum-source
residuals at2,3 are respectively -eta(b-eta) and-(1+d)eta^2.
Every residual into the optimum is zero. This checks every pair.

In particular the zero-cost finite paths are

    4->1->0, 4->2, 4->3, and i->4 for every i<4.        (20)

All value differences are therefore forced, and the optimum pairs fix
their common shift. Both finite-flow and fixed-optimum prices vanish.
The function(16) with K=conv{0,Z,U,-eta U} realizes these samples.
Its minimizer set is{(0,t):t<=0}; the initial second coordinate is
u sqrt(nu)>0, so0 is the closest minimizer. Its true squared initial
distance is ||U+uZ||^2, not an independent calibration variable.

The energy is exactly

    Erow=||U+uZ||^2-C^2 eta^2
        =a+3+1/a-[(1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a]eta-C^2 eta^2.     (21)

Value-flow pricing returns only Erow>=0. It cannot recover a strictly
positive lower bound on Erow on this SAME geometry: the auxiliary flow
minimum is attained at zero and the values have no remaining freedom.
The further full-matrix restrictions in2948d4cd3660413f use PSD coupling
with a different quadratic direction and the first row. This result
neither assumes that unverified coupling theorem nor refutes it; it
identifies exactly what the present price can and cannot add to it.

## 6. C82 triangle: exact nonzero price and a real interpolant

Here keep canonical h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) with a,b,c,d>0, and let

    C=5+a+b+c+d, D=2+d, eta=bc/D,
    ell=max{0,c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2},
    t=a v-1, Htri(v)=(v+1)(a v-1).

Use parameters v>=b and z>=0 with ell<=z^2<=Htri(v). For nondegenerate
triangles take z>0; the boundary z=0 is legal by the same normal checks.
These inequalities imply t>=0. Define

    X_0=((1+a)v-1,z), G_0=(v,0),
    X_1=G_1=(-1,z),
    X_2=(b,-bz),     G_2=(b,0),
    X_3=(-bc,-bz),   G_3=(-eta,0),
    X_4=(-eta,-bz),  G_4=(-eta,0),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (22)

Every GD equation has the original four step lengths. For completeness,
K=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3} contains0. At target0 the residual normal is(t,z).
Its comparisons with G_1,G_2,G_3 and0 are, respectively,

    z^2-(v+1)t, (b-v)t, -(eta+v)t, -v t,

all nonpositive. At target1 the normal vanishes. At target2 the normal
is(0,-bz), so all horizontal-gradient sources give zero and source1
gives -bz^2. At target3 the normal is(-(1+d)eta,-bz). Its source1
residual is b*[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0; its horizontal-gradient
sources and optimum source are nonpositive. At target4 the normal is
(0,-bz), again giving zero for horizontal-gradient sources and -bz^2
for source1. Every residual into the optimum is zero. Thus all finite
and optimum pairs hold, and(16) gives one actual base function.

The finite zero-cost arcs4->1,4->2,4->3 show d_1=d_2=d_3=d_4=0.
The least cost into0 is

    Dprice=min{(v-b)t, (v+1)t-z^2}.                      (23)

The other incoming costs, (v+eta)t and v t from the optimum, are no
smaller than(v-b)t. Every path into0 has a final incoming arc, while
each minimizing source1 or2 is reached at cost zero. Therefore

    d_0=d_0^*=Dprice, all other d_i=d_i^*=0,
    Pflow=2(1+a)Dprice.                                 (24)

The complete fixed-geometry value optimum is attained by

    F'_0=F_0-Dprice, F'_i=F_i for1<=i<=4, F'_star=0.      (25)

All incoming residuals at0 increase by Dprice and stay nonpositive
by(23); all outgoing residuals from0 decrease. All other pairs are
unchanged. This is a direct check of all thirty ordered pairs including
the optimum, independently confirming the path calculation.

For the actual function use(8) with these values and the optimum.
Its affine constants beta_i are

    beta_0=-v t+Dprice<=-b t,
    beta_1=beta_2=beta_4=beta_star=0,
    beta_3=-(1+d)eta^2<0.

When t>0, the active normals in(9) are0 and(0,-bz). Therefore its
minimizer set is the segment joining these two points, and0 is the
closest minimizer to X_0, whose second coordinate z is nonnegative.
If t=0, z=0 and the initial normal also vanishes, with the same
closest minimizer0. Thus the true squared initial distance is

    ((1+a)v-1)^2+z^2.

The base telescoping energy and the exact optimized energy are

    Ebase(v,z)=((1+a)v-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2 eta^2,
    Eprice(v,z)=Ebase(v,z)-2(1+a)Dprice.                 (26)

In particular a generic claim that saturated projection values are
always rigid is FALSE. Formula(23) gives a strictly positive price
whenever v>b, t>0, and z^2<(v+1)t. This is a statement about a fixed
actual geometry, not yet about improvement over an optimized family.

## 7. Full C82 value-and-parameter minimum for0<a<1,0<b<=1

Keep c,d>0 arbitrary and ell as above. Put

    R=[1-a+sqrt((1+a)^2+4a ell)]/(2a),
    v_cross=[1+ell/(b+1)]/a,
    v_stationary=a/(1-a),
    v_best=min{v_cross,max{R,v_stationary}},
    K=C^2 eta^2.                                       (27)

Then R>=1/a>b and R<=v_cross. The first assertion follows because
Htri has its nonnegative zero at1/a and is increasing thereafter. For
the second, Htri(R)=ell implies(b+1)(aR-1)<=ell.

The exact infimum of(26) over EVERY legal triangle parameter is

    Enew=(1-a^2)v_best^2-2(1+a)a v_best+1+2(1+a)
                +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.                 (28)

If ell>0 the minimizing triangle has z=sqrt(ell)>0 and v=v_best.
If ell=0 the same formula is attained by the displayed degenerate
legal data; alternatively it is the limit of nondegenerate triangles.

Here is the complete minimization. For fixed v>=R, the price switches
at z^2=(b+1)t. Below this value its coefficient of z^2 is1-b^2>=0;
above it the coefficient is1-b^2+2(1+a)>0. Therefore z^2=ell is always
a minimizer. For R<=v<=v_cross the second term in(23) is the minimum,
and(26) becomes

    J_left(v)=(1-a^2)v^2-2(1+a)a v+1+2(1+a)
                         +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.         (29)

For v>=v_cross it becomes

    J_right(v)=(1-a^2)v^2+2(1+a)ab v+1-2(1+a)b
                              +(1-b^2)ell-K.           (30)

The two expressions agree at v_cross. J_right has strictly positive
derivative on that half-line. J_left is a strictly convex quadratic
with stationary point a/(1-a). Clipping that point to[R,v_cross]
proves(27)--(28), including both endpoint cases.

The original unpriced C82 minimum in this domain is

    Eold=((1+a)R-1)^2+(1-b^2)ell-K,                    (31)

since its energy increases in v and is minimized at z^2=ell. At v=R,
Htri(R)=ell makes Dprice=0, and J_left(R)=Eold.

Consequently value pricing adds EXACTLY NO information to this fully
optimized C82 condition whenever

    R>=a/(1-a).                                        (32)

In particular(32) holds throughout a^2+a<=1, because R>=1/a.
The positive root of a^2+a=1 is(sqrt(5)-1)/2. This includes the
first-step projection range39/100<=a<=41/100 from d3abee1b17da52da,
with its b range79/100<=b<=81/100. The C83 price was already zero there
by Section4, independently of this comparison.

When ell>0 and R<a/(1-a), one has v_cross>R and v_best>R, so the
optimized energy is strictly smaller. The precise positive reduction is

    Eold-Enew=(1-a^2)*[(R-v_stationary)^2
                                 -(v_best-v_stationary)^2]>0. (33)

Equivalently this strict-improvement regime is

    a^2+a>1,
    0<ell<(a^2+a-1)/(1-a)^2.                            (34)

This proves a strict improvement of the C82 FAMILY VALUE in its stated
domain. It does NOT prove that a word satisfying all the other current
necessary conditions is rejected by(28). In particular(34) alone is
not a surviving full-cone region and must not be advertised as one.

## 8. Comparison, resources, and the precise remaining interface

* C83: Section4 completely resolves the proposed value-price mechanism
  for all legal amplitudes of all unit-curvature repaired cuts. The
  optimum/source flow has an explicit zero-cost realization; the full
  value system is rigid. This includes the actual unit-curvature j=2
  separator in d3abee1b17da52da. Curvatures strictly between0 and1 are
  NOT covered by this rigidity theorem.

* C82: Sections6--7 solve the fixed geometry, construct its optimized
  actual function, and eliminate both geometric parameters in the
  explicitly stated domain. A positive price at a nonoptimal triangle
  cannot be called a new word obstruction when(32) holds.

* 554ec17f7f590bf0 and d3abee1b17da52da: their endpoint core is not
  being declared empty. These findings contain further triangle,
  clipping and endpoint conditions; no claim of their complete
  redundancy is made. On the specified projected prism, the tested
  C82/C83 value re-selections add no new condition beyond those
  already present.

* 2948d4cd3660413f: Section5 gives an exact zero-price result for its
  actual lifted-cut geometry, with every pair reconstructed. A
  transport minimum cannot replace that draft's simultaneous full
  PSD/first-row analysis. Its quantitative gap remains a separate
  unverified joint result.

* 04219554e41d9750: its ORIGINAL twenty-sign, exact-flow, full R5 PSD
  interface remains the relevant sufficient seed condition. Every
  implication here uses precisely that identity. No sparse flow
  constructed for pricing is promoted to a feasible certificate.

Two concrete analytical directions remain for the next authorized
round: decide whether the explicit C82 improvement(28) excludes a
region surviving the OTHER full necessary conditions; or apply the
exact price to a genuinely non-unit-curvature geometry, where zero
paths need not pin the values. Either needs an actual static function
and a comparison on the same word. Neither has been decided here.
