# Chronological and optimum anchors control every metric of two complex modes

Status: complete candidate proof, UNVERIFIED. No routine fact submission is
allocated. The only correctness inputs used are facts 1252b8823bb5a290
(necessary interpolation inequalities and smoothness) and b98bb807d514e80d
(the exact benchmark recursions). Every new estimate is given below. Earlier
unverified spectral and covariance findings are not premises.

## 1. Actual schedule, full added space, and claim

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2). For an integer m>=128 put
c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, d_j=1+a c^j. Within this horizon these
parameters are fixed. Set H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), n=2^m-1,
q=n-1, and let h be H_m with ONLY its original last step d_0 removed.
Write S=sum h_t. For 0<=i<=q put P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z).
Fix zeta_1=(1+i)/2, zeta_2=(4+i)/2, and z_l=zeta_l/S.

For a pair of complex numbers define the real column

 J(v_1,v_2)=(Re(v_1-v_2), Im v_1, Im v_2)^T.

Let w be arbitrary in [0,1] and T be ANY real matrix with three columns,
with no upper bound on its entries or number of rows. Define Euclidean data

 x_i=(sqrt(w)Re P_i(z_1),sqrt(w)Im P_i(z_1),
      sqrt(1-w)Re P_i(z_2),sqrt(1-w)Im P_i(z_2),
      T J(P_i(z_1),P_i(z_2))),
 g_i=(sqrt(w)Re[z_1 P_i(z_1)],sqrt(w)Im[z_1 P_i(z_1)],
      sqrt(1-w)Re[z_2 P_i(z_2)],sqrt(1-w)Im[z_2 P_i(z_2)],
      T J(z_1 P_i(z_1),z_2 P_i(z_2))).                    (1)

Then ||x_0||=1 and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. Every real linear functional
on (v_1,v_2) that vanishes at (1,1) is a linear combination of the three
components of J: its two real coefficients must sum to zero, and its two
imaginary coefficients are unrestricted. Thus (1) includes EVERY PSD
addition annihilating the initial vector, added orthogonally to the two
normal blocks. Write M=T^T T; it is an arbitrary PSD 3 by 3 matrix.

Suppose arbitrary JOINT queried values are interpolated by a convex
differentiable 1-smooth function F, with F(0)=0=min F and grad F(x_i)=g_i.
No separate modal values or modal legality are assumed. Then, for every
sufficiently large m, uniformly over w,T,F and b in [0,2],

                 2F(x_q-b g_q) <= 2/(3S).               (2)

For B_n=1/U_n^ZJ=2S_bullet(n)+1 this implies

 limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(w,T,F,b) B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)
                       <= 8/(3e) < 64/65 < 1.           (3)

The actual final step b=d_0 lies in [0,2]. The statement concerns exactly
the displayed spectral family. Sections 8--9 prove a broader result: for
ANY real linear map A from C^2, viewed as R^4, to a finite-dimensional
Euclidean space, with ||A(1,1)||=1, set

 x_i=A(P_i(z_1),P_i(z_2)),
 g_i=A(z_1 P_i(z_1),z_2 P_i(z_2)).

For arbitrary jointly legal values and all b in [0,2], eventually,
uniformly over these maps and their conditioning,

 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=21/(20S),
 limsup sup B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=21/(8e)<63/65<1.          (19)

Thus the broader result includes every PSD metric on the complete
four-dimensional two-mode evaluation space, including initial-direction
cross terms. Neither result bounds unrestricted W_n(H_m) or arbitrary
spectra. The number of modes is fixed at the two specified parameters.

## 2. Two exact shared interpolation inequalities

Use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0 from the accepted
fact 1252b8823bb5a290. First sum Q_(0,star) and Q_(t+1,t) for every
0<=t<q. The values telescope, and GD gives

 f_q <= <g_0,x_0>-sum_(t<q) h_t<g_(t+1),g_t>
                -||g_0||^2/2-sum_(t<q)||g_(t+1)-g_t||^2/2.

Dropping only the explicitly nonnegative squared terms yields

 f_q <= <g_0,x_0>-sum_(t<q)h_t<g_(t+1),g_t>.             (4)

Second, for any actual queried r, sum Q_(q,r) and Q_(r,star), and drop
their two squared terms. This gives

 f_q <= <g_q,x_q>+<g_r-g_q,x_r>.                         (5)

Choose r=2^(m-1), the query immediately AFTER the largest bridge. Both
inequalities concern the same joint transcript and the same function.
Their convex combination uses the fixed coefficients

                theta=6/25 on (4), 1-theta=19/25 on (5). (6)

For scaled gradients U_i=J(zeta_1 P_i(z_1),zeta_2 P_i(z_2)), and
Y_i=J(P_i(z_1),P_i(z_2)), let sym(u,v)=(uv^T+vu^T)/2. Define

 E_m=sum_(t<q) (h_t/S) sym(U_(t+1),U_t),
 Kplus_m=sym(U_q,Y_q)+sym(U_r-U_q,Y_r),
 K_m=(19/25)Kplus_m-(6/25)E_m.                          (7)

For l=1,2 let e_t=h_t/S and p_i,l=P_i(z_l), and define

 C_l,m=Re zeta_l-|zeta_l|^2 sum_(t<q)
                   e_t |p_t,l|^2(1-e_t Re zeta_l),
 Dplus_l,m=Re[zeta_l p_q,l conj(p_q,l)
                     +zeta_l(p_r,l-p_q,l)conj(p_r,l)],
 D_l,m=(6/25)C_l,m+(19/25)Dplus_l,m.                    (8)

The initial added coordinates are zero. Substituting (1) into (4)--(6)
therefore gives the EXACT finite-word bound

             S f_q <= w D_1,m+(1-w)D_2,m+trace(M K_m).  (9)

The proof will show K_m is negative definite and D_l,m<1/3 for all
sufficiently large m. Crucially these properties will hold at each such
FINITE m before allowing M to vary or grow without bound.

## 3. Limit word and continuity of its chronological work

Directly summing the multiplicity of every step gives

 S=2^m-2-a+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2),      S/lambda^m -> e,
 d_(m-1-j)/S -> delta lambda^(-j),
 delta=1-2/lambda=3-2sqrt(2).                            (10)

Here a->1/lambda, (1+1/m)^m->e, and lambda(lambda-2)=1. The largest
normalized step has limit delta. The normalized limiting word consists
of a central step delta, two copies with central step delta/lambda,
four copies with central step delta/lambda^2, and so on, with their
recursive chronology retained. The total mass at level j is
delta(2/lambda)^j; the total mass is one.

Its truncation after N largest levels is the finite word obtained from
H <- (H,d,H), starting empty and using, in ascending order,

 d=delta/lambda^(N-1), ..., delta/lambda, delta.

The omitted mass is exactly epsilon_N=(2/lambda)^N. This definition
does not introduce any new physical terminal step.

For the four modal numbers

 zeta_1, conjugate(zeta_1), zeta_2, conjugate(zeta_2),

every normalized factor satisfies |1-d zeta|<=1 whenever 0<=d<=1/5:
expanding the square reduces this to d<=2Re(zeta)/|zeta|^2, whose
minimum here is 16/17. All limiting steps and, eventually, all actual
normalized steps satisfy the stronger bound d<1/5.

Deleting steps of total mass tau from such a chronological word changes
any modal prefix product by at most |zeta|tau. To see this, telescope
the deletion one factor at a time; all other factors have modulus<=1,
and the deleted factor differs from one by d zeta.

For any two of the four modal numbers A,B define the scalar work moment

 W_word(A,B)=sum_t d_t P_t(A)P_t(B)[1-d_t(A+B)/2].        (11)

The bracket is the average of 1-d_t A and 1-d_t B, so its modulus is
at most one. Deleted terms have total modulus<=tau. At retained terms,
the change in the two-product is at most (|A|+|B|)tau. Since the total
step mass is at most one,

 |W_word-W_deleted| <= (1+|A|+|B|)tau.                  (12)

After multiplying by AB this is <23tau, because |A|,|B|<=sqrt(17)/2
<21/10 and |AB|<=17/4. Expressing the three real coordinates J in these
four complex modes uses rows with absolute coefficient sums 2,1,1.
Consequently deleting mass tau changes any entry of the real work
matrix by less than 92tau. We use the simpler uniform bound 100tau.
The normal work terms have error less than 23tau.

These inequalities prove existence of the infinite-word moments. They
also prove convergence from the ACTUAL words in (10). Indeed, retain
only their N largest levels. The resulting finite word converges to
the displayed N-level truncation, including its chronological order.
For fixed N the deleted normalized mass converges to epsilon_N:
the normalized total mass is exactly one and the retained masses
converge individually. Apply (12), first let m tend to infinity, and
then let N tend to infinity. The removed original d_0 is among the
deleted levels when m>N, so this argument also handles it explicitly.
We obtain E_m->E and convergence of the two normal work coefficients.

The left-half product and bridge/endpoint products are

 L(zeta)=product_(j>=1)(1-delta lambda^(-j)zeta)^(2^(j-1)),
 p_l=L(zeta_l),   v_l=(1-delta zeta_l)p_l,
 t_l=p_l v_l.                                          (13)

For the actual words, the corresponding products are exactly
v_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m and
t_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m^2/(1-d_0 z_l).
The last denominator tends to one. Deletion of a small total mass and
the same product bound prove convergence to (13). Thus K_m and D_l,m
converge entrywise to the quantities K,D_l given by (7)--(8) and the
limiting work moments.

## 4. Finite rational enclosures of the fixed limiting coefficients

Everything in this section is bounded arithmetic for one fixed
coefficient matrix; no PEP or optimization evaluation is used.

For the product (13) define mu_s=delta^s/(lambda^s-2). Absolutely
convergent logarithmic expansion gives

 log L(zeta)=-sum_(s>=1)mu_s zeta^s/s.

Let A_10 be the sum through s=10. Since mu_2<1/125,
|zeta|^2<=17/4, and eta=|zeta|delta/lambda<3/20, the omitted tail is

 <=mu_2 |zeta|^2 eta^9/[11(1-eta)] < 1/10^9.           (14)

The bound on mu_s used here is mu_s<=mu_2(delta/lambda)^(s-2), obtained
directly by summing the positive powers of the left-half step sizes.
Compute exp(A_10) through degree18. The bounds Re A_10<0 and |A_10|<1
give exponential tail <3/19!. Perturbing its argument by (14) changes
the exponential by <(10^-9)/(1-10^-9). Hence error 2/10^9 in each real
component safely covers both tails.

For the moment (11), its exact concatenation recurrence is short.
If a word has products p_A,p_B and moment W, appending its central
step d and another copy gives

 p_A,new=p_A^2(1-dA),  p_B,new=p_B^2(1-dB),
 W_new=W[1+p_A p_B(1-dA)(1-dB)]
                     +d p_A p_B[1-d(A+B)/2].           (15)

The first copy contributes W, the central step gives the last term,
and the second copy is multiplied by p_A p_B(1-dA)(1-dB), proving
the identity. Use N=112 levels in (15). The rational bound

                 100(2/lambda)^112 < 1/10^7             (16)

and (12) supply the error for every resulting real work coefficient.

For a completely specified finite arithmetic procedure, use the interval

 r_- = 1.41421356237309504880168872420969807856967187537694,
 r_+ = r_-+10^-50

for sqrt(2); its squared endpoints straddle 2. Set lambda=1+[r_-,r_+]
and delta=1-2/lambda. Interval additions, products, and positive
reciprocals enclose their exact values. At each stored log-series,
exponential-series, or word-recursion update, round the lower endpoint
down and upper endpoint up to a
multiple of 10^-60. This only enlarges an enclosure and prevents
denominator growth. Evaluate A_10, then its degree18 exponential using
t_0=1, t_j=t_(j-1)A_10/j. Add the componentwise error in (14). For
the work moments, use (15) on all sixteen pairs of the four modes.

The coefficients expressing J in these four modes, in that order, are

 c_1=(1/2,1/2,-1/2,-1/2),
 c_2=(-i/2,i/2,0,0), c_3=(0,0,-i/2,i/2).

The real work matrix entries are
E_kl=Re sum_(r,s)c_kr c_ls zeta_r zeta_s W(zeta_r,zeta_s).
The two normal chain coefficients are
C_1=1/2-(1/2)W(zeta_1,conjugate(zeta_1)) and
C_2=2-(17/4)W(zeta_2,conjugate(zeta_2)).
Add error interval [-10^-7,10^-7] to these expressions. This includes
(16); all preceding rounding errors are already in the intervals.

The following outward rational enclosures result. Finite decimals are
exact rational numbers; the margins are deliberately larger than the
rounding errors.

| quantity | enclosing interval |
|---|---|
| Re p_1 | [.7952608488,.7952608529] |
| Im p_1 | [-.1687257189,-.1687257148] |
| Re p_2 | [.4200940450,.4200940491] |
| Im p_2 | [-.0920602833,-.0920602792] |
| E_11 | [.3738957,.3738960] |
| E_12 | [-.1629610,-.1629607] |
| E_13 | [-.0946327,-.0946324] |
| E_22 | [.0952537,.0952540] |
| E_23 | [.0317060,.0317063] |
| E_33 | [.0282053,.0282056] |
| C_1 | [.1841427,.1841430] |
| C_2 | [.9918514,.9918517] |

Substituting the directly computed intervals in (7)--(8), at theta=6/25,
gives

 19/100 < D_1 < 193/1000,       329/1000 < D_2 < 33/100,
 -132/1000 < K_11 < -131/1000,
 21/10000 < K_11 K_22-K_12^2 < 22/10000,
 -18/10^8 < det K < -15/10^8.                           (17)

These are finite rational inequalities obtained by the displayed
recurrences, without eigenvalue computation. The three successive
diagonal pivots in completing the square are
K_11, (K_11 K_22-K_12^2)/K_11, det K/(K_11 K_22-K_12^2).
They are all negative by (17). Thus K is negative definite, and both
normal coefficients are strictly below 1/3.

The companion chain_anchor_interval_check.py implements these exact
interval operations and asserts (17). Its first version was stopped
because an unrounded exponential expression caused denominator growth.
The corrected version rounds the intermediate log/exponential terms
and the fixed-size moment recurrence; it exits zero. An earlier
ordinary floating-point exploration was not used as proof: repeated
squaring of factors extremely close to one loses significant small-step
mass in floating arithmetic. The rational calculation and its explicit
tail estimates are the evidence for (17).

## 5. Finite-word uniformity, free terminal step, and exact benchmark

Entrywise convergence and the strict inequalities (17) imply that for
all sufficiently large m, the EXACT K_m is negative definite and
D_l,m<1/3. None of those coefficients depends on w or T. Since
trace(T^T T K_m) is a sum of nonpositive row quadratic forms, (9) gives
F(x_q)<=1/(3S), uniformly even for coupling matrices diverging with m.

Integration of the 1-Lipschitz gradient along the final segment gives

 F(x_q-b g_q)<=F(x_q)-b(1-b/2)||g_q||^2<=F(x_q),

for all b in [0,2]. The terminal gradient and value are free; this
proves (2) for the same interpolating F.

Here is the comparison with the exact project benchmark, proved rather
than imported from an unverified finding. Let rho=log_2 lambda,
C_k=S_circle(k)+1, and B_k=2S_bullet(k)+1. The accepted recursions give

 C_0=B_0=1,
 C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
 B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
 Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,
 G(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.

For V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, differentiation gives, with t=exp u,

 d^2/du^2 log V(exp u)
 =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2 > 0.

The bracket vanishes at zero and has positive derivative for t>0.
Convexity at u and -u implies V(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=2. If A,B>0 and
M=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho, this means (M-A)(M-B)>=2AB;
the positive root of this quadratic is Omega(A,B). Thus
Omega(A,B)<=M and induction gives C_k<=(k+1)^rho.
The inequality sqrt(B^2+8AB)<=B+4A yields G(A,B)<=B+4A.
Using u^rho+v^rho<=(u+v)^rho for nonnegative u,v proves by induction
B_n<=1+4n^rho. The latter inequality follows by differentiating
(1+t)^rho-t^rho, whose minimum for t>=0 is one.

At n=2^m-1, n^rho/lambda^m->1 and S/lambda^m->e by (10).
Multiplying (2) by B_n proves (3). Finally e>sum_(j=0)^4 1/j!=65/24,
so 8/(3e)<64/65.

## 6. Why the chronology is necessary in this certificate

Define chi(v_1,v_2)=Re(v_1-v_2)/4+Im v_1/5+Im v_2.
It vanishes at (1,1). Apply it to the left, right, and endpoint modal
pairs in (13), obtaining y_-,y_+,y_q and, after multiplication by
the modal zeta values, u_-,u_+,u_q. The coefficients of its squared
amplitude in the two individual bridge-anchor bounds are

 k_-=u_q y_q+(u_--u_q)y_-,
 k_+=u_q y_q+(u_+-u_q)y_+.

The same rational product enclosures give

        23/100000 < k_- < 24/100000,
       103/100000 < k_+ < 104/100000.                    (18)

Every convex combination of just these two anchor matrices therefore
has a strictly positive direction. This refutes a purely algebraic
extension of the two-anchor method to the full three-dimensional
space. It is NOT a legal high-payoff construction. Adding the actual
chronological chain supplies a different constraint and yields (17).

## 7. Scope of the first coefficient bound

The bound (2) covers arbitrary PSD zero-initial additions to the two
normal blocks. Negative definiteness on J alone does not establish (2)
for arbitrary metrics: the initial-direction cross terms must also be
controlled. The next section does this with an additional actual
interpolation bound and a larger, explicitly paid coefficient. It uses
no unverified result as a premise: the complete coefficient derivation
and all estimates are part of this same proof.

## 8. Full initial-direction correlations and the third inequality

Let A be any map in the broader claim (19), and put e=A(1,1), so ||e||=1.
Write a(v_1,v_2)=Re v_1. The scalar map <e,A(v_1,v_2)> has value one
at (1,1), and therefore equals a+c^T J for some real column c in R^3.
The orthogonal projection of A onto e-perpendicular annihilates (1,1),
so it has the form T J, where T has range perpendicular to e. Hence

 A(v)=e[a(v)+c^T J(v)]+T J(v).                          (20)

This is an exact decomposition of EVERY normalized map A, not a
restriction to a chosen initial direction. The matrix M=T^T T is PSD
and c is unrestricted. Both may depend arbitrarily on m.

Apply the combination (6) to (20). At finite m it gives

 S f_q <= k00_m+2 k0_m^T c+c^T K_m c+trace(M K_m).      (21)

Here K_m is still (7). To specify the new coefficients, set
a_i=Re P_i(z_1), u_i=Re[zeta_1 P_i(z_1)],
u_init=(-3/2,1/2,1/2)^T. Define the scalar and vector work moments

 E00_m=sum_t e_t u_(t+1)u_t,
 E0_m=sum_t e_t[u_(t+1)U_t+u_t U_(t+1)]/2.

The coefficient formulas, with r the post-bridge query, are

 k00_m=(19/25)[u_q a_q+(u_r-u_q)a_r]+(6/25)[1/2-E00_m],
 k0_m=(19/50)[u_q Y_q+U_q a_q+(u_r-u_q)Y_r+(U_r-U_q)a_r]
                                  +(6/25)[u_init/2-E0_m]. (22)

All these moments converge by the same deletion proof (12). They use
the same sixteen scalar moments (15). In that formula the coefficient
row for a is c_0=(1/2,1/2,0,0). Thus E00 and E0_j are the real parts
of the contractions with c_0,c_0 and c_0,c_j respectively, with the
same error bound 10^-7. Alternatively E00=1/2-C_1-E_22, since the
first normal block is the sum of its real and imaginary coordinates.

For precision, direct outward interval calculation in the procedure
of Section 4 gives

 k00 in [.26264513,.26264525],
 k0_1 in [-.10147100,-.10147092],
 k0_2 in [.03390797,.03390804],
 k0_3 in [.03049014,.03049021].                          (23)

The coefficient from (21) alone is too large after unrestricted square
completion. This is a limitation of that bound, not a legal adversary.
The needed additional inequality is simply the ACTUAL Q_(q,star)<=0:

                   S f_q <= <S g_q,x_q>.               (24)

Its omitted squared gradient term is nonnegative. Add tau=2/3 times
(24) to (21), and divide by 1+tau=5/3. The actual weights on the
chronological chain, post-bridge anchor and optimum anchor are
18/125,57/125,2/5, respectively. They are nonnegative and sum to one.

Before dividing, define

 Kbar_m=K_m+tau sym(U_q,Y_q),
 kbar_m=k0_m+(tau/2)(u_q Y_q+U_q a_q),
 kbar00_m=k00_m+tau u_q a_q.                            (25)

Then

 (1+tau) S f_q <= kbar00_m+2 kbar_m^T c
                            +c^T Kbar_m c+trace(M Kbar_m). (26)

The limiting coefficients from the same rational procedure satisfy

 -.082622 < Kbar_11 < -.082621,
 .0007105 < Kbar_11 Kbar_22-Kbar_12^2 < .0007106,
 -81/10^9 < det Kbar < -79/10^9.                        (27)

In particular Kbar is negative definite. For an entirely specified
arithmetic check of the remaining scalar, let cof_ji(Kbar) denote its
cofactor obtained by deleting row j and column i, with sign (-1)^(i+j).
Evaluate

 R_full = (3/5)[kbar00
           -sum_(i,j)kbar_i cof_ji(Kbar) kbar_j/det Kbar].

The direct enclosing interval from (15), (22), (25) is

            .5173 < R_full < .5205 < 21/40.             (28)

These cofactor formulas use only products, sums, and division by the
strictly negative interval in (27). No eigenvalue or optimization
routine is needed. The companion initial_cross_coefficient_check.py
implements this same fixed coefficient arithmetic. Its finer interval
is [.5173336917833667,.5204116728479283]. These displayed endpoints
report the outward rational result; the exact coarse inequalities
(27)--(28) are the claimed bounds.

For finite m sufficiently large, Kbar_m is negative definite and
R_full,m<21/40 by convergence and the strict margins. Completing the
square gives

 kbar00_m+2 kbar_m^T c+c^T Kbar_m c
 =kbar00_m-kbar_m^T Kbar_m^(-1)kbar_m
  +(c+Kbar_m^(-1)kbar_m)^T Kbar_m(c+Kbar_m^(-1)kbar_m)
 <=(5/3) R_full,m.

Also trace(M Kbar_m)<=0. Equation (26) gives F(x_q)<=21/(40S), uniformly
over ALL normalized maps A, including arbitrarily growing initial
correlations and transverse metrics. The same final descent inequality
as in Section 5 proves the first assertion of (19).

One scope audit can be stated explicitly. The scalar row a+5chi, with
chi from Section 6, has unit initial value. Its coefficient in the
original combination (21) lies in (.374,.375), exceeding 1/3. Thus
one cannot extend the OLD1/3 coefficient just by ignoring the initial
cross terms. This row is not a legal counterexample: if its scaled
terminal gradient is U and scaled derivative is V, the exact limiting
coefficient UV is in (-.084,-.082). At the actual last retained
adjacent pair, cocoercivity implies UV>=V^2/S, so this scalar row
fails legality for sufficiently large m. The third inequality and
square completion prove (19) without assuming that this row, or any
other unsupported coefficient direction, is realizable.

## 9. A self-contained benchmark envelope sufficient for the full metric

This proof needs only the following weaker scalar comparison, which
is established here directly:

             G(x,(5/2)y) <= (5/2)Omega(x,y), x,y>=0.    (29)

Let k=5/2 and p=Omega(x,y). The plane
R=(16/15)x+(5/3)y bounds p from above. Indeed R>=max(x,y), and

 (R-x)(R-y)-2xy
  =(16x^2-40xy+250y^2)/225
  =[16(x-5y/4)^2+225y^2]/225 >=0.

Since p is the larger root of (p-x)(p-y)=2xy, this proves p<=R.
The number G(x,ky) is the larger root of
t^2-(4x+ky)t+4x^2=0. Substituting t=kp and using
p^2=(x+y)p+xy gives

 (kp)^2-(4x+ky)kp+4x^2
        =x[4x+(25/4)y-(15/4)p] >=0.

Moreover p>=x+y, so kp>=2x+(k/2)y, the vertex of the quadratic.
It follows that kp is at least its larger root, proving (29).
The reasoning includes x=0 or y=0; these cases also follow directly.

The function G is nondecreasing in its second nonnegative argument,
as follows immediately from its formula. Starting from B_0=C_0=1,
induction in the accepted recursions and (29) gives

 B_n=max_j G(C_j,B_(n-j-1))
     <=max_j G(C_j,(5/2)C_(n-j-1))
     <=(5/2)max_j Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1))=(5/2)C_n.

Section 5 already proved C_n<=(n+1)^rho directly from the recursions.
Therefore B_(2^m-1)<=(5/2)lambda^m. Together with S/lambda^m->e and
the first bound in (19), this proves

 limsup sup B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=21/(8e)<63/65,

where the strict inequality uses e>65/24. No numerical estimate for
the benchmark or presumed global profile limit enters this comparison.
The stronger coefficient 1+sqrt2 requested elsewhere is not assumed.

## 10. Remaining target

Every normalized common PSD metric for THESE two complex modes is
covered, with full joint prefix legality, exact initial distance, and
free final value/gradient. The result is still an upper bound for a
restricted adversary family. It supplies neither an upper certificate
on the entire formal PEP space nor a matching legal lower witness for
the original problem. Additional spectral support, growing modal
dimension, and nonlinear trajectory mechanisms remain outside the
statement. The all-schedule exact-profile target is unresolved.

# All circular parameters for the unchanged actual two-checkpoint law

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No formal gate was
allocated or called, and this document has no fact_id. All new assertions
below, including the lemmas, form one candidate proof. The only accepted
input is fact04219554e41d9750, used for the original benchmark ray and its
enclosure. The unrestricted all-word/all-horizon target remains OPEN.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; one analytical round after
completed57, completed ceiling58 / attempted ceiling59. Current assignment
TASK.md; guidance235f55c47f06b19a; selected plan6a516e8ff97d78ce.
Latest guidance reread83b74bbdf2d83c86 leaves this assignment unchanged.
No automatic renewal. The physical function and word are unchanged.

The complete drafts7d145b115817e82d and4c4a21a484564bdc were read in full.
Neither is an accepted predecessor. Every construction, radius, trajectory,
finite-tail and local-data ingredient used here is reconstructed below.
The improvement is one normalization-aware last-crossing comparison: its
cap deficit is paid by the ACTUAL flat width. No comparison repair was
needed. No additional checkpoint, bridge, component or tail reordering is
introduced.

## 1. Statement, including all parameter quantifiers

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), d=lambda-2=1/lambda. For an integer m>=4 and
tau in[1/5,1/4], define the SAME enclosing parameters in every child:

    eta_m=1-tau/m, c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=eta_m/c,
    b_j=1+a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j,
    n_m=2^m-1, J=m-2, L=m-1, S=S_J.                 (1.1)

Only the first occurrences of levels J and J+1 are selected. Their
zero-based indices are2^J-1 and2^(J+1)-1. Thus the entire physical word is

    H_m=(H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_L).                  (1.2)

For EVERY beta=beta_arc in(0,1/4], use the three-dimensional static circular
function F defined in Sections2--3, initialized at e_1=(1,0,0). All updates
are x^+=x-h*grad F for the original physical step h in(1.2). Set

    E_m(tau,beta)=2F(x_(n_m))/R_true^2,
    R_true=dist(e_1,argmin F)>0,
    C=beta+sqrt(1+2beta),
    T_1=b_J/2-1, T_2=b_(J+1)/2-1, A_loc=1+S/2.     (1.3)

Write x_fin=x_(n_m); a component evaluated at x_fin means its value
on the corresponding two coordinates of this three-coordinate point.

The local numbers u,y,M=u+y/beta and scales D_2=T_1/M,
D_3=T_1*T_2/M^2 are defined by the ACTUAL discrete backward construction
in Section2. Let B_n be the original all-split denominator defined in
Section8. Define the following word-only quantities:

    K_J=2+2^(J+1),
    Q_m(tau)=B_(n_m)*T_1^2*T_2^2
                     /[2(1+S_L)*A_loc^4],
    epsilon_m(tau)=16*B_(n_m)*K_J/S^2.              (1.4)

There is one finite integer m_0 such that for EVERY integer m>=m_0,
EVERY tau in[1/5,1/4], and EVERY beta in(0,1/4], the following hold.

(a) With Phi_1 and Phi_2 denoting BOTH old components of this same F,

    0 <= [Phi_1(x_fin)+Phi_2(x_fin)]/R_true^2
                                             <=16*K_J/S^2;             (1.5)
    B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,beta)
                         <= Q_m(tau)/C^4+epsilon_m(tau).               (1.6)

The terminal-coordinate Huber is included in(1.6). No old value is
discarded before(1.5) is proved. The bound is valid even if R_true tends
to zero along a choice beta=beta_m.

(b) Both old terminal components are EXACTLY zero whenever

    beta^2*T_1 >= K_J.                              (1.7)

They are also exactly zero whenever the actual incoming margin in(4.7)
is nonpositive. Criterion(1.7) holds uniformly in tau for beta=1/m,
and also for beta=8/m, for all sufficiently large m. These are statements
about their actual full H_L tails, not their post-root snapshots.
No exact-zero claim is required for every smaller beta.

(c) Let Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m) B_(2^m-1)>0, supplied by accepted
fact04219554e41d9750. Uniformly for tau in[1/5,1/4],

    Q_m(tau) -> Z_1*exp(-tau)/(2lambda),
    epsilon_m(tau) ->0.                             (1.8)

More precisely the full family has the sharp supremal limit

    sup_(tau in[1/5,1/4])
      | sup_(0<beta<=1/4) B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,beta)
                              -Z_1*exp(-tau)/(2lambda) | ->0.          (1.9)

The specific beta_m=8/m gives the lower comparison needed for(1.9),
with its actual backward data proved in Section7. There is no exchange
of an uncontrolled beta supremum and a horizon limit. In particular,

    lim_m sup_(0<beta<=1/4) B_(n_m)*E_m(1/4,beta)
                            =Z_1*exp(-1/4)/(2lambda)<7/10,             (1.10)
    lim_m sup_(tau in[1/5,1/4],0<beta<=1/4) B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,beta)
                            =Z_1*exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)<3/4.             (1.11)

Consequently the respective finite-horizon suprema are below7/10 and3/4
for every sufficiently large m. This closes the remaining0<beta<8/m,
including every parameter choice depending on m, for this PRECISE
two-checkpoint circular family. It is an upper bound on these witnesses,
not an upper bound on W_n. Other checkpoint selections/counts, other
functions and the original minimax problem are separate and remain open.

## 2. Circular body, smooth envelope and actual backward data

Fix0<beta<=1/4 and write k=sqrt(1+2beta), C=beta+k. Define

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,
    v(q)=(p(q),-q),
    K_ctr=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).             (2.1)

This is the same centered circular body as in the two source drafts.
The circle has center(beta,beta) and radius1+beta. Its closed disk
contains0, since2beta^2<=(1+beta)^2. The radial vector at each displayed
arc point therefore supports K_ctr. The endpoints are(C,0) and(beta,-1).
All points(p,-q) of K_ctr satisfy

    0<=p<=C, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta*q, (p,0) in K_ctr.     (2.2)

The horizontal assertion uses the segment from0 to(C,0); the other
assertions hold on the generating arc and are preserved by convex hulls.
The cone of K_ctr is generated by its endpoints, because

    (p,-q)=((p-beta*q)/C)(C,0)+q(beta,-1).            (2.3)

For a compact convex K containing0, put

    E_K(r)=max_(g in K){<g,r>-||g||^2/2}.            (2.4)

Completing the square gives the unique maximizer Pi_K(r), the Euclidean
projection. Its characterization is

    <r-Pi_K(r),g-Pi_K(r)> <=0 for all g in K.        (2.5)

Adding this inequality at two points proves
||Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s)||^2<=<Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s),r-s>, so the projection is
1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing expressions in(2.4) gives
the linear increment <Pi_K(r),s-r> with remainder at most||s-r||^2.
Thus E_K is differentiable with gradient Pi_K, convex, nonnegative and
1-smooth. Its zero set is the polar cone: if a supporting inner product
is positive, a sufficiently small positive multiple of that vector
gives positive value in(2.4); if all are nonpositive,0 maximizes.
It follows from(2.3), for every sigma>0, that

    E_(sigma K_ctr)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U<=V.    (2.6)

Define the one-sided Huber, for delta>0, by

    Hub_delta(z)=0                    for z<=0,
                 z^2/2                for0<=z<=delta,
                 delta*z-delta^2/2    for z>=delta.  (2.7)

Its derivative is min(delta,max(z,0)); hence it is convex and1-smooth.

Write the actual steps of H_J in order as h_1,...,h_N, N=2^J-1, and
let alpha_i=h_i/2. These are local half-times, not altered physical
steps. Set r_N=(beta,-1), q_N=1, and construct r_i=(u_i,-y_i) backward.
At each i=N,...,1 choose the unique q_(i-1) in[0,1) satisfying

    (q_(i-1)+beta)/(p(q_(i-1))-beta)
        =max{beta/k,
              [y_i+(1-alpha_i)beta]/[u_i-(1-alpha_i)beta]},             (2.8)
    r_(i-1)=r_i+alpha_i*v(q_(i-1)).                  (2.9)

All steps are positive. Inductively u_i>=beta, y_i>=1, so the denominator
on the right is at least alpha_i*beta>0. The left side is continuous
and strictly increasing from beta/k to infinity. Thus the construction
is finite and well defined. It is a mathematical definition; no recurrence
was executed in this round.

Here are its projection and ordering checks. At an interior current
point, the supporting radial relation is
(y_i+beta)/(u_i-beta)=(q_i+beta)/(p(q_i)-beta).
Subtracting alpha_i*beta from the first numerator and adding it to the
denominator can only decrease this positive ratio. This proves
q_(i-1)<=q_i. At q_i=1 the latter bound follows from q_(i-1)<1. At the
capped point q_i=0, the supporting condition is

    y_i<=beta*(u_i-C)/k.                            (2.10)

It forces the second entry in(2.8) to be at most beta/k, so the new
point is also capped. For an interior new point, equations(2.8)--(2.9)
place r_(i-1) on the outward centered ray through v(q_(i-1)). Its radial
factor is(y_(i-1)+beta)/(q_(i-1)+beta)>=1, because y_(i-1)>=1 and
q_(i-1)<=1. The circular supporting normal proves(2.5). For a capped
new point, (2.8) instead gives(2.10) with index i-1. The displacement
from(C,0) is a nonnegative combination of(k,-beta) and(0,1), both
supporting normals there. This again proves(2.5). The terminal r_N
belongs to K_ctr. We have therefore proved

    Pi_(K_ctr)(r_i)=v(q_i), q_(i-1)<=q_i,
    u_i>=beta, y_i>=1,
    u:=u_0=beta+sum_i alpha_i*p(q_(i-1)),
    y:=y_0=1+sum_i alpha_i*q_(i-1),
    u>=beta*y, M=u+y/beta.                          (2.11)

For an incoming threshold ell>=0 and amplitude D>0 define

    sigma=D/M, ell_out=sigma*y, A=ell+ell_out/beta,
    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K_ctr)(X-A,Y-ell_out).         (2.12)

It is zero whenever X<=ell and Y>=0, by(2.6). Its output derivative
is zero whenever Y>=ell_out, for EVERY X: replacing a nonzero negative
gradient coordinate -q by0 increases the maximized expression by
q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2. The entire horizontal segment needed for that
replacement belongs to the body by(2.2).

The actual local states are

    (X_i,Y_i)=(A+sigma*u_i,ell_out-sigma*y_i).       (2.13)

They start at(ell+D,0). Equations(2.9),(2.11) and projection scaling
show that every physical step h_i updates them by-(h_i/2)*grad Phi.
They have X_i>ell and0<=Y_i<=ell_out-sigma<ell_out. Their final
gradient is sigma(beta,-1). A selected physical b>2 with T=b/2-1 gives

    (X^+,Y^+)=(A-beta*sigma*T,ell_out+sigma*T).       (2.14)

This lies in the zero set(2.6). Both the gradient change along the arc
and this checkpoint identity concern the unchanged physical word.

## 3. The whole static function, prefix and exact closest radius

The two ordinary blocks in(1.2) are identical, so use the SAME u,y,M
from Section2 for both. Set

    sigma_1=1/M, D_2=T_1/M, sigma_2=D_2/M,
    D_3=T_1*T_2/M^2,
    ell_2=y/M, ell_3=D_2*y/M,
    A_1=ell_2/beta, A_2=ell_2+ell_3/beta,
    delta=D_3/(1+S_L).                             (3.1)

For all sufficiently large m the two T_i are positive. Before starting
GD, define on R^3

    Phi_1(X,Y)=E_(sigma_1 K_ctr)(X-A_1,Y-ell_2),
    Phi_2(Y,Z)=E_(sigma_2 K_ctr)(Y-A_2,Z-ell_3),
    F(X,Y,Z)=[Phi_1(X,Y)+Phi_2(Y,Z)
                                  +Hub_delta(Z-ell_3)]/2.             (3.2)

The coordinate-stacking map(X,Y,Z)->((X,Y),(Y,Z),Z) has squared
norm at most2, since its squared output norm is X^2+2Y^2+2Z^2.
The separable sum of the three1-smooth functions has1-Lipschitz
gradient in the product space. Composition and multiplication by1/2
give a convex1-smooth F. All summands are nonnegative and F(0)=0.

If the two projections are(P_1,-Q_1),(P_2,-Q_2), and
P_3=min(delta,max(Z-ell_3,0)), its EXACT full update is

    X^+=X-h*P_1/2,
    Y^+=Y-h*(P_2-Q_1)/2,
    Z^+=Z-h*(P_3-Q_2)/2.                           (3.3)

During the first H_J and b_J, the first pair follows(2.13)--(2.14);
its output is below ell_2 at every query, and the remaining components
are inactive. The first checkpoint gives

    X=A_1-beta*D_2, Y=ell_2+D_2, Z=0.               (3.4)

During the next H_J and its checkpoint b_(J+1), the second pair follows
the same local law with ell=ell_2,D=D_2. Its input is at least ell_2
at every query. Meanwhile X stays below A_1. Thus the first component
has value and gradient0 at those queries by(2.6), and the last Huber
is inactive below ell_3. The actual state immediately after the root is

    v_in=A_1-beta*D_2,
    w_in=A_2-beta*D_3,
    Z_in=ell_3+D_3.                                (3.5)

This is a trajectory identity in the fixed function, not a free
assignment of incoming tail data.

The ENTIRE minimum set is, by nonnegativity and(2.6),

    X<=A_1, Y>=beta*X,
    Y<=A_2, Z>=beta*(Y-ell_2), Z<=ell_3.            (3.6)

From(2.11),

    1-A_1=u/M, ell_2=y/M=beta*A_1,
    0<A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).                           (3.7)

The point x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0) satisfies all of(3.6). Any feasible
point with X<0 has squared distance from e_1 at least1. For a feasible
point with0<=X<=A_1, that distance is at least
(1-X)^2+beta^2*X^2. This quadratic is decreasing until
X=1/(1+beta^2), so its minimum on[0,A_1] is at A_1. The displayed
x_star attains this lower bound, which is at most1. Hence

    R_true^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u^2+y^2)/M^2>0.     (3.8)

This is the closest radius for the WHOLE function. It may shrink with
beta and has not been replaced by the distance to the specified point0.

All smooth-convex interpolation rows follow from this actual function.
For arbitrary test points x_test,y_test, subtract <grad F(y_test),z>
from F(z); the resulting function is minimized at y_test. Its1-smooth
descent bound at x_test with displacement
-(grad F(x_test)-grad F(y_test)) yields
F(x_test)-F(y_test)-<grad F(y_test),x_test-y_test>
>=||grad F(x_test)-grad F(y_test)||^2/2. Thus all ordered rows,
including the terminal sample and x_star, hold. The actual Euclidean
Gram matrices are positive semidefinite. For any smoothness L_smooth>0,
use f=L_smooth*F and x^+=x-(h/L_smooth)*grad f; the same trajectory and
normalized payoff2(f(x_fin)-min f)/(L_smooth*R_true^2)=E_m result.

## 4. Every physical tail step and the incoming-activity split

Throughout H_L the third-coordinate excess after traversed mass s' is

    Z-ell_3=D_3-s'*delta/2>=delta, 0<=s'<=S_L.       (4.1)

Indeed this identity, by induction, ensures Q_2=0 by output isolation,
so P_3=delta and the next physical update continues the identity.
For Z>=ell_3, maximizing over the second body's negative coordinate
sets it to0, and hence

    Phi_2(w,Z)=Hub_(C*sigma_2)(w-A_2).              (4.2)

The first coordinate never increases, so v<=A_1 throughout the tail.
For fixed negative gradient coordinate -q, its maximizer then chooses
the smallest permissible horizontal coordinate beta*q. Indeed reducing
p to beta*q increases p(v-A_1)-p^2/2. This segment is in K_ctr. Maximizing
along it and using beta*A_1=ell_2 gives exactly

    Phi_1(v,w)=Hub_((1+beta^2)*sigma_1)(beta*v-w)
                                                   /(1+beta^2).      (4.3)

Abbreviate sigma=1/M, kappa=1+beta^2, r=C*D_2. Here r is the cap
ratio, not a backward point; kappa is the tail coefficient, not a
condition number. For EVERY physical tail step h,

    q=min(sigma,max(beta*v-w,0)/kappa),
    s=min(r*sigma,max(w-A_2,0)),
    v^+=v-h*beta*q/2,
    w^+=w+h*(q-s)/2.                               (4.4)

The letter s in(4.4) denotes a capped derivative only, not a word mass.
The flat interval is beta*v<=w<=A_2, of width

    g=A_2-beta*v,
    g_in=D_2*(A_1+beta^2)>0,
    g^+=g+h*beta^2*q/2>=g.                         (4.5)

The two derivatives cannot both be positive. Once the pair is flat,
both vanish forever, with(4.1) maintaining decoupling from coordinate3.
The ENTIRE actual final payoff is

    E_m=[Phi_1(v_fin,w_fin)+Phi_2(w_fin,Z_fin)
                          +D_3^2/(2(1+S_L))]/R_true^2.               (4.6)

The final term follows by substituting(4.1) at s'=S_L into(2.7).
It is never used alone as an upper bound without bounding the old terms.

The incoming point is strictly below A_2. Its exact left margin is

    z_in=beta*v_in-w_in
         =D_2*(beta*T_2/M-A_1-beta^2).              (4.7)

If z_in<=0 it is already flat, and both old terms vanish for the
whole tail. If z_in>0, the exact inequality gives

    A_1<beta*T_2/M,
    y<beta^2*T_2.                                  (4.8)

These are constraints on ACTUAL discrete incoming data. An upper bound
such as exp(2/beta) is not being treated as attained displacement.

## 5. A radius-aware geometric estimate for every beta

The function p(q) in(2.1) is concave: differentiating gives
p'(q)=-(q+beta)/sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2), whose derivative is
negative on[0,1). Its endpoint chord therefore yields

    p(q)+k*q>=C, k=sqrt(1+2beta).                  (5.1)

Using the actual backward sums in(2.11) and beta+k=C proves

    u+k*y>=C*(1+S/2)=C*A_loc,
    M=u+y/beta>=C*A_loc,
    u<=beta+C*S/2.                                 (5.2)

The second inequality follows from1/beta>=k. For a stronger bound let
N_arc=sqrt(u^2+y^2). Since beta<=1/4, k^2<=3/2 and k<5/4, so

    2k+k^2<4<=1/beta.                             (5.3)

All coefficients below are nonnegative, and N_arc>=u,N_arc>=y. Split
the coefficient y/beta into2k*y, k^2*y and its nonnegative remainder:

    M*N_arc >=(u+2k*y)*u+(k^2*y)*y
               =(u+k*y)^2 >=C^2*A_loc^2.          (5.4)

This pays the exact radius even when it shrinks, since(3.8) gives

    D_3/R_true=T_1*T_2/(M*N_arc)
                         <=T_1*T_2/(C^2*A_loc^2). (5.5)

In particular the normalized FINAL Huber contribution obeys

    B_(n_m)*D_3^2/[2(1+S_L)*R_true^2]
                                      <=Q_m(tau)/C^4.                (5.6)

No estimate R_true approximately1 was used here.

## 6. The finite last-crossing residual, with the gap retained

First establish the word-only conditions used in this section. Write

    d_m=c-2, v_mass=a/d_m.

Here c>lambda>2, eta_m<1 and c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1, so
0<v_mass<1. Direct substitution into S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j proves

    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,
    b_j=d_m*S_j+c-1-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^j.             (6.1)

The same enclosing m,tau appears everywhere. Uniformly in
tau in[1/5,1/4], as m tends to infinity,

    c->lambda, v_mass->1,
    S_J/lambda^J ->exp(tau), S_L/lambda^L ->exp(tau),
    T_1/A_loc ->d, T_2/A_loc ->1.                  (6.2)

For example0<=tau-m*log(1+tau/m)<=tau^2/(2m), by integrating
x/(1+x)<=x. This proves the uniform exponential limits; the2^j terms
in(6.1) are smaller by a fixed geometric factor. Both T limits follow
by dividing their exact b formulas by S+2.

For each j let

    P_j=sum_(h in H_j, h>=2) h.

This is only a lower mass bound in the tail proof, not a replacement
of the ordered tail. Every omitted step is below2, and H_j has2^j-1
entries, so

    0<=S_j-P_j<=2(2^j-1)<=2^(j+1).                 (6.3)

Equations(6.1)--(6.3) imply that one word-only m_0 suffices for

    2<c<3, 0<d_m<1, T_1>0, 0<T_2<=S, P_L>=S.      (6.4)

Indeed T_2/S tends1/2 and P_L/S tends lambda>2 uniformly. Increase
m_0 if necessary; it has no dependence on beta.

Two consequences of(5.2) are essential. First the exact cap ratio is

    0<r=C*T_1/M <=(b_J-2)/(S+2)<d_m<1.             (6.5)

For the strict inequality, (6.1) gives
d_m*(S+2)-(b_J-2)=d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^J+d_m+1>0.
Second, if the incoming point is active, equations(4.8),(5.2),(6.4)
give

    A_1<beta*T_2/M<=2beta*S/(S+2)<=2beta<=1/2,
    R_true^2=(1-A_1)^2+beta^2*A_1^2>1/4.           (6.6)

This lower radius bound is asserted ONLY in the active case. The
inactive case already has exactly zero old values and uses(5.5).
Also D_2=r/C<1.

Now suppose the terminal pair is active. It must have been active at
every earlier sample, since the closed flat interval is absorbing. If
z=beta*v-w>0 at consecutive samples on the left, (4.4) gives

    z^+=z-h*kappa*min(sigma,z/kappa)/2.             (6.7)

For h>=2, staying strictly on the left requires the saturated cap
q=sigma: otherwise z^+=(1-h/2)z<=0. This also accounts for the cap
boundary, since landing at0 would not stay strictly left. Each such
high step loses h*kappa*sigma/2. Low steps give nonnegative loss.
On consecutive right samples, t=w-A_2>0 satisfies

    t^+=t-h*min(r*sigma,t)/2.                       (6.8)

Thus staying right across h>=2 requires the saturated cap r*sigma
and loses h*r*sigma/2. No quadratic-region or overshoot case has
been suppressed.

If there is no side change, all samples remain left because the
incoming point is below A_2. But(4.7) and(6.4)--(6.5) imply

    M*z_in < beta*D_2*T_2 <=S/4 < kappa*P_L/2.      (6.9)

Summing the high-step losses in(6.7) would make z_fin negative.
Therefore terminal activity entails at least one crossing. Take its
LAST side change. If this is at a step of level j,0<=j<=J, it is
immediately followed by a complete H_j inside the actual remaining
tail. This follows inductively from H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j): the right
subtree is read immediately after its node. That full following block
has no further crossing, by lastness. Any steps after that block also
stay on the same side and only decrease the appropriate margin.

For a last left-to-right crossing, before it let z>0 and
g=A_2-beta*v>=g_in. Its new right margin is

    t^+=b_j*q/2-z-g < b_j*sigma/2-g_in.

Using the high-step losses of its following H_j in(6.8) gives the
necessary terminal bound

    0<t_fin< [b_j-r*P_j]/(2M)-g_in.                (6.10)

For a last right-to-left crossing, the first coordinate is unchanged
on that step. With t>0 at its query, the new left margin is
z^+=b_j*s/2-t-g<b_j*r*sigma/2-g_in. Equation(6.7) then gives

    0<z_fin< [r*b_j-kappa*P_j]/(2M)-g_in.          (6.11)

These inequalities retain the moving interval, both caps, all
overshoots and every original large bridge. They also show why an
equal-mass tail replacement would not justify the result.

Bound(6.10) simultaneously for every possible level. By(6.1),(6.3)
and r<d_m, and because S_j<=S for j<=J,

    b_j-r*P_j
      <=(d_m-r)*S+c-1+r*2^(J+1)
      = b_J-r*S+d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^J+r*2^(J+1).      (6.12)

The critical exact cancellation uses the upper bound on u from(5.2):

    1-C*S/(2M)<=A_1+beta/M,
    b_J-r*S=2+2*T_1*(1-C*S/(2M)).                 (6.13)

Consequently, subtracting the ACTUAL g_in=D_2*(A_1+beta^2),

    [b_j-r*P_j]/(2M)-g_in
      <= [1+beta*D_2+
            (d_m*(1-v_mass)/2+r)*2^J]/M-beta^2*D_2
      <= [K_J-beta^2*T_1]/M.                      (6.14)

For the last inequality use beta*D_2<=1/4,
d_m*(1-v_mass)/2+r<3/2, and
5/4+(3/2)*2^J <=2+2^(J+1)=K_J. The term D_2*A_1 cancels EXACTLY.
Thus the nonuniform local cap defect is paid by an actual part of the
flat interval, with no assertion M/S approximately C/2.

For(6.11), the coefficient r*d_m-kappa is negative by(6.5) and
kappa>=1. Using(6.1),(6.3) and dropping only nonpositive terms gives

    r*b_j-kappa*P_j
       <= r*(c-1)+kappa*2^(J+1)
       <= 2+2^(J+2),                              (6.15)

since c-1<2 and kappa<=17/16<2. Thus

    [r*b_j-kappa*P_j]/(2M)-g_in
       <=[1+2^(J+1)-beta^2*T_1]/M
       <=[K_J-beta^2*T_1]/M.                      (6.16)

Equations(6.9)--(6.16) prove that any active terminal margin, on
either side, is less than K_J/M. If beta^2*T_1>=K_J the strict
positive bounds(6.10)--(6.11) are impossible; this proves the exact
absorption criterion(1.7), including equality in that criterion.

For the quantitative bound, Hub_(kappa*sigma)(z)/kappa<=sigma*z
when z>=0, and Hub_(r*sigma)(t)<=r*sigma*t when t>=0.
At most one old term is active, by the positive gap. Therefore

    Phi_1(x_fin)+Phi_2(x_fin)<=K_J/M^2.            (6.17)

This also holds if the terminal point is flat. In the incoming-active
case, divide by the proved radius bound(6.6) and use M>=S/2:

    [Phi_1(x_fin)+Phi_2(x_fin)]/R_true^2
                                             <=16*K_J/S^2.           (6.18)

In the incoming-flat case the left side is exactly0. This completes
the all-beta proof of(1.5). Combining(6.18) with(5.6) and the exact
whole payoff(4.6) proves(1.6).

For beta=1/m, (6.2) shows T_1/m^2 grows like a positive constant
times lambda^m/m^2, uniformly in tau; K_J is at most a fixed
constant times2^m. Since m^2*(2/lambda)^m tends to0, (1.7) holds
eventually. The same argument applies to beta=8/m. More generally,
the explicit criterion(1.7) is valid as written, without restricting
beta to a fixed or polynomial scale.

## 7. Actual backward estimates only for the attaining comparison

To obtain the sharp lower comparison for the family supremum, it
suffices to analyze one permitted sequence beta_m=8/m. This section
derives the needed finite backward bound; it is not used in the
all-beta upper bound or as an assertion of attained exponential size.

For fixed beta let

    I_beta=sqrt(2)*(1+beta)-beta<2.

At each backward state put w_i=(y_i+beta)/(q_i+beta). If q_i>0,
the radial relation gives u_i-beta=(p(q_i)-beta)*w_i.
For a transition with q_i>0 and q_(i-1)>0, take the cross product
of(2.9) with v(q_(i-1)); the alpha_i term cancels, giving

    w_(i-1)=w_i*[1+
       (p(q_(i-1))*q_i-q_(i-1)*p(q_i))
                   /{beta*(p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1))}]. (7.1)

If q_(i-1)=0, then y_(i-1)=y_i, so (7.1) follows directly from
w_i*(q_i+beta)=y_i+beta. If q_i=0, both q values are0 by their
ordering and w_(i-1)=w_i. Thus all cap cases are covered.

The nonnegative numerator in(7.1) is

    integral_(q_(i-1))^(q_i)
                  [p(q_(i-1))-q_(i-1)*p'(t)]dt.

After division by p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1), its integrand is a weighted
mean of1 and -p'(t), at most max(1,-p'(t)). Multiplying(7.1) and
using1+x<=exp(x), q_N=1 and w_N=1 gives

    y+beta <=(q_0+beta)*exp[(1/beta)
                          integral_(q_0)^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt]
            <= beta*exp[(1/beta) integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt].         (7.2)

The last step uses log(1+q_0/beta)<=q_0/beta and the integrand's
lower bound1 on[0,q_0]. The derivative -p'(t) crosses1 at
t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta. Integrating separately below and above
that point yields exactly I_beta. The integrable endpoint derivative
at1 causes no problem. Hence for the actual finite backward word,

    1<=y<=beta*(exp(I_beta/beta)-1)
                                      <=beta*exp(2/beta).            (7.3)

Combine(5.1)--(5.2) with(2.11). They give the tighter elementary
finite estimates

    C*S/2+C-k*y <=u<=beta+C*S/2,
    0<M-C*S/2<=beta+y/beta<=beta+exp(2/beta).       (7.4)

For beta=8/m and m>=32, the rightmost exponential is exp(m/4).
Since exp(1/4)<sum_(j>=0)(1/4)^j=4/3<lambda and
S is bounded below by a positive constant times lambda^m uniformly
in tau, equations(7.3)--(7.4) imply uniformly in tau

    M/S-C/2 ->0, u/S-C/2 ->0, y/(beta*M)->0.        (7.5)

Here C depends on beta_m and tends to1. These are genuine estimates
for the constructed discrete data, not values assigned to them.
Equations(3.1),(3.8),(6.2),(7.5) imply

    D_2->d, D_3->d, R_true^2->1.                  (7.6)

The old terms are in fact0 by(1.7) for this sequence, as proved in
Section6; alternatively their nonnegativity and(1.5) suffice. The
exact whole payoff(4.6) therefore has the claimed attaining limit
after the original benchmark normalization in Section8.

## 8. Original benchmark and uniform all-beta limits

Keep the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences, without recalculation:

    C_0^bench=B_0=1,
    C_n^bench=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r^bench,C_(n-r-1)^bench),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r^bench,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                  (8.1)

The superscript bench only distinguishes the original primitive C_n
from the local circular endpoint C; it changes no recurrence.
Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, statement and proof Section7, gives
Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1)>0 finite. Its b=1,j=0 enclosure gives

    Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=(203/100)^2<21/5,      (8.2)

where B_1=G(1,1)=4. This unconditional benchmark conclusion is the
only verifier-accepted predecessor used. No conditional seed premise
or saved benchmark calculation is imported or replayed.

Using(6.2), uniformly in tau,

    B_(n_m)/(1+S_L) ->Z_1*lambda*exp(-tau),
    T_1*T_2/A_loc^2 ->d.                          (8.3)

These prove the Q_m limit in(1.8). Also B_(n_m)/S is uniformly
bounded and K_J/S tends to0 geometrically, since K_J=2+2^(J+1)
and S is uniformly comparable with lambda^m. Thus epsilon_m tends
to0 uniformly. All words and all beta choices share this same
word-only error bound.

Let V_m(tau)=sup_(0<beta<=1/4) B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,beta) and
V(tau)=Z_1*exp(-tau)/(2lambda). Since C>=1, (1.6) implies

    V_m(tau)<=Q_m(tau)+epsilon_m(tau).             (8.4)

On the other hand beta_m=8/m is allowed for m>=32. Equations(4.6),
(7.6),(8.3) show uniformly in tau

    B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,8/m)->V(tau).                 (8.5)

Thus V_m(tau) is bounded BELOW by a specific family member converging
uniformly to V(tau), and ABOVE by a word-only bound with the same
uniform limit. These two inequalities prove(1.9). No pointwise
limiting estimate was maximized over an uncontrolled beta interval.
They prove(1.10)--(1.11), since exp(-tau) is decreasing in tau.

For the stated strict constants, lambda>12/5 since sqrt(2)>7/5,
exp(1/4)>1+1/4 and exp(1/5)>1+1/5. Together with(8.2),

    Z_1*exp(-1/4)/(2lambda)
                         <(21/5)*(4/5)/(24/5)=7/10,
    Z_1*exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)
                         <(21/5)*(5/6)/(24/5)=35/48<3/4.             (8.6)

The strict margins and uniform convergence give a single sufficiently
large cutoff for each asserted finite deficit. These symbolic
inequalities required no numerical evaluation or arithmetic program.

## 9. Comparison mechanism, provenance and residual scope

The older large-beta argument estimated r approximately d_m before
comparing it to the gap. That approximation was not uniform below8/m.
Here the EXACT inequalities M>=C*A_loc, u<=beta+C*S/2 yield r<d_m
and(6.13); subtracting the true g_in cancels D_2*A_1. This is the
selected comparison's central identity. It is valid through every
small-beta transition, whether the initial radius shrinks or not.

The initial-radius issue is handled in two distinct proved cases,
not by assuming R_true tends to1 everywhere. If the old pair is
incoming-flat, it stays flat and(5.5) alone pays the radius. If it
reactivates, the exact root inequality itself gives R_true^2>1/4,
and the finite last-crossing bound controls its actual final value.

There is no remaining beta gap in this two-checkpoint comparison.
Both old terms and the terminal-coordinate term have an actual upper
bound, uniformly for the entire permitted interval0<beta<=1/4.
The limiting supremum and the finite eventual deficits are claims of
this complete draft, pending verification. Exact absorption has the
explicit sufficient criterion(1.7); claiming absorption for every
beta is unnecessary and is not part of the theorem.

The word is precisely(1.1)--(1.2), the function precisely(3.2), and
the closest radius precisely(3.8). Neither source-format normalization,
an older spiral radius, an extra bridge, nor a reordered tail enters.
The unrestricted W_n is a supremum over many other functions, so
this witness-family upper deficit cannot bound W_n above. Other
checkpoint selections and arbitrary counts are also outside this
statement. The original all-word minimax goal remains OPEN.

Provenance of the circular mechanism: Kaizhao Liu and Yuhan Ye,
Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent: The Strongly
Convex Case, arXiv:2609.26873v1,
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.26873v1 . The complete source draft
4c4a21a484564bdc records the earlier reading and direct convex
reconstruction. The present proof is finite and self-contained; it
imports no strong-convexity exponent, condition-number limit or
external theorem. Both frozen shared drafts were read completely.

Current repair retrieval: Matlas returned extragradient, small-step
inexact proximal-GD and line-search statements with incompatible
hypotheses. Web retrieval gave silver-schedule and circular-source
background. No theorem was imported. This continues the same already
audited finite construction; no new external machinery branch was
opened. Skills used: query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results.
The verify-proof instructions were read, but the current task and
guidance expressly prohibit the formal gate, so no fact is claimed.

## 10. Resource handoff

Mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0; arithmetic replays0;
executed recurrences0; solver calls0; benchmark DP0; installations0;
formal submissions0; newly accepted facts0; consults0; subagents0;
physical construction repairs0; analytical comparison repairs0;
additional bridge/checkpoint positions0. All prior grants stay closed.

Operations were text/source retrieval, administrative file I/O,

used scope was read directly. No computational or verifier job is

/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/small_arc_full_tail_20261001/.
Existing private local-memory files are appended for continuity.
This whole report is published as shared UNVERIFIED proof evidence.

Full self-contained proof is in shared research/max2_chronological_kernel_terminal_charge.md. Fix actual epsilon=1/m,c=(1+sqrt2)(1+epsilon),a=(1-epsilon)/c,d_k=1+a c^k. Extend only the level FORMULA with this same epsilon. The infinite birth array B from B00=1 has B_ik=0 when k>i or i>=2^k. Its elapsed-time quadratic defines a symmetric ROW-FINITE E, with E_kl=0 for k<l and l>2^k. Its first m rows/columns equal the original H_m-minus-last-d0 residual kernel. Polarization after setting every extended y_l=d_l v gives the EXACT constant load ellhat_k=(S+1)ell_k=sum_l E_kl d_l, not an independent variable. Define A_i=E_i,i+1, e_i=E_i,i+2, b_i=A_i-2(e_i-1+e_i), tau_i^-=sum_l<=i-3 |E_il|, tau_i^+=sum_l>=i+3 |E_il|, and t_i=E_ii-A_i-1-A_i+e_i-2-tau_i^-. Exact finite truncation gives r_i^[m]=t_i+e_i-sum_i+3<=l<m |E_il| for i<=m-3, r_m-2^[m]=t_m-2, r_m-1^[m]=t_m-1+A_m-1; b_last^[m]=b_last+2e_last. Thus the penultimate row loses an entire e term, which a bulk-only argument misses. ASSUME e_i>=0,tau_i^+<=e_i,t_i>=0,b_i>=2/15,A_i>=2/5 for i>=2, and 2d_i<=ellhat_i<=11d_i/4 for i>=2. These give all finite square signs, b^[m]>=2/15,r_last^[m]>=2/5. Exact early loads ellhat0=1,ellhat1=d0²/d1+d0+1 and d_i>2d_i-1 for i>=3 make ellhat positive increasing. For alpha_-1=0,alpha_i=ell_i-alpha_i-1, therefore 0<=alpha_i<=ell_i. Complete square charge C=sum_i<m-1 alpha_i²/b_i^[m]+alpha_last²/r_last^[m] is <=(121/16)/(S+1)²[(15/2)sum_i<m-1 d_i²+(5/2)d_last²]. Exact S+1=a c^m/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))2^m-d0>a c^m/(c-2), together with 2<c<39/16,a>2/5, bounds C by F(c)+2^-120, F(c)=(121/16)(c-2)²/c²[15/(2(c²-1))+5/2]. F increases for c>2; F(39/16)=1095787/1119456<49/50-1/1000, so C<49/50. D=S/(S+1)>999/1000 gives Delta=D-C>19/1000>1/100. The proof includes the full exact pair/triple/signed-far square identity, and the untouched original square ||x0-Sv-sum d_j w_j||². No pseudoinverse/range condition omitted. Further exact formula: R^-1_jk=prod_r<=k,r!=j(1-d_r/d_j)^-1=(-1)^(k-j)q_c^((k-j)(k-j+1)/2)(d_j/(d_j-1))^k/(P_j P_k-j), where q_c=1/c and P_r=prod_s=1..r(1-q_c^s). Lagrange interpolation proves this; P_r>1/4 gives |R^-1_jk|<16q_c^((k-j)(k-j+1)/2)exp(k/(ac^j)). This is an exact starting point for scalar estimates, NOT a proof of them. No new kernel case or verifier call.
