# Conditional multiscale backward terminal paths: full construction and scoped failure

JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT — UNVERIFIED. Worker max, allocated invocation76
after ended baseline75, ended ceiling76 / attempted ceiling77, cap1.
THREE explicit laws are constructed. No formal gate, mathematical program,
numerical/arithmetic replay, executed trajectory/recurrence, optimizer,
installation, consult or subagent is used. Controller completion and
counters are not observed. The original exact-profile goal remains OPEN.

All three laws are genuinely destination dependent: each original terminal
receiver has its own admissible dyadic nodes and its own normalization.
They use a backward path through THREE original boundary queries, rather
than an independent intermediate vertex. Law A uses every dyadic depth:
it pays all first four-block screens, but a larger ancestor defeats
the entire old H4 plane. Law B uses even dyadic depths: its entire old
H4 plane, actual root gradient plane and first four-block screens at
EVERY relevant depth pass. A different, two-gradient zero-displacement
vector defeats its FULL original matrix. Law C appends the original
first query as a relay; the named screens still pass, but its original
first-gradient diagonal is negative. Only these three specified laws
on this specified base are excluded.

## 1. Original target, physical word and authority

For a nonnegative length-n word h and L>0, W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite-dimensional
Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz
gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using the actual GD
updates x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. Define the original
all-split benchmark by

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every split k is an integer. Fix ONE enclosing integer m>=1536 and put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+a*c^j,Hword_j),
    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum Hword_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1, t_j=d_j/A_j, t_star=1/lambda.

Hword_j denotes a PHYSICAL word, not the conjugate quadratic H_j in
fact1252b8823bb5a290. All child words keep the SAME enclosing m and
epsilon. The final physical word is h=Hword_m=H_(m,1/4), n=n_m,
D=D_m. No child reset, coordinate compression or horizon change occurs.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 was read in full and supplies the original
interpolation convention and the exact terminal-square theorem.
Accepted04219554e41d9750 Sections10–11 supplies the full primitive and
objective conventions. Accepted5fd396eba75651e7 Section10 supplies ONLY

    B_(n_m)/D_m<237/250, m>=1536.                         (1)

An actual full-function upper zeta_m/D_m with sup zeta_m<250/237
would produce an original benchmark separation on unbounded horizons.
This report produces no such upper. Restricted witness-family values
and negative formal arrays are not W upper bounds.

FULL9161eec3956e6969 and scoped companions e66de81a517b1e1d,
18deb80e726bbbcc,37353de9815ac111,c20e154f37432d63,ffe859645ec57eec
were recalled as UNVERIFIED awareness. The full shared evidence equals
the local prior manuscript read in bounded portions. No formula from
it is used as an accepted brick: all needed algebra is derived jointly
below. Guidance87f657463efe08da and plan65f1af923eccb229 govern this
bounded invocation; its initial one-law selection is followed by the
second and third permitted laws after the audits described below,
recorded in plan95b53e237fb46483. Latest guidancebd2b8e8fc46ee8bb
explicitly leaves max76 unchanged; no new allocation is inferred.

## 2. Full original matrix and total common/contact prices

Normalize smoothness1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep every independent
formal vector (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including optimum, terminal, every bridge
and any zero-step query. Put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t,
    S=sum h, D=1+2S, h_n=0,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Actual legal samples have Q_ij<=0 by accepted1252b8823bb5a290,
proof paragraph1. Let K_ij be finite-node multipliers, nu_i outgoing
optimum multipliers i->star, xiCert_i incoming optimum multipliers
star->i, and K_ii=0. Full value cancellation is

    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).        (2)

After cancellation, the ENTIRE original certificate is

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum K_ij Q_ij
                       +sum nu_i Q_(i,star)+sum xiCert_i Q_(star,i)
        =q||x_0||^2-sum nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g).          (3)

Writing out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij, its full gradient matrix is

    Cgrad_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2Cgrad_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv
                            +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (4)

Indeed half the incident multiplier mass at i equals out_i-1_(i=n)
by(2). A forward position term contributes -h_i K_iv on its source
diagonal. For i<j the difference-square terms give -(K_ij+K_ji),
source i's later destinations give the second term, and source j's
earlier destinations and optimum row give the last term. This derives
ALL diagonals, off-diagonals and the initial mixed column -nu/2.
For q>0, full PSD is equivalent to

    Cgrad-nu nu^T/(4q)>=0 on the ENTIRE gradient space.    (5)

Keep both optimum directions explicit before selecting xiCert=0.
Let e be the all-ones gradient column, csum=Cgrad e,
X=sum xiCert_i, E=sum(1+Ppos_i)xiCert_i, Hprice=D X-E.
Summing(2) gives sum nu=2+X. Multiplying(2) by Ppos_i and summing
gives sum K_ij(Ppos_i-Ppos_j)=2S-sum(nu_i-xiCert_i)Ppos_i.
Consequently

    e^T Cgrad e=D+E,
    M(x_0=r,g=t e)=q r^2-(2+X)r t+(D+E)t^2.           (6)

The one-sided Huber function is zero on z<=0, z^2/2 on[0,1], and
z-1/2 on z>=1. Its gradient is1-Lipschitz and its minimum is zero.
Starting at x_0=D gives all gradients1 and all positions D-Ppos_i>=1.
All finite and outgoing optimum residuals vanish; the incoming one is
Q_(star,i)=-(2S-Ppos_i). At q=zeta/D,

    M(D,e)=(zeta-1)D-Hprice.                            (7)

Thus Hprice<=(zeta-1)D is ONE whole-word allowance. Completing the
initial/common block, for a transverse gradient vector v with e^T v=0,
requires the exact charge

    Price(v)=[(D+E)(nu^T v)^2/4
      -(2+X)(nu^T v)(csum^T v)/2+q(csum^T v)^2]
          /[q(D+E)-(2+X)^2/4]                         (8)

when the denominator is positive. It is obtained by inverting the
2-by-2 matrix in(6), with coupling(-nu^T v/2,csum^T v).
When that matrix is singular, the coupling must annihilate its kernel.
No common, initial or singular direction is deleted.

All three laws choose every xiCert_i=0 and preserve every nu_i. Then
sum nu=2, e^T Cgrad e=D, Hprice=0. Set

    dcontact=csum-D nu/2, e^T dcontact=0.

For zeta>1, the full charge becomes

    Price(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D
                    +(dcontact^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)].     (9)

For example zeta=101/100 has the coefficient100/D on the second
square. These are exact retained expenses, not claimed paid estimates.
An original negative direction with x_0=0 survives every inflation of q.

## 3. Explicit base signs and value flow

We prescribe a base, not a PSD-selection oracle. Its coefficients are
closed finite sums/products over actual dyadic nodes. Summing the word
gives

    v_h=a/(c-2),
    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                   (10)

Here0<v_h<1 because c(c-2)>1>beta. Therefore t_j increases and
t_j<c-2<1/2. Exact small identities are

    b=1+a=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],
    A_1=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], d_1=1-epsilon,
    A_2=lambda^2-epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))],
    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2), d_3=c*d_2,
    lambda d_1-A_1=-epsilon[lambda-2/(lambda(1+epsilon))],
    lambda d_2-A_2=epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda^2 epsilon]. (11)

The first bracket is positive and the second is positive, using
12/5<lambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/6144. Also t_0<t_star. Hence

    t_0,t_1<t_star<t_2,
    0<t_j-t_star<lambda epsilon for j>=2,
    A_(j+1)>lambda A_j for j>=2, A_j>=2^j.            (12)

The last inequality follows from A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j. Elementary rational
enclosures707/500<sqrt(2)<283/200 in(11) also give

    7/5<b<sqrt(2)<10/7,
    582/100<A_2<584/100,
    241/100<d_2<242/100, d_3<584/100,
    A_3<15, A_2+d_2<9.                              (13)

For instance A_2=3+2b-epsilon and the displayed formula for b give
the A_2 enclosure directly; d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2) gives its interval;
d_3=lambda^2(1-epsilon^2)(1+epsilon)<584/100 follows by inserting
lambda<483/200 and epsilon<=1/6144. All are textual rational inequalities.

Define a primitive finite array Lambda^j on0..n_j, starting Lambda^0=0.
At the join Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+d_j,Hword_j), let p be the bridge,
z its terminal, and i range over nonterminal queries of its right child.
Embed the left array with weight1 and the right array with weight k_j;
add the four row types

    i->p: u_j h_i,   i->z: v_j h_i,
    z->p: w_j,       p->z: z_j,
    u_j=2lambda,
    v_j=[2lambda d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,
    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,
    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-2lambda(A_j-1), z_j=2A_j.          (14)

All other additions are zero and overlapping rows are summed. The
unrolled coefficient of each original pair is the finite sum of these
four row types over every dyadic node, multiplied by k_l over exactly
the ancestors in whose right child the node lies; left ancestor factors
are1. This specifies every coefficient without computing a recurrence.

All rows are nonnegative: u,v,k,z are positive and
w_j=2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda). For j>=2 use(12); for j=0 use
w_0=4+2a; for j=1 use A_1<lambda and w_1>2lambda-2>0.
The identities u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1) and
u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j) prove divergence -2h_i
at each nonterminal query and2(A_(j+1)-1) at its terminal.

## 4. All-direction source and base identities, jointly expanded

Define the complete source polynomial

    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))
          +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
          -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,
    Psrc_j=V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.

At one join put r=sum_(i right,i<z)h_i g_i,
Y=r-(A_j-1)g_z, Z=g_p-g_z. The four added row polynomials in(14),
before their scalar coefficients, are respectively

    d_j<r,g_p>+||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                     +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;
    -||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                     +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r,g_z>;
    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;
    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r>-<g_p,g_z>.

Their r^2,r*g_p,r*g_z coefficients are(u_j-v_j)/2,
u_j d_j-z_j,w_j-v_j. Adding the displayed V_j and child polynomials
and using(14) yields the identity on ALL original gradients

    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right
       +(t_star-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2
          -2lambda A_j<Y,Z>+A_j^2(2+t_star+t_j)||Z||^2]. (15)

The bracket is positive definite: its determinant divided by A_j^2
is(lambda+1)(2+t_star+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0. Since t_j>t_star
for j>=2, this identity does not prove positive-epsilon child PSD.
An all-common child has Psrc_j=0 and zero derivative by(15).

Define the complete objective base Kbase^j,mubase^j by
Kbase^0=0,mubase^0_0=2,xiCert=0. At the same join put

    A=A_j-1, bjoin=1+d_j, Dp=D_(j+1), Dc=D_j,
    s_j=(A+bjoin)/(3A+2bjoin), theta_j=1-s_j.

With original maps L_l=l,p=L_(n_j),R_i=n_j+1+i, prescribe

    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/Dp,
    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,
    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mubase^j_i,
    mubase_(L_l)=2h_l/Dp for l<n_j,
    mubase_p=2s_j-2A/Dp,
    mubase_(R_i)=theta_j mubase^j_i.                  (16)

Unlisted finite rows and all incoming optimum rows are zero. The bridge
optimum coefficient is positive because
(A+bjoin)Dp-A(3A+2bjoin)=A^2+4A*bjoin+2bjoin^2+A+bjoin>0.
Here s_0=1/2 and1/3<s_j<1/2 for j>=1. Left primitive divergence
is canceled by its optimum coefficients and the bridge; each right row
keeps its flow since s_j+theta_j=1. This proves(2) and sum mubase=2.
The original terminal has no incoming finite rows.

For a closed original-index formula at depth r, each right-spine node
of depth l has offset o_l=2^r-2^l and bridge
p_l=n_r-2^(l-1),1<=l<=r. Include Lambda^(l-1)/D_l on its
left block[o_l,p_l], and s_(l-1)mubase^(l-1)_i from each right
query, including the final query, to p_l. These are all finite rows.
The optimum row at i<p_l in that left block is
(2h_i/D_l)product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t; at p_l it is
[2s_(l-1)-2(A_(l-1)-1)/D_l]product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t.
At n_r it is2product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t. The same closed formula
within each indicated child supplies every mubase in the spine rows.

Let astar_r=product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t, astar_0=1. Every original
terminal outgoing probability is therefore explicitly

    alpha_star^base=astar_r,
    alpha_(p_l)^base=s_(l-1)astar_(l-1),1<=l<=r,
    alpha_i^base=0 otherwise.                        (17)

They sum to1 since s_j astar_j=astar_j-astar_(j+1).

To retain the initial and all inherited mixed terms, put
r_R=sum_i mubase^j_i g_(R_i),
x_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_(L_l),
Rchild_R=Cbase^j(g_R)-Dc||r_R||^2/4, kappa_j=Dc-Dp theta_j^2.
After extracting the source, the remaining full polynomial is

    ||x_p||^2/Dp+2(A/Dp-s_j)<x_p,g_p>
      +(A^2/Dp+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,r_R>
      +(bjoin-s_j)<g_p,r_R>+Dc||r_R||^2/4.

Using s_j(3A+2bjoin)=A+bjoin completes it to

    Mbase_(j+1)(1/Dp)=Psrc_j(g_L)/Dp+Rchild_R
      +||x_p+(A-Dp s_j)g_p-(Dp theta_j/2)r_R||^2/Dp
      +(kappa_j/4)||r_R-2g_p||^2.                    (18)

Neither Rchild_R nor kappa_j is assumed nonnegative at epsilon>0.
At x_0=Dp,g=e, the source and all displayed squares have value and
derivative zero, by the inherited contact and sum mubase=2. Starting
from the empty square proves the BASE contact identity

    Cbase e=D mubase/2.                              (19)

It is an algebraic contact identity, not a PSD inference.

## 5. THREE complete conditional destination/scale-dependent laws

A dyadic node inside Hword_r of depth d and offset o means the actual
embedded child word Hword_d with query indices o..o+n_d. These nodes
are specified equivalently by offsets o=k*2^d,0<=k<2^(r-d).
For each PROPER node of depth d=s+2>=4, define three original queries

    i1(vnode)=o+2^s-1,
    i2(vnode)=o+2^(s+1)-1,
    i3(vnode)=o+3*2^s-1,
    end(vnode)=o+2^(s+2)-1,
    wt(vnode)=A_s.                                   (20)

The node label vnode is distinct from an original receiver index v.
The physical steps at these three queries are1+d_s,1+d_(s+1),1+d_s.
For each finite base terminal receiver v=p_l, make exactly these THREE
choices of eligible sets and total fractions:

    Law A: Elig_v contains ALL proper dyadic vnode of depth s+2>=4
           with end(vnode)<=v; rhoPath=256epsilon=64/m<=1/24.
    Law B: Elig_v contains exactly the EVEN-depth proper vnode,
           depth=s+2>=4 with s even and end(vnode)<=v;
           rhoPath=4096epsilon=1024/m<=2/3.
    Law C: SAME Elig_v, ZPath_v, conditional probabilities and fraction
           as Law B, but append ORIGINAL query0 to its prefix path.

For each specified law separately, set

    ZPath_v=sum_(vnode in Elig_v) A_s,
    cond_(vnode|v)=A_s/ZPath_v on Elig_v, zero elsewhere. (21)

For r>=5 every ZPath_v is positive in ALL three laws: the first depth4
node ends at15<=p_r<=p_l and is allowed in both choices. For r<=4
the eligible sets are empty and all three laws make no change. At r>=5 set

    omegaPath_(v,vnode)=rhoPath alpha_v^base A_s/ZPath_v. (22)

For every listed v and vnode, replace exactly2omegaPath of the old
terminal edge n_r->v by the FOUR edges

    n_r->i3(vnode)->i2(vnode)->i1(vnode)->v.           (23)

This is the path in Laws A and B. Law C replaces the same terminal
mass by the FIVE-edge path

    n_r->i3(vnode)->i2(vnode)->i1(vnode)->0->v.        (23c)

Its original indices are all distinct; i1>=3 and v>i3. In particular
no coordinate at the first physical query is omitted or identified.

Every index is an ORIGINAL query and
i1<i2<i3<end<=v<n_r. This changes both terminal and prefix rows;
there is no averaged primal trajectory or expected-orthogonality step.
Eligibility and normalization depend on v; node mass depends on s.
For example the last receiver's prefix admits right-child nodes that
the root receiver's prefix excludes. The law is not separable pi_i*alpha_v.

Here are every FINAL terminal probability and finite/optimum multiplier,
with n=n_r and alpha_v=alpha_v^base. For r>=5,

    alpha_star^path=astar_r,
    alpha_i^path=(1-rhoPath)alpha_i
       +sum_(v,vnode)omegaPath_(v,vnode)*1_(i=i3(vnode)), i<n,
    Kpath_(n,i)=2alpha_i^path, nupath_n=2astar_r,
    Kpath_(i,n)=0 for i<n,
    Kpath_(u,v)=Kbase_(u,v)
       +2sum_(w,vnode)omegaPath_(w,vnode)*[
          1_(u=i3(vnode),v=i2(vnode))
         +1_(u=i2(vnode),v=i1(vnode))
         +1_(u=i1(vnode),v=w)], u,v<n,u!=v,
    nupath_i=mubase_i for i<n, xiCertpath_i=0 for all i. (24)

Formula(24) gives Laws A and B exactly. Law C has the SAME explicit
terminal probabilities, terminal rows and optimum rows as Law B, but
in its finite-prefix bracket replace1_(u=i1,v=w) by the TWO indicators
1_(u=i1,v=0)+1_(u=0,v=w). This gives every original Law C coefficient
as the same closed finite sum with FOUR prefix edges per selected path.

All repeated original pairs are summed. No diagonal path edge occurs.
At r<=4 use the complete base unchanged, and at r=0 use the empty
certificate of1252b8823bb5a290. Formula(24), together with(10),(14),
(16),(17),(20),(21), is a closed finite sum/product formula for every row.

The sole decrement at each actual terminal column v is
2sum_(vnode in Elig_v)omegaPath=2rhoPath alpha_v. It is paid by that
SAME original edge2alpha_v, leaving(1-rhoPath)2alpha_v>=0.
No prefix row or optimum row is decremented. All final signs are thus
nonnegative. Each replacement path has exactly the original divergence:
source n has unchanged outgoing mass, destination v has unchanged
incoming mass, and every internal path vertex has one incoming and one
outgoing addition. Therefore every original value-flow equation(2) holds.
Inside the prefix its divergence is exactly2alpha_i^path, including
coincident occurrences from different paths. The terminal mass remains2.

With Tmass=1-astar_r, the ENTIRE terminal decrement is2rhoPath Tmass,
the shifted terminal probability is rhoPath Tmass, and the ENTIRE
prefix addition is6rhoPath Tmass in Laws A/B and8rhoPath Tmass in
Law C. The respective total bounds are1/4,4,16/3 (strict in B/C).
Laws B/C retain at least one third of each original terminal edge's
coefficient; Law A retains at least23/24. These are sums over ALL
allowed scales in this ONE word. No objective allowance is renewed
per node, and prefix mass is not itself a PSD or objective-slack bound.

## 6. Complete path polynomial, terminal square and contact defect

For original indices n,k,i,v, direct cancellation gives

    Q_(n,k)+Q_(k,i)-Q_(n,i)=<g_n-g_k,y_k-y_i>, y_i=x_i-g_i.

The values cancel. The position terms give <g_n-g_k,x_k-x_i>;
the gradient-difference terms give -<g_n-g_k,g_k-g_i>.
Applying this identity successively along(23), or expanding its four
rows, yields the WHOLE original polynomial

    Mpath(q)=Mbase(q)+2sum_(v,vnode)omegaPath EPath_(v,vnode),
    EPath=<g_n-g_i3,y_i3-y_v>
           +<g_i3-g_i2,y_i2-y_v>
           +<g_i2-g_i1,y_i1-y_v>.                    (25)

This is EPath in Laws A/B. Law C's complete path polynomial is

    EPath_C=EPath_B+<g_i1-g_0,y_0-y_v>.              (25c)

It follows by the same triangle expansion of i1->0->v. Law C is
constructed directly from the base by(23c), so it decrements no original
prefix row: the minus term in the comparison with B is paid by B's
explicit ADDED i1->v row, not an undeclared original edge.

Every original position in y uses the unchanged physical prefix. There
is no x_0 contribution to a position difference, so the entire mixed
column remains -mubase/2. Equations(4),(24),(25) specify all coefficients.

Using the ACTUAL alpha^path and prefix rows in(24), accepted
1252b8823bb5a290(c) gives the exact full terminal completion

    V_path=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j^path y_j,
    R_path=q||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j^path delta_j
              -||V_path||^2+sum_(i!=j in J_pre)lambda^pre_path_ij Q_ij,
    delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2, delta_star=y_star=0,
    Mpath=||g_n-V_path||^2+R_path.                    (26)

Here J_pre={star,0,...,n-1}; lambda^pre consists exactly of the prefix
Kpath and nupath, with every incoming optimum row zero. No earlier
prefix interpolation pair is deleted. With
w_t^path=astar_r+sum_(i<=t)alpha_i^path,

    V_path=astar_r*x_0-sum_(t<n)(h_t w_t^path-alpha_t^path)g_t.

These are explicit finite coefficients, not a free feasible-set oracle.
Full PSD requires PSD of the ENTIRE R_path, not a conditional covariance
or selected-plane estimate.

At a common gradient, y_i-y_v=Ppos_v-Ppos_i. Differentiating(25)
gives the FULL contact defect, since the base contact is(19): sum the
following column increments over all v,vnode:

    at n:  omegaPath*(Ppos_v-Ppos_i3),
    at i3: omegaPath*(Ppos_i3-Ppos_i2),
    at i2: omegaPath*(Ppos_i2-Ppos_i1),
    at i1: omegaPath*(Ppos_i1-Ppos_v).                 (27)

For Law C keep the n,i3,i2 increments unchanged and replace the last
one by increments omegaPath*Ppos_i1 at i1 and -omegaPath*Ppos_v at0.
These are obtained by differentiating(25c), with Ppos_0=0. Their sum
is again zero; no original initial-point mixed row changes.

Every other component is zero for that path; overlapping components are
summed. Their total is zero. The n component is strictly positive because
every path has i3<v and all physical steps are positive. Thus the
singular zeta=1 common/initial block fails its required kernel condition:
dcontact!=0. For zeta>1, substitute this full column in(9); the exact
transverse charge remains. Huber slack is exactly(zeta-1)D, the entire
common block is [[zeta/D,-1],[-1,D]], and the initial Schur charge is
mubase mubase^T/(4q). Nothing in these charges is claimed free or paid
by the screens below. The final counterdirection evaluates the ENTIRE
polynomial(25), including the terminal square and all these coordinates.

## 7. Entire H2 and Silver calibrations

At r<=4 all laws have no path; their H2 calibration is the complete base.
The first original join gives

    Psrc_1=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2,
    Mbase_1(1/D_1)=||x_0-D_1(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/D_1
                                 +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4. (28)

These follow by substituting A_0=1,d_0=b-1 into(15),(18).
Both are PSD since b<sqrt(2). At H2 the physical bridge is2-epsilon;
D_1>19/5,D_2<75/7, and

    theta_1=(2b+2-epsilon)/(3b+4-2epsilon)<59/100.

The ratio increases in b and decreases in the bridge. Use b<10/7,
2-epsilon>199/100, then cross-multiply. It follows that
D_2 theta_1^2<15/4 and kappa_1>1/20. The ENTIRE H1 Schur remainder
is PSD by(28), and(18) then proves full original-vector H2 PSD.

At epsilon=0, all rhoPath are0 and t_j=t_star at every depth, so Psrc_j=0
by(15). Writing A=A_j-1 and bjoin=1+t_star(A+1), exact expansion gives

    (3A+2bjoin)^2*kappa_j
      =2(1-t_star)A^3+(6-8t_star)A^2
        +(1+t_star)(8t_star-2)A+(1+t_star)^2(1-2t_star)>0. (29)

Every coefficient is positive because1/4<t_star<1/2. Starting with the
empty square, use(18),(19) to prove FULL Silver PSD inductively.
Only in this proved epsilon=0 calibration is child PSD invoked.

## 8. One exact enclosing-m normalization for every screen

At depth m let

    Z_all=sum_(s=2)^(m-3)2^(m-s-2)A_s,
    XiPath=sum_(l=1)^m alpha_(p_l)^base/ZPath_(p_l).    (30)

There are exactly2^(m-s-2) proper depth-(s+2) nodes in Hword_m.
Every eligible-node normalization is <=Z_all. Put Bmass=A_(m-1).
By(12), A_s<Bmass/lambda^(m-1-s). Therefore, using lambda(lambda-2)=1,

    Z_all<(Bmass/2)sum_(r=2)^infinity(2/lambda)^r
          =2Bmass,
    D_m/Z_all>(4Bmass-1)/(2Bmass)>3/2.               (31)

Since theta_j<=2/3, astar_m<1/100; for example m>=12 and
(2/3)^12<1/100 suffice. Hence Tmass>99/100 and

    D_m XiPath>Tmass*D_m/Z_all>297/200>7/5,
    2rhoPath D_m XiPath>700epsilon in Law A,
    2rhoPath D_m XiPath>11000epsilon in Laws B/C.      (32)

These are sums/bounds over the SAME entire enclosing word, not a
separate constant allowance at every scale.

For any test with x_0=0, zero total physical displacement and zero
gradient at the terminal of the FIRST root left child, supported
within that child, every base receiver and its y_v are zero.
For Laws A/B, all ALLOWED nodes in that root left child occur in EVERY Elig_(p_l); their
omega coefficients sum to rhoPath XiPath A_s. Nodes outside it have
all path-endpoint gradients zero. Thus(25) reduces EXACTLY to

    D_m Mpath(q)=D_m Mbase(q)
       +2rhoPath D_m XiPath*sum_(active nodes)A_s EPath_node. (33)

Equation(33) concerns Laws A/B. Law C's additional origin-relay term
can be nonzero on nodes outside the root left child; its ENTIRE extra
sum is retained separately in Section13. The named screens below
identify every active node. No change of
normalizer, child PSD estimate or locally repeated slack enters(33).

## 9. Entire old two-dimensional original H4 plane

Put Wloc=A_2+d_2,Uloc=A_2+d_3,rloc=Uloc/Wloc and

    Edef=(t_2-t_star)A_2^2(lambda+t_2)>0,
    delta3=t_3-t_star>0.

Choose ORIGINAL scalar gradients u on0..3, v0 on4..7, w on8..11,
and zero on12..n_m, with x_0=0 and Wloc(u+w)+Uloc*v0=0.
Call this two-dimensional plane N and normN=u^2+v0^2+w^2.
There is no equality constraint on formal coordinates: this is a
particular assignment of their test values.

The two H3 joins and then the H4 join in(15) give the entire source

    P_N=-Edef(u-v0)^2-k_3 Edef*w^2
       -delta3[(lambda+1)Wloc^2*w^2
        -2lambda A_3 Wloc*w*v0+A_3^2(2+t_star+t_3)*v0^2]. (34)

For the left H3, Y=0,Z=u-v0; the right H3 gives -Edef*w^2;
at H4, Y=Wloc*w,Z=v0. All later all-left source joins have Y=Z=0.
All boundary and right variables in the final(18) vanish. Therefore
D_m Mbase(q)=P_N for every real q. By(11)–(13),
Edef<24epsilon,k_3<7,delta3<(5/2)epsilon,Wloc<9,A_3<15.
Bound the cross coefficient by338(w^2+v0^2), and use
(u-v0)^2<=2(u^2+v0^2),lambda+1<4,2+t_star+t_3<3. This yields

    P_N>-3000epsilon*normN.                         (35)

The first depth4 node has endpoints3,7,11. Its actual proximal points are

    y_3=-A_2 u,
    y_7=-Wloc*u-A_2*v0,
    y_11=-Wloc*u-Uloc*v0-A_2*w.

Substitution into the THREE terms of(25), retaining y_v=0, gives

    FPath_N^target=A_2*[A_2(u^2+v0^2+w^2)+d_2*u*v0+d_3*v0*w]. (36)

Indeed -w*y_11+(w-v0)*y_7+(v0-u)*y_3 cancels the nonlocal
u*w terms and leaves precisely the bracket in(36). This displayed
three-variable bracket is positive definite: by(13) and Young's inequality,

    bracket >=[A_2-(d_2+d_3)/2]*normN
             >(169/100)*normN,
    FPath_N^target>9normN.                          (37)

However Law A has ONE additional active node: the first depth5 ancestor,
whose FIRST selected boundary is query7 carrying v0, and whose other
selected boundaries15,23 have gradient zero. Its exact energy is
-v0*y_7=Wloc*u*v0+A_2*v0^2. Every larger ancestor's selected endpoints
have zero gradients. Thus the WHOLE Law A restriction is

    FPath_N^A=FPath_N^target
                +A_3[Wloc*u*v0+A_2*v0^2].          (38a)

At w=0,v0=1,u=-rloc, put t=d_2/A_2,q_3=d_3/A_2=c*t,
q_0=t(2+t)>1. Direct substitution into(38a) gives

    FPath_N^A/A_2^2=
      [q_3^2-t(2+t)(3+t)q_3+(2+t)]/(1+t)^2<0.       (38b)

Indeed q_3>q_0 by(10), while c<5/2 implies
2q_3-t(2+t)(3+t)=t[2c-(2+t)(3+t)]<0. The numerator decreases
throughout[q_0,q_3], and at q_0 equals(2+t)(1-q_0^2)<0.
The base P_N is strictly negative by(34),(15), so Law A's COMPLETE
original matrix is negative on this N vector for EVERY real q.

The depth5 ancestor is excluded in Law B. No other allowed node has
nonzero endpoint gradients on N. Therefore its whole FPath_N^B is
EXACTLY FPath_N^target. Using(32),(33),(35),(37), at every nonzero N point,

    D_m Mpath_B(q)|N>96000epsilon*normN>0.           (38c)

This pays the WHOLE old plane at the actual global normalization for
Law B only. Before shared publication, the manuscript audit found
the missing depth5 term in the initial Law A draft. That draft's old
plane positivity claim was withdrawn; (38a),(38b) give the corrected
complete restriction. No false screen result is published as a fact.

## 10. All relevant first four-block depths, with exact total action

For each integer0<=j<=m-3 set Kblock=2^j and Lblock=A_j+d_j.
Inside the FIRST actual Hword_(j+2), choose original scalar gradients
1 on[0,Kblock-1],0 on[Kblock,2Kblock-1],-1 on[2Kblock,3Kblock-1],
and zero on[3Kblock,n_m], with x_0=0. Both active blocks are the
physical Hword_j followed by its actual bridge1+d_j. Their displacements
cancel exactly. Every later gradient and position, the optimum and
global terminal included, is zero.

Put E_j=(t_j-t_star)A_j^2(lambda+t_j). Identity(15) gives

    P_j^four=-(1+k_(j+1))E_j
          -(t_(j+1)-t_star)(lambda+1)Lblock^2,
    D_m Mbase(q)=P_j^four.                          (39)

The first Hword_(j+1) has Y=0,Z=1; the third active copy gives the
same source; the Hword_(j+2) join has Y=-Lblock,Z=0.
At j>=2, all coefficients in(39) are losses, and(12) gives

    -100epsilon*A_j^2<P_j^four<0.                  (40)

In detail E_j<(15/2)epsilon A_j^2,1+k_(j+1)<8,
and the second loss is less(45/2)epsilon A_j^2; their sum is <100.
At j=0, both t_0-t_star and t_1-t_star are negative, hence(39)>0.
At j=1, |t_1-t_star|>(6/25)epsilon,A_1>12/5,lambda>12/5 imply
|E_1|>3epsilon and1+k_2>5. Also(11) implies
t_2-t_star<epsilon/5 and Lblock=A_1+d_1<7/2.
The negative second term is then <343epsilon/40, so P_1^four>6epsilon.
Thus every depth0..m-3 is explicitly accounted for.

At j=0,1 no selected node has a nonzero endpoint gradient, so the path
addition is zero. At j=2 the only active node is the target depth4 node,
and its EPath=2A_2. For j>=3 Law A's full weighted action in(33) is

    FPath_j^A=2A_j^2+A_(j-1)(A_j-d_j)
       +sum_(s=2)^(j-2)2^(j-s-2)A_s[2A_s+d_(s+1)-d_j]. (41)

Empty sums are zero, and FPath_2^A=2A_2^2.
Here is the full node accounting. The target node has endpoint
gradients(1,0,-1), giving EPath=2A_j. Its two depth-(j+1) children
have endpoint gradients(1,1,0) and(-1,-1,0), with paired energy
A_j-d_j; their common weight is A_(j-1). Every smaller node of depth
s+2 in a constant active block has all three endpoint gradients equal,
so EPath=-g_i3*y_i3. Pair its first and third-block copies. At that
fixed depth there are2^(j-s-2) node pairs. Palindromicity of Hword_j
gives mean node-start physical prefix(S_j-S_(s+2))/2. In Hword_(s+2),

    Ppos_i3=3A_s+d_s+d_(s+1)-1,
    2Ppos_i3-S_(s+2)=2A_s+d_(s+1)-1.

Therefore the paired sum is2A_s+d_(s+1)-d_j, giving the last term
of(41). Every inactive block has zero endpoint gradients. Every larger
ancestor's selected endpoints are in zero-gradient blocks, so contributes
zero. This accounts for ALL nodes and their actual prefix positions.

Finally(12) gives

    sum_(s=2)^(j-2)2^(j-s-2)A_s
       <(A_j/4)sum_(r=2)^infinity(2/lambda)^r=A_j.

Discard the positive child term in(41) and the positive2A_s+d_(s+1)
part of the last sum. Since d_j<A_j/2,

    FPath_j^A>(3/2)A_j^2 for every j>=2.             (42)

Using the SAME XiPath and D_m in(32),(33), all these original modes have

    D_m Mpath_A(q)>950epsilon*A_j^2,2<=j<=m-3.       (43a)

For Law B, retaining exactly the even s in the SAME complete node
accounting gives

    FPath_j^B=2*1_(j even)A_j^2
       +1_(j odd)A_(j-1)(A_j-d_j)
       +sum_(2<=s<=j-2,s even)2^(j-s-2)A_s
                                     [2A_s+d_(s+1)-d_j]. (43b)

For even j, the proof of(42) applies with fewer negative descendants,
so FPath_j^B>(3/2)A_j^2. For odd j>=3, use c<49/20 and
t_j<c-2<9/20. Its positive child term is >11A_j^2/49.
Writing r=j-s, its descendant exponents are odd r>=3, and

    sum_(2<=s<=j-2,s even)2^(j-s-2)A_s
       <(A_j/4)sum_(r>=3,r odd)(2/lambda)^r
       <(125/264)A_j,

because2/lambda<5/6 and the geometric sum is125/66. Their total
negative part is less(75/352)A_j^2. Hence

    FPath_j^B>[11/49-75/352]A_j^2
              =(197/17248)A_j^2>A_j^2/100, j odd.

Combining its OWN same-word XiPath with(32),(33),(40) now gives

    D_m Mpath_B(q)>10epsilon*A_j^2,2<=j<=m-3.        (43c)

The positive base action at j=0,1 remains unchanged. In particular
the prior actual larger j=m-3 mode is paid in BOTH laws, irrespective
of its parity. This is an all-depth screen
calculation, not an assertion that these modes exhaust the original space.

## 11. Actual root bridge/right-first gradient plane

Let j=m-1,Bmass=A_j,p=n_j,qright=p+1. No path endpoint equals p:
the highest bridge p belongs only to the excluded whole-word root node.
No endpoint equals qright either: all selected endpoints are3 modulo4,
whereas qright is0 modulo4. In the test with x_0=g_n=0 and only
g_p,g_qright nonzero, EVERY gradient difference multiplying y in(25)
is zero in Laws A/B. The WHOLE path action is zero on this plane, including
all remote receivers and all positions.

For an explicit full base check let pterm_j=Psrc_j with only its
terminal gradient1. Substitution in(15) gives the finite sum

    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(t_star-t_l)F_l product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v,
    F_l=A_l^2(1+t_l)-2A_l+(lambda+1)>0.             (44)

This is an exact symbolic identity, not an executed recurrence. For l>=2,

    k_l-(2+t_l)^2=(t_star-t_l)(1+t_l)<0,
    F_l/A_(l+1)^2<=(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2<=1/4.

The first two terms are nonnegative. If the normalized diagonal is
negative, multiplication by k_l/(2+t_l)^2 in(0,1) increases it;
if positive, the product stays positive. Each later decrement is at most
lambda epsilon/4. Therefore

    pterm_j>=-j lambda epsilon*A_j^2/4>-5A_j^2/32,
                                                   j=m-1. (45)

Collecting the original rows(16) with(4), or expanding(18) on the
two-query restriction, gives

    Cbase_pp=2s_j+[(Bmass-1)^2+pterm_j]/D_m,
    Cbase_qright,qright=2/D_j,
    Cbase_p,qright=b(1+d_j-s_j)/D_j.               (46)

The source terminal coefficient is A_j^2-1+pterm_j before insertion;
the right-first row has mubase_0=2b/D_j. These identities retain all
inherited finite rows. Since Bmass>1250,2Bmass/5<d_j<Bmass/2,
D_m<5Bmass, (45),(46) give

    Cbase_pp>Bmass/6,
    Cbase_qright,qright>1/Bmass,
    7/25<Cbase_p,qright<2/5.                        (47)

For the first use[(27/32)Bmass^2-2Bmass+1]/(5Bmass)>Bmass/6;
the others follow from7/5<b<10/7 and1/3<s_j<1/2 by cross multiplication.
The determinant is >1/6-4/25=1/150>0. Hence the entire specified
root bridge/right-first GRADIENT plane passes unchanged. This does
not remove the initial/terminal variables or claim their larger Schur
space is PSD: those variables and charges remain in(3)–(9),(25),(26).

## 12. A different original two-gradient wave defeats FULL PSD

For EVERY integer2<=j<=m-3 let rbridge=2^j-1 and hbridge=1+d_j.
Use the following scalar values for the ORIGINAL independent vectors:

    x_0=0,
    g_0=-hbridge/b,
    g_rbridge=1,
    EVERY other g_i=0, INCLUDING g_n.                (48)

The physical first step is b, the physical step at rbridge is hbridge.
Thus their exact displacement is b*g_0+hbridge*g_rbridge=0.
Every subsequent original point, including every base terminal receiver,
is zero. The original query at rbridge has x_rbridge=hbridge,
y_rbridge=d_j. No interpolable primal status is presumed or needed
for a test of full-space PSD.

The first Hword_j has only its first and terminal gradients nonzero.
For j>=2, their source cross coefficient is ZERO by(15): g_0 appears
only in the all-left child chain, whereas the source join variables Y,Z
depend only on the bridge/right/terminal gradients. Its first diagonal
is2-b^2 by(28), and its terminal diagonal is pterm_j by(44).
At the Hword_(j+1) join, Y=0,Z=1. Every larger join has Y=Z=0,
and(18)'s whole-word boundary and right variables vanish by the exact
zero displacement. Hence the ENTIRE original base action is

    D_m Mbase(q;48)=(2-b^2)(hbridge/b)^2+pterm_j-E_j. (49)

We bound its complete value from above without a child PSD claim.
Equation(11) gives0<2-b^2<3epsilon and0<t_star-t_1<epsilon.
Also k_1<7,F_1<13, so pterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2.
For l>=2 the normalized multiplier k_l/(2+t_l)^2 is in(0,1)
and the new term is negative. A positive normalized pterm therefore
cannot exceed its depth2 value; a negative one remains negative.
Consequently

    pterm_j<2epsilon*A_j^2 for every j>=2.           (50)

Since A_j>5,d_j<A_j/2,b>7/5,
hbridge/b<(7A_j/10)/(7/5)=A_j/2. With E_j>0, (49),(50) yield

    D_m Mbase(q;48)<3epsilon*A_j^2.                 (51)

Now account for EVERY selected path node in(25). Index0 is never a
selected endpoint. The original bridge rbridge is the FIRST selected
boundary of the first depth-(j+2) node. That node has EPath=-d_j,
because y_rbridge=d_j and its other two endpoint gradients are zero.
If j>=3, rbridge is also the SECOND selected boundary of the first
depth-(j+1) node. Its first selected endpoint lies before rbridge and
after index0, so has position hbridge and gradient zero. Its third
endpoint has gradient zero. Its exact path energy is

    -y_rbridge+y_i1=-d_j+hbridge=1.

At j=2 this child depth3 node is excluded. The bridge rbridge is never
a THIRD selected boundary, and no other node contains it as a selected
endpoint: node offsets are multiples of their2^depth and rbridge+1=2^j.
All other path endpoint gradients and g_n are zero, so their entire
bilinear action is zero even if their positions before rbridge are large.
All active nodes lie in the first root left child and are eligible for
every receiver. Therefore the EXACT total same-word addition is

    D_m DeltaM_A(48)=2rhoPath_A D_m XiPath_A*
                    [1_(j>=3)A_(j-1)-A_j d_j].      (52)

This includes every terminal change, all three added prefix rows per
path, optimum rows, original terminal-square remainder and initial terms.
Since t_j>t_star>2/5,A_(j-1)<A_j/2 and A_j>5,

    A_j d_j-1_(j>=3)A_(j-1)>(3/10)A_j^2.           (53)

Combining(32),(51)–(53), the FULL original matrix obeys

    D_m Mpath_A(q;48)<-200epsilon*A_j^2<0            (54a)

for EVERY m>=1536, EVERY2<=j<=m-3 and EVERY real objective level q.
In particular Law A's actual full-depth choice j=m-3 is negative.

In Law B choose any EVEN j with2<=j<=m-3. The first-boundary parent
node of depth j+2 is allowed; its second-boundary child of depth j+1
is excluded. Its exact addition therefore is

    D_m DeltaM_B(48)=-2rhoPath_B D_m XiPath_B*A_j d_j.

Using the ENTIRE base upper(51), d_j>2A_j/5 and its OWN normalization
bound(32), Law B has

    D_m Mpath_B(q;48)<-4000epsilon*A_j^2<0           (54b)

for every m>=1536, every even2<=j<=m-3 and every real q. The explicit
same-word full-depth choice j=2floor((m-3)/2) lies in[m-4,m-3]; no
child parameter, physical horizon or global expenditure is changed.
Inflating q, including any zeta/D_m with zeta<250/237, cannot repair
either law's x_0=0 negative direction.

The final forward leg from the FIRST backward-path endpoint to a remote
receiver still sees its own actual large physical step. Coarse constant
block tests disguise that cost by earlier endpoint correlations. The
first-ordinary-gradient/large-bridge pair(48) cancels the displacement
while retaining a negative forward-leg action. This is the precise
mechanism failure of THESE laws, not an impossibility theorem for general
conditional transport or any full terminal cone.

## 13. Law C: first-query relay passes named screens, fails its first diagonal

Law C is the exact conditional path(23c), with every original coefficient
specified after(24), its entire polynomial(25c), and its full contact
increment after(27). It has the SAME terminal probabilities and
normalizations as Law B. Its total prefix addition is8rhoPath_B Tmass,
with the single whole-word bound <16/3. No additional slack is allocated.

On the old N plane, y_0=-u and every receiver y_v=0. In the even-depth
node set, the only FIRST selected boundary with nonzero gradient is
query3 of the first depth4 node, whose gradient is u. The exact
extra action of the origin relay is therefore

    DeltaM_(C-B)|N=2rhoPath_B*(Tmass-XiPath_B*A_2)*u^2>=0. (55)

For each v, its conditional probability of selecting that node is
A_2/ZPath_v<=1. Summing alpha_v proves the nonnegative coefficient
in(55) without treating it as an assumed covariance. Thus Law C
inherits the entire old-plane lower bound(38c).

On EVERY four-block test in Section10, g_0=1,y_0=-1,y_v=0 and
g_i1 belongs to{-1,0,1}. Each extra path term in(25c) is exactly
1-g_i1>=0. Thus the ENTIRE actual same-word screen(43c) remains
positive for Law C at every0<=j<=m-3 (including the unchanged positive
base at j=0,1, to which the extra relay may add positive mass).
On the actual root plane, g_0 and every selected endpoint gradient
are zero; the relay term vanishes. It inherits the complete specified
root gradient plane(47). No larger full-space positivity follows.

Now take the ORIGINAL independent scalar vectors

    x_0=0,g_0=1,EVERY other g_i=0,including g_n.      (56)

The actual formal positions are x_0=0 and x_i=-b for every i>=1.
All selected endpoint gradients are zero; y_0=-1,y_v=-b.
Hence Law B's path action is zero but every Law C extra term is

    <0-1,-1-(-b)>=-(b-1)=-a.

All path masses sum to rhoPath_B Tmass. Independently, the ENTIRE
original base first diagonal is2/D_m: source first diagonal2-b^2
from(28) and the whole-word boundary square b^2/D_m in(18) sum
to2/D_m. Therefore the complete Law C value, with no omitted terms,
is EXACTLY

    Mpath_C(q;56)=2/D_m-2rhoPath_B Tmass*a.           (57)

Because a=b-1>2/5,Tmass>99/100 and rhoPath_B=1024/m,
rhoPath_B Tmass*a>400/m=1600epsilon. Also D_m>=2^(m+1)-1>m,
using A_m>=2^m. In particular rhoPath_B Tmass*a*D_m>400>2.
Equation(57) thus gives

    D_m Mpath_C(q;56)<-1600epsilon*D_m<0,
    Mpath_C(q;56)<-1600epsilon                       (58)

for EVERY enclosing m>=1536 and EVERY real q. This is its actual
original first-gradient diagonal, not a compressed prefix or benchmark
estimate. The extra original-query relay exposes its OWN forward
physical step b. Its coordinate is retained, and that cost defeats
every advertised full objective level. No W conclusion follows.

## 14. Verdict, retrieval and authorized resource handoff

# Geometry-adaptive single-anchor terminal mixtures

JOINT UNVERIFIED — max4 invocation83, project gd-exact-profile-20260926.
Current steering ab8fdd0dc7b752a2; plan14ef9bbc976ba162.
This is one joint mathematical draft. No formal submission was authorized
or made, no new fact_id exists, and its new assertions are not truth-store
building blocks. The original target and the full W threshold remain OPEN.

## 1. Verdict and exact scope

The assigned selector has NOT been refuted, and its uniform all-function
inequality has NOT been proved. Only Mechanism1 was opened. Its required
legal all-depth first-law failure was not obtained, so Mechanism2
(optimum plus two selected anchors) remains unopened. There is no third law.

The following analytical screens are completed jointly in this draft:

* The known root-sign-reversing quadratic passes. On that precise prefix,
  the last anchor is proved to be the unique optimal single anchor AND
  to attain the TRUE full-prefix functional. The functional is below1/(4D).
* The canonical Huber contact passes with equality1/D. EVERY original
  anchor attains this value, so the deterministic index tie rule chooses0,
  not the last anchor.
* A genuinely multidirectional, separable Huber/quadratic family passes
  uniformly for all initial coordinate amplitudes and every allowed m.
  Its actual gradient vectors span two dimensions when both amplitudes
  are nonzero; no equality of formal gradient variables is imposed.
* Every legal prefix with ALL heights delta_i=0 passes with the stronger
  bound below1/D, in arbitrary dimension. The original rows force these
  anchors to be projections onto ONE convex set. This closes the whole
  zero-height case, not just a preselected cloud of points or a scalar
  projection example.
* The same selector passes for EVERY legal prefix with
  max_i delta_i<=||x_0||^2/(12D*sqrt(m)), by an explicit bound on
  the sum of all physical suffix products. This is a quantitative
  positive-height extension, not a full all-prefix theorem.

An exact finite correlation-cover inequality states the remaining
interface. It has not been proved for arbitrary positive-height prefixes.
The zero-height argument does not transfer merely by dropping height
terms; those terms change the normal-cone inequalities themselves.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the full original interpolation model
and maximal prefix extension. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the
same-enclosing-m scalar benchmark comparison. No unverified prior
finding or external theorem is used as a mathematical premise. In
particular the known quadratic is rederived here as part of this joint
argument, rather than importing its previous unverified proof.

## 2. Original model, hierarchy, and normalization

Normalize L=1, an actual selected minimizer x_star=0, and f_star=0.
All norms and inner products are Euclidean. The original updates are

    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i,     g_i=grad f(x_i),     f_i=f(x_i).

For all original ordered pairs, including the optimum, retain

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.

This orientation agrees with accepted1252b8823bb5a290. All statements
about a full certificate would have to retain independent physical
formal gradients; no new PSD certificate is asserted in this report.
The score is W=2R, namely2f_n/||x_0||^2 about the specified minimizer.

Fix an integer m>=1536 ONCE. Throughout every child uses

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j).

Let h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum H_m, D=D_m=1+2S, R=n-1, and

    A=h_R=1+a, J=2^(m-1)-1,
    t=a*c^(m-1), T=h_J=1+t.

Here T is the actual whole-root step; A is the actual last leaf.
The complete prefix set is I={star,0,...,R}; the actual terminal is n.
No tail, leaf, child, query, or original row is removed.

We will use only the following deliberately loose parameter bounds:

    12/5<c<5/2, 1/3<a<1/2, 1<A<3/2, T>18,
    D>36, S/T<15/2, D/T<16, D<2*c^m.

Here are their symbolic justifications. Since sqrt(2)>7/5 and
sqrt(2)<10/7, and epsilon<=1/6144,
12/5<c<(17/7)*(6145/6144)<5/2. Also beta>99/100, so
a>(99/100)/(5/2)>1/3, while a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<1/2.
For v=a/(c-2), one has0<v<1 because
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta. Literal concatenation gives

    1+S_j=(1-v)*2^j+v*c^j.                             (2.1)

This identity follows by substituting into
1+S_(j+1)=2*(1+S_j)+a*c^j and checking j=0; no recurrence
was executed. For j>=1, (2.1) is strictly less than c^j.
Consequently D=2*(1+S_m)-1<2*c^m, and
S/T<c^m/(a*c^(m-1))=c/a<15/2. Moreover
T>1+c^(m-1)/3>18, and D/T=1/T+2S/T<16.
Every step exceeds1, so S>n and D>2^(m+1)-1>36.
These bounds apply to EVERY declared m with the SAME parameters.

## 3. Exact complete-prefix functional and the explicit selector

Put

    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    y_star=delta_star=0.

The original rows Q_(star,i)<=0 imply delta_i>=0.
For a probability alpha on I define

    Psi(alpha)=2*sum_i alpha_i*delta_i
                         +||x_n-sum_i alpha_i*y_i||^2.

Accepted1252 constructs the maximal legal prefix extension F from

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I)(<y_i,v>-delta_i),
    F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)).

We jointly derive the finite variational step used here:

    2F(x_n)=min_(alpha probability on ALL I) Psi(alpha). (3.1)

For any alpha, H dominates its alpha-average quadratic; completing
the square in v gives2F(x_n)<=Psi(alpha). A minimizing alpha exists
by compactness. Put V=x_n-sum alpha_i*y_i at this minimum.
The partial derivatives of Psi/2 are delta_i-<V,y_i>. Probability
exchanges show they have a common value d on positive coordinates
and are at least d elsewhere. Thus
max_i(<y_i,V>-delta_i)=-d equals its alpha-average. Evaluating
<x_n,V>-H(V) now gives Psi(alpha)/2. This proves the reverse
inequality and (3.1), with the full vector geometry retained.

For each actual finite anchor define

    d_i=||y_i||^2, b_i=<x_n,y_i>-delta_i.

If d_i>0, set theta_i=clip_[0,1](b_i/d_i); if d_i=0 set theta_i=0.
Put

    Psi_i=||x_n-theta_i*y_i||^2+2theta_i*delta_i,
    M_1=min_(0<=i<=R) Psi_i.

The selector is the least original index attaining this finite minimum.
It is exactly the stated rule; choosing a particular anchor later is
only a valid upper bound on M_1 unless optimality is actually proved.
If d_i=0, b_i=-delta_i<=0, so the zero convention is minimizing.
For d>0 define the scalar gain

    G(b,d)=0                  if b<=0,
           b^2/d              if0<b<d,
           2b-d               if b>=d.

At b=0,d the adjacent formulas agree. Set G(b,0)=0 for the only
legal case b<=0. Completing the scalar square yields exactly

    M_1=||x_n||^2-max_i G(b_i,d_i).                    (3.2)

There is no unevaluated one-variable optimization left. The chain
for every legal actual terminal is

    2f_n <=2F(x_n) <=M_1.                             (3.3)

The assigned quantitative assertion is

    M_1 <=17||x_0||^2/(16D)                           (3.4)

for EVERY legal original H_m prefix in EVERY finite dimension.
A family passing (3.4) is not a proof of this assertion.

## 4. Exact root-sign-reversing quadratic screen

Choose the ONE global scalar function

    kappa=2/T, f(x)=kappa*x^2/2, x_0=1.

It is convex differentiable and1-smooth because0<kappa<1; its
unique minimizer is0 and the true initial radius is1. All physical
updates are exactly

    x_i=product_(s<i)(1-kappa*h_s), g_i=kappa*x_i,
    x_n=P=product_(s<n)(1-kappa*h_s).

Products here are symbolic identities, not computed trajectories.
For every ordered pair, including the terminal and optimum,

    Q_ij=-kappa*(1-kappa)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0.           (4.1)

The root multiplier is -1. Every other step is at most1+t/c;
with z_i=kappa*h_i,

    0<z_i<=2*(1+t/c)/(1+t)<9/10      (i!=J).        (4.2)

For the last comparison use c>12/5 and t>17: after multiplying
positive denominators it is enough that
2+(5/6)t<(9/10)(1+t), which holds for t>33/2.
Every non-root multiplier is therefore in(0,1). All x_i before
and at the root query are positive. All subsequent x_i are negative
and strictly increase toward0. In particular P<0, and x_R is the
negative prefix value closest to0.

Both physical immediate-child roots have
z_i=2*(1+t/c)/(1+t)>2/c>4/5. Their multipliers are each less
than1/5; all other non-root factors have magnitude less than1.
Consequently

    P^2<1/625, D*kappa*P^2<32/625<1/16.             (4.3)

Thus this function's ACTUAL score is below1/(16D).

Let b=1-kappa*A. The bounds above give1/2<b<1 and
x_R=P/b. Every prefix anchor has

    y_i=(1-kappa)*x_i,
    delta_i=kappa*(1-kappa)*x_i^2/2.

For R the optimal scalar weight is

    theta_R=(b-kappa/2)/(1-kappa)
            =[1-kappa*(A+1/2)]/(1-kappa),
    0<theta_R<1.                                    (4.4)

Its positivity follows from kappa*(A+1/2)<4/T<1; the upper
bound uses A+1/2>1. Direct substitution gives

    Psi_R=P^2-[P-(kappa/2)*x_R]^2
         =[kappa*b-kappa^2/4]*x_R^2
         <kappa*x_R^2<4*kappa*P^2<128/(625D)<1/(4D).
                                                               (4.5)

We now evaluate the TRUE full-prefix functional, rather than assuming
this anchor is best. Set v_*=kappa*x_R/2<0. At every original anchor,

    <y_i,v_*>-delta_i
      =[kappa*(1-kappa)/2]*x_i*(x_R-x_i)<=0.          (4.6)

For positive x_i, the second factor is negative. For every negative
prefix value, x_i<=x_R, the second factor is nonnegative. Equality
in (4.6) occurs only at i=R; star also has value0. Thus the maximum
in H(v_*) is0. Furthermore x_n=v_*+theta_R*y_R by (4.4), and
0<theta_R<1. Evaluation of the defining conjugate at v_* gives

    2F(x_n)>=2P*v_*-v_*^2=Psi_R.

Together with (3.1) and the valid star/R probability this proves

    2F(x_n)=M_1=Psi_R<1/(4D).                        (4.7)

The last anchor is also the UNIQUE best finite anchor here. Indeed
the gap between any alpha-average and the maximum at v_* is a sum
of nonnegative terms, strictly positive for positive mass at any
finite i!=R by (4.6). Equality in the square-completion bound also
forces residual v_*. A star-only choice would have residual P!=v_*.
Hence any exact minimizing probability has positive mass at R and
no other finite node. In particular no other single-anchor law ties R.
This argument is specific to this legal quadratic; it is not a general
last-anchor theorem.

For comparison to the previous round, the physical occupation average
has y_bar=(1-kappa)*(1-P)/(kappa*S)>0 by summing the actual GD
updates, whereas x_n=P<0. Its optimum scalar mix with star is zero,
giving surrogate P^2. Equation(4.7) displays exactly why the present
adaptive support repairs that earlier failure. No conclusion about
all other prefixes follows.

## 5. Exact Huber contact and its deterministic selector

Put k=1/D and define the ONE global scalar Huber function

    f_H(x)=x^2/2                       if |x|<=k,
           k*|x|-k^2/2                 if |x|>=k,
    x_0=1.

Its derivative clip(x,-k,k) is continuous, monotone and1-Lipschitz.
The unique minimizer is0. At original cumulative mass
s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, all original prefix and terminal points satisfy

    x_i=1-k*s_i>k, g_i=k,
    f_i=k*x_i-k^2/2,
    y_i=x_i-k>0, delta_i=k*y_i.

Indeed s_i<=S and1-k*S=(S+1)/D>k. All updates use the original
steps; finite-to-finite Q rows vanish. Rows to and from star are
Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-k*y_i<=0. The terminal obeys the same
identities. The closest initial radius is1.

Let z=x_n-k=S/D>0. For every actual prefix index i,

    y_i>=y_R=x_n+a*k>z,
    theta_i=z/y_i in(0,1),
    Psi_i=2k*x_n-k^2=1/D.                            (5.1)

The equality follows from the scalar minimizer x_n-theta_i*y_i=k.
For the full probability simplex, delta_i=k*y_i including star, and
its barycenter y ranges over[0,y_0]. Thus (3.1) minimizes
2k*y+(x_n-y)^2 over that interval; its minimum is at y=z and equals
1/D. Equivalently the actual Huber value already attains this upper
bound. Hence

    2f_H(x_n)=2F(x_n)=M_1=1/D.                        (5.2)

EVERY prefix anchor is best. Under the stipulated least-index tie
break, the selected index is0. This is an explicit reason not to
identify the rule with a fixed last-anchor law.

## 6. A genuine two-dimensional positive-height screen

Take arbitrary u>0 and v!=0. Define a scalar Huber function with
threshold k_u=u/D by the formula of Section5, replacing k by k_u.
On R^2 use the ONE static function

    f(z,w)=f_(H,u)(z)+(kappa/2)*w^2,
    x_0=(u,v), kappa=2/T.

Its Hessian bounds, or directly its coordinate gradients, show convexity
and1-Lipschitz gradient. Its unique minimizer is(0,0), so the exact
initial radius squared is u^2+v^2. Every original iterate is

    z_i=u*(1-s_i/D),
    w_i=v*product_(t<i)(1-kappa*h_t),
    g_i=(u/D,kappa*w_i).

The first coordinate remains on the positive affine Huber ray through
the actual terminal. The second has exactly the one root sign change
from Section4. All original updates hold coordinatewise. Every Q row
is the sum of the Huber row calculated in Section5 and the nonpositive
quadratic row in(4.1), including optimum and actual terminal rows.
This proves legality of the entire original transcript, not only of
its collapsed endpoint data.

It is genuinely multidirectional. The first two gradients have
nonzero determinant

    det(g_0,g_1)=-(u/D)*kappa^2*A*v !=0,

because the first physical step is A. Therefore the trajectory is
not a scalar common-gradient embedding.

Evaluate the allowed R segment at theta=1, without claiming its
optimum is attained there and without choosing different coordinate
weights. Its Huber contribution is exactly

    u^2/D+A^2*u^2/D^2.

To check, at R the first-coordinate y is z_n+a*k_u and
x_n-y=-a*k_u; hence2delta_R+|x_n-y|^2 differs from the true
Huber score u^2/D by (a+1)^2*k_u^2=A^2*u^2/D^2.
The quadratic contribution is

    [kappa*(1-kappa)+a^2*kappa^2]*v^2*(P/b)^2
       <kappa*v^2*(P/b)^2
       <128*v^2/(625D),                              (6.1)

using a<1 and (4.3). Because A^2<9/4 and D>36,

    M_1 <=Psi_R(theta=1)
        <(17/16)*u^2/D+(128/625)*v^2/D
        <17*(u^2+v^2)/(16D).                         (6.2)

The same upper bound includes the zero-amplitude endpoints by the
already treated scalar cases. No optimizer or executed product is
needed. The TRUE full-prefix functional obeys, on this same prefix,

    u^2/D+kappa*v^2*P^2 <=2F(x_n)
       <=M_1<17*(u^2+v^2)/(16D).                     (6.3)

The lower inequality uses this actual globally defined function;
the upper inequality uses(3.3). We do not assert exact equality for
the two-dimensional full functional. These bounds suffice to show
that this rotating-gradient test is not a selector failure or full-W
failure. They do not cover arbitrary two-dimensional functions.

## 7. The entire zero-height legal-prefix class

Here start from ANY legal original prefix with delta_i=0 for EVERY
finite i. Do not assume that a given cloud of convex-hull points is
a GD trajectory. Retain all original ordered rows. Define the ONE
compact convex set

    K=conv{0,y_0,...,y_R}.

Since f_i=||g_i||^2/2 and x_i=y_i+g_i, direct cancellation gives

    Q_ij=<g_i,y_j-y_i>                               (7.1)

for every ordered pair, including star. Thus all original rows imply
<g_i,z-y_i><=0 for all z in K. As y_i belongs to K and x_i=y_i+g_i,
for every z in K one has

    ||x_i-z||^2
      =||g_i||^2+||z-y_i||^2-2<g_i,z-y_i>
      >=||g_i||^2.

The unique nearest point in K to x_i is therefore y_i. Consequently

    y_i=P_K(x_i), g_i=x_i-P_K(x_i)                   (7.2)

at EVERY original queried index, with no free reassignment of anchors.

For clarity this also constructs a legal static global function:

    f_K(x)=dist(x,K)^2/2.

A nearest point exists by compactness, and uniqueness follows from
strict convexity of squared norm along a segment of K. Projection
optimality gives <x-P_K(x),z-P_K(x)><=0 for z in K. Applying this
at x and x' and adding proves

    <P_K(x)-P_K(x'),x-x'> >=||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2.

Hence g_K(x)=x-P_K(x) is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the distance values
using P_K(x) and P_K(x') as trial points shows
f_K(x+d)<=f_K(x)+<g_K(x),d>+||d||^2/2 and, by reversing the
comparison, f_K(x+d)>=f_K(x)+<g_K(x+d),d>-||d||^2/2.
Continuity of g_K proves differentiability with gradient g_K.
For convexity, a convex combination of the projections is a valid
point in K; convexity of squared norm then proves the convexity
inequality for f_K. Thus f_K is finite convex differentiable and
1-smooth, with its complete minimum set exactly K. It interpolates
all queried points, gradients, and values by(7.2), and follows the
original GD trajectory. The designated minimum0 belongs to K and
is explicitly valid. If desired choose the closest point P_K(x_0)
as the reference minimum; the bound below holds for that minimum too.

In this case(3.1) additionally evaluates the TRUE maximal prefix value:

    2F(x_n)=min_(y in K)||x_n-y||^2=dist(x_n,K)^2.    (7.3)

For each original step, its already computed projection y_i is a
valid trial point for the NEXT distance. Therefore

    dist(x_(i+1),K)
      <=||x_i-h_i*g_i-y_i||
      =(h_i-1)*dist(x_i,K),                         (7.4)

because every h_i>1. Only the original geometry yields this bound.
At the actual last anchor, theta=1 gives

    M_1<=||x_n-y_R||^2
       =a^2*dist(x_R,K)^2
       <=[product_(i<n)(h_i-1)]^2*dist(x_0,K)^2.     (7.5)

The product has a closed symbolic value for the physical hierarchy.
A level ell step1+a*c^ell occurs2^(m-1-ell) times. The multiplicity
sum is n and the level-weighted sum is n-m (both follow by the
literal binary concatenation, or induction on these finite sums).
Consequently

    product_(i<n)(h_i-1)
       =a^n*c^(n-m)=beta^n*c^(-m).                  (7.6)

No trajectory, recurrence, or product was numerically evaluated.
Using D<2*c^m and0<beta<1,

    D*beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)<2*c^(-m)<1.

Thus for every such prefix and every specified minimum0,

    M_1<=beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)*dist(x_0,K)^2
        <=beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)*||x_0||^2
        <||x_0||^2/D                                (7.7)

when x_0!=0; if the distance is zero the non-strict zero bound
already suffices. Since any supplied legal prefix interpolant has
terminal value at most F(x_n), this proves the assigned inequality
for the ENTIRE zero-height legal-prefix class, including all of its
multidirectional configurations. A nonzero initial radius for f_K
is dist(x_0,K); if x_0 belongs to K, all queried gradients and the
actual terminal error are zero, a harmless degenerate case.

An unconstrained convex-hull configuration can have a large gap
between distance to its hull and distance to all rays[0,y_i]. It
cannot refute the assigned selector through delta_i=0: the GD and
normal conditions(7.1)--(7.4) force the last ray already to be close
enough at the required quantitative scale. This distinction uses
all the original rows and the actual last leaf.

## 7b. A quantitative small-positive-height extension

The preceding geometry need not be assumed when the heights are small.
For ANY legal original prefix put

    Delta=max_(0<=i<=R) delta_i, r_i=h_i-1>0.

Direct expansion of the original row, using
x_(i+1)=y_i-r_i*g_i and y_(i+1)=x_(i+1)-g_(i+1),

    Q_(i+1,i)=delta_(i+1)-delta_i
                +r_i*<g_i,g_(i+1)>+||g_(i+1)||^2<=0.

Consequently

    r_i^2*||g_i||^2+2delta_i-2delta_(i+1)-||g_(i+1)||^2
       =||r_i*g_i+g_(i+1)||^2-2Q_(i+1,i)>=0,

and hence

    ||g_(i+1)||^2<=r_i^2*||g_i||^2+2Delta          (i<R).
                                                               (7b.1)

Both optimum rows at0 imply||g_0||^2<=<g_0,x_0>, so
||g_0||<=||x_0||. Applying(7b.1) successively, by finite induction
as an inequality rather than an executed recurrence, and evaluating
the same R segment at theta=1, gives

    M_1 <=2delta_R+a^2||g_R||^2
        <=p_m*||x_0||^2+2Delta*e_m,                 (7b.2)

where the two explicit physical word functionals are

    p_j=product_(i in H_j)(h_i-1)^2
       =beta^(2(2^j-1))*c^(-2j),
    e_j=sum_(i in H_j) product_(t in H_j,t>i)(h_t-1)^2,
    p_0=1, e_0=0.

Here indices inside a child are its original chronological order;
the SAME enclosing m parameters are used. We now bound e_m
quantitatively, rather than leaving its size to a recurrence oracle.
Concatenation as left child, root, right child gives the identity

    e_(j+1)=M_j*e_j+p_j,
    M_j=1+a^2*c^(2j)*p_j
       =1+a^2*beta^(2(2^j-1)).                     (7b.3)

The left-child suffix factors cross the actual root and whole right
child; the root contribution is p_j; the right-child contribution
is e_j. This proves(7b.3) with every physical suffix retained.
As all M_j>=1, expansion of this identity as a finite sum yields

    e_m <=[product_(j=0)^(m-1) M_j]*sum_(j=0)^(m-1)p_j.
                                                               (7b.4)

Let q=ceil(log_2(4m)); q is only an analytic splitting index, not a
new schedule parameter. Then4m<=2^q<8m. For j<q,
M_j<5/4<sqrt(2), and hence the product of at most q such factors
is less than2^(q/2)<3sqrt(m).
For j=q+r with r>=0, use log(beta)<=-1/(4m) and
2^j-1>=2^(j-1) to obtain

    beta^(2(2^j-1))<=exp(-2^j/(4m))<=exp(-2^r).

Since2^r>=r+1, the sum over r>=0 is at most
sum_(r>=0)exp(-(r+1))=1/(exp(1)-1)<1.
The logarithm of the product of all remaining M_j is thus less
than a^2<1/4, using log(1+s)<=s for s>=0. This product is less
than exp(1/4)<4/3. The last inequality follows by comparing the
exponential series term by term with the geometric series at1/4.
These bounds also apply if the finite tail is empty.
Consequently product M_j<4sqrt(m). Furthermore
p_j<=c^(-2j)<=4^(-j), so sum p_j<4/3. Equation(7b.4) gives

    e_m<(16/3)*sqrt(m)<6sqrt(m).                    (7b.5)

The constants are valid for EVERY m>=1536. No arithmetic, product,
or recurrence was evaluated by a program. The product decay already
proved above gives D*p_m<2c^(-m)<1/16 for these m. Therefore

    M_1 <=p_m*||x_0||^2+2Delta*e_m,
    M_1 <||x_0||^2/(16D)+12sqrt(m)*Delta            if Delta>0.
                                                               (7b.6)

For Delta=0 the first inequality directly gives the stronger bound.
In particular the assigned target inequality holds for EVERY legal
original prefix satisfying the explicit positive-height condition

    max_i delta_i <=||x_0||^2/(12D*sqrt(m)).         (7b.7)

This is a uniform quantitative sufficient condition, not a conjecture
about all prefixes. It uses an available point on the SAME single-
anchor segment and does not introduce any new terminal probability
law. It does not claim that a failed (7b.7) forces selector failure;
Huber is an example with larger heights which passes by Section5.
No new minimizer or function is needed: (7b.1)--(7b.7) apply to the
supplied legal static prefix and its original chosen minimizer.


## 8. Exact remaining positive-height interface and failed transfers

Let chi=17/(16D) and E=||x_n||^2-chi*||x_0||^2.
If E<=0 the optimum-only choice theta=0 proves(3.4). If E>0,
(3.2) proves that(3.4) is equivalent to the existence of an actual
finite anchor with d_i>0 such that

    b_i>=sqrt(E*d_i)              if d_i>=E,
    b_i>=(E+d_i)/2                if0<d_i<E.         (8.1)

To verify the cases, solve G(b_i,d_i)>=E on its two increasing
positive branches; at d_i=E they both require b_i>=E.
The threshold requires b_i>0, so the zero-gain branch cannot help.
Equation(8.1) is an exact finite geometry condition, not its proof.
Its unproved quantifier is: every positive-height legal original
H_m prefix satisfies one of these original-anchor inequalities.

Two direct proof routes were screened within Mechanism1.

First, a stronger sufficient last-anchor estimate would be

    2f_R-(1-a^2)||g_R||^2 <=chi*||x_0||^2.           (8.2)

Its left side is exactly Psi_R(theta=1), so(8.2) would imply(3.4).
No bound(8.2) for arbitrary legal prefixes is established here.
Failure of the previous backward-only multiplier support does not
refute(8.2): its negative direction was not interpolable. Conversely,
the family calculations above cannot establish(8.2) outside their
hypotheses. We retain (8.1), rather than replacing the selector by R.

The original optimum rows give a useful exact last-anchor formula.
Write y=y_R, g=g_R, delta=delta_R, d=||y||^2, and

    t_R=a*<g,y>+delta.

From Q_(R,star)<=0 one has <g,y>>=delta>=0, so t_R>=0.
Since x_n=y-a*g, the segment numerator is b=d-t_R. Thus if d>0,

    theta_R=max(0,1-t_R/d),
    Psi_R=2delta+a^2||g||^2-t_R^2/d    when0<=t_R<d,
    Psi_R=||x_n||^2                   when t_R>=d.  (8.3)

At equality the formulas agree. If d=0, the optimum rows imply
delta=0 and the stated zero-anchor convention applies. These are
exact scalar evaluations; bounding their minimum together with
all OTHER anchors remains necessary. They do not leave an unknown
one-dimensional optimizer and do not supply the missing global rate.

Second, the full normal-cone contraction closes the zero-height
case but does not extend unchanged. For general heights, expanding
ALL the original rows gives instead

    Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i><=0,
    <g_i,y_j-y_i><=delta_j-delta_i.                  (8.4)

The right side is not zero and has no fixed sign across all pairs.
Therefore y_i need not be the projection of x_i onto the convex
hull K. Discarding delta_i>=0 from Psi while continuing to use
(7.2) or(7.4) is invalid. As a concrete legal check, in the Huber
contact all finite y_i are positive and K=[0,y_0], whereas for a
later query with x_i in[0,y_0] the projection is x_i itself and the
actual y_i=x_i-k differs. The height differences in(8.4) account
for this. Such an i exists, for example i=R: s_R=S-A>1 gives
0<x_R<y_0. All values here use the original Huber trajectory.

Thus no counterexample to the selector has emerged, but the attempted
projection transfer has a concrete failed hypothesis. The unresolved
case requires controlling positive heights and the changed geometry
jointly at scale1/D. It is not a support-representation issue or an
unevaluated scalar minimization.

## 9. Literature, memory, and resource record

Query-memory recovered TASK.md, current guidanceab8fdd0dc7b752a2,
previous quadratic finding1b246cab08578ca1 and its precise failure
scope, and the relevant accepted facts. The first broad combined

The procedures used were query-memory, direct-proving, and

The existing own extracted paper text at

One administrative report-formatting patch returned "Failed to find
